# Solvio Android — Pixel-Parity Audit (2026-06-09)

Generated by 23-agent parallel audit (iOS SwiftUI = source of truth vs Android Compose port).

**23 areas, 473 findings.** Severity legend: 🔴 critical · 🟠 high · 🟡 medium · ⚪ low


## DataLayer — parity ~58% (38 findings)

_The Android DataLayer ports the iOS model graph and repository surface reasonably well in shape, but it diverges in numerous behavior- and contract-critical ways that will break real calls against the Next.js backend, because this Kotlin was never compiled or run. The most severe: the Settings discriminated-union POST sends wrong `type` discriminators ("updateSettings"/"addCategory"/"upsertBudget" instead of "settings"/"category"/"budget"); sign-in/demo decode the wrong response shapes and silently drop the authenticated email; there is NO CSRF token handling at all (iOS fetches /api/auth/csrf, attaches x-csrf-token, and retries on 403) so every write call will 403 if the backend enforces CSRF; report generation sends JSON instead of multipart form-data with the wrong field names; and several AI endpoints omit `currency`/`force`/`period` body fields. There are also data correctness issues (MoneyString formatting uses raw locale grouping vs iOS NSDecimalRound; missing FX-rates provider; missing 401 session-expired broadcast/auto-logout; missing payloadTooLarge/timeout/cancelled error classification; missing GDPR export, delete-account, and pendingCount endpoints; missing money math helpers and computed properties like monthlyAmount/displayPrice/displayItemCount/label). Parity of the model field set is high but the live behavior parity is much lower._

- 🔴 **[bug] Settings POST sends wrong discriminator `type` values**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:62-87` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:90-115`
  - iOS sends `{ type: 'settings'|'category'|'budget', data }` to /api/data/settings, matching the backend discriminated union. Android sends `Update("updateSettings", ...)`, `Update("addCategory", ...)`, `Update("upsertBudget", ...)`. The backend will not match any of these discriminators, so settings/category/budget writes will all fail (or no-op) on Android.
  - **Fix:** Change the type strings to exactly `"settings"`, `"category"`, `"budget"` to match iOS/backend.
- 🔴 **[bug] Sign-in decodes wrong response shape; email always null**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Session/SessionStore.swift:122-134` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:27-28; android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/session/SessionStore.kt:32-36`
  - iOS POST /api/auth/session returns `{ ok, userId }` (SessionLoginResponse) and carries the *input* email forward as the authoritative current user. Android decodes the response into SessionMe (expects `email`), but the endpoint never returns email, so `me.email` is null and `signIn()` returns false even on a successful login. iOS also lowercases+trims the email and sends `lang`; Android sends raw email and no `lang` (so backend can't seed categories in the right language).
  - **Fix:** Decode SessionLoginResponse; build the current user from the trimmed/lowercased input email + returned userId; add a `lang` field to SessionPostBody read from the persisted locale. Set `_currentUser` from the input email, not the response.
- 🔴 **[bug] Demo login decodes wrong shape and skips the /me follow-up**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Session/SessionStore.swift:138-149` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:32-33; android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/session/SessionStore.kt:38-42`
  - iOS POST /api/auth/demo returns `{ success, redirect }` (DemoLoginResponse); iOS then calls GET /api/auth/session/me to discover the demo email (demo@solvio.app). Android decodes /api/auth/demo directly into SessionMe (which the endpoint does not return), so the demo user's email will be null and the second /me call is never made.
  - **Fix:** Decode DemoLoginResponse, verify `success`, then call AuthRepo.me() to populate the current user (fallback email "demo@solvio.app"), mirroring iOS.
- 🔴 **[bug] No CSRF token handling — all write requests can 403**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/ApiClient.swift:66-74,262-265,326-347,349-399` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/ApiClient.kt:128-153`
  - iOS fetches a CSRF token from /api/auth/csrf, attaches it as `x-csrf-token` on every POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE (except a bypass-prefix list), and on a 403 with X-CSRF-Reason missing/mismatch it force-refreshes the token and retries once. Android's ApiClient has no CSRF logic at all — no token fetch, no header, no retry. If the backend enforces CSRF, every mutating Android request fails with 403.
  - **Fix:** Port the CSRF flow: GET /api/auth/csrf, cache token, add `x-csrf-token` header to mutating methods (skip the bypass prefixes /api/auth/session, /api/auth/demo, /api/auth/magic-login, /api/auth/csrf, /api/settlement/, /api/cron/), and retry once on a 403 with X-CSRF-Reason missing/mismatch.
- 🔴 **[bug] Report generation uses JSON body instead of multipart form-data with wrong fields**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:387-404; native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/ApiClient.swift:222-249` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:228-239`
  - iOS /api/reports/generate is a multipart formData POST with fields `type: 'yearly'|'monthly'` and `year` (yearly) or `ym` (YYYY-MM, monthly). Android posts JSON `{ year: Int, format }` / `{ yearMonth: String, format }` — wrong content type, wrong field names (`year`/`yearMonth`/`format` vs `type`/`year`/`ym`), and missing the required `type` discriminator. Report generation will not work on Android.
  - **Fix:** Add a multipart-form POST helper to ApiClient and send fields `type=yearly`,`year=<year>` and `type=monthly`,`ym=<YYYY-MM>` exactly as iOS.
- 🟠 **[bug] Audit request body omits `currency` and `force`**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:347-362` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:208-214`
  - iOS AuditRepo.generate sends `{ lang, currency, force }`; `force=true` bypasses the 6h backend cache and currency drives formatting. Android AuditGenerateBody only has `lang`, so the audit can never be force-refreshed and currency context is lost.
  - **Fix:** Add `currency: String?` and `force: Boolean?` to AuditGenerateBody and the generate() signature.
- 🟠 **[bug] Analysis request body omits `currency`**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:366-383` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:218-224`
  - iOS AnalysisRepo.run sends `{ lang, currency, period }`. Android AnalysisRunBody only has `lang` and `period` — no `currency`. AI analysis currency formatting/context differs from iOS.
  - **Fix:** Add `currency: String?` to AnalysisRunBody and run().
- 🟠 **[behavior] Prices compare may omit `force`/`currency` at call sites; Promotions uses GET not POST**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:331-343,432-446` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:201-204,259-264`
  - Promotions: iOS POSTs /api/personal/promotions with a JSON body `{ lang, currency }` (PromotionsRepo.fetch). Android does a GET with `?force=true` query and no lang/currency body — a different HTTP method and shape than iOS/backend. PricesRepo.compare body fields exist but ensure callers pass currency.
  - **Fix:** Make PromotionsRepo.fetch POST a `{ lang, currency }` body matching iOS (add an AdvisorRunBody-like body type), not a GET with a force query param.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] FXRates currency-converter provider entirely missing on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/ApiClient.swift:541-667` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS ships an in-app FX provider (NBP api.nbp.pl) used by the expense/receipt-detail currency converter: static Q1-2026 fallbacks, 6h UserDefaults cache, parallel per-code fetch, convert(amount, from, to). Android has no FXRates equivalent anywhere in the DataLayer, so any currency-conversion UI cannot function.
  - **Fix:** Port FXRates: a singleton with the same static fallback map (PLN 1.0, EUR 4.30, USD 4.05, GBP 5.10, CHF 4.55, CZK 0.18), 6h DataStore/SharedPreferences cache, NBP table-A fetch with 4s timeout, and convert().
- 🟠 **[behavior] 401 auto-logout / session-expired broadcast not implemented**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/ApiClient.swift:456-463,669-675; native-ios/Solvio/Core/Session/SessionStore.swift:32-51` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/ApiClient.kt:147-205; android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/session/SessionStore.kt:13-49`
  - On any HTTP 401 iOS posts a `.solvioSessionExpired` NotificationCenter event; SessionStore observes it and force-logs-out so the app reliably returns to login instead of sitting in a half-authenticated zombie state. Android maps 401 to ApiError.Unauthorized but never broadcasts a global session-expired signal — only SessionStore.restore() handles Unauthorized; mid-session 401s on arbitrary screens leave the user stuck.
  - **Fix:** Add a shared SharedFlow/event bus (or callback) emitted from ApiError.from/execute on 401, and have SessionStore collect it to clear the cookie + currentUser globally.
- 🟠 **[behavior] ApiError lacks timeout / noConnection / cancelled / rateLimited / forbidden / decoding cases**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/ApiClient.swift:3-58,289-313` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/ApiClient.kt:190-205`
  - iOS ApiError has invalidURL, transport, decoding, unauthorized, forbidden(403), notFound, rateLimited(429), timeout, noConnection, server, payloadTooLarge(413), cancelled, unknown — plus `isRetryable` and localized descriptions, and classifies URLSession transport errors (cancelled/timeout/offline). Android only has Unauthorized(401), NotFound(404), Cancelled, PayloadTooLarge(413), Server. It maps 403 and 429 into generic Server, has no transport-error classification (timeouts/offline surface as raw IOException), no isRetryable, and no user-facing localized messages. Error UX and retry logic will diverge sharply.
  - **Fix:** Expand ApiError to mirror iOS (add Forbidden 403, RateLimited 429, Timeout, NoConnection, Decoding, Transport, Unknown), classify OkHttp/IO exceptions (SocketTimeoutException→Timeout, UnknownHost/ConnectException→NoConnection, cancellation→Cancelled), and add an isRetryable property.
- 🟠 **[behavior] MoneyString formatting/value semantics differ from iOS (locale grouping, comma handling, rounding)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Models/Money.swift:7-53` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/models/Models.kt:14-30; android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/Formatters.kt:16-29`
  - iOS MoneyString stores a Decimal (exact), exposes `description` via NSDecimalRound(2, .plain), `double`, `formatted(currency:)`, `.zero`, and `+`/`-` operators. Android MoneyString stores only the raw String and exposes only toDouble() (which replaces ',' with '.'). It loses the exact Decimal, has no rounding-aware description, no money arithmetic, and no formatted(). Any iOS code summing MoneyString values or printing `.description` has no Android equivalent. Separately, Fmt.amount(value:String) in Android does `value.toDoubleOrNull()` without replacing ',' (line 27 does replace, OK) — but MoneyString.toDouble replaces ',', while a backend decimal like "1,234.56" with a thousands comma would be corrupted (iOS Decimal(string:) would too, minor).
  - **Fix:** Use BigDecimal-backed MoneyString with a rounded toPlainString(), add zero/plus/minus and a formatted(currency) helper so summation and display match iOS exactly; ensure parsing only treats ',' as decimal separator when there is no '.'.
- 🟠 **[bug] ExpensesRepo.create returns raw JsonElement instead of the created Expense**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:97-100` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:46-47`
  - iOS ExpensesRepo.create decodes `{ expense: Expense }` (ExpenseWrap) and returns the typed Expense. Android returns an undecoded JsonElement, pushing the unwrap/parse burden onto every caller and risking inconsistent decoding. There is also no ExpenseWrap model on Android.
  - **Fix:** Add an ExpenseWrap (`{ expense: Expense }`) model and have create() decode it and return Expense, matching iOS.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] GDPR export-data, delete-account, and recategorize pendingCount endpoints missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:536-557,599-608` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS has ExportDataRepo.download() (GET /api/personal/export-data → raw JSON blob), AccountRepo.deleteAccount() (POST /api/personal/delete-account, required for App Store 5.1.1(v)), and MaintenanceRepo.pendingCount() (GET /api/v1/recategorize-receipts). Android Repositories.kt has none of these. Account deletion is a store-compliance requirement; data export is GDPR.
  - **Fix:** Add ExportDataRepo (download raw bytes via a download helper), AccountRepo.deleteAccount() (POST), and MaintenanceRepo.pendingCount() returning a PendingCount(totalReceipts, pendingReceipts, pendingItems) model.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] ApiClient has no raw byte-download helper (reports / e-receipts / export)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/ApiClient.swift:167-182` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS ApiClient.download(path) returns (Data, suggestedFilename) and is used for generated reports, e-receipts, and GDPR export. Android ApiClient exposes only string/JSON helpers and a multipart upload — there is no way to fetch raw bytes or read the Content-Disposition filename. Report download and export-data are impossible.
  - **Fix:** Add a `download(path, query): Pair<ByteArray, String?>` helper that reads response bytes and parses the Content-Disposition filename.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Income model and IncomesRepo entirely missing on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Models/Models.swift:935-983; native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:501-520` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS has the full Income data layer (Income, IncomeCreate, IncomeUpdate, IncomeDeleteBody, IncomesListResponse, IncomeWrap, and IncomesRepo with list/create/update/delete against /api/personal/incomes) powering the savings hub's monthly-income aggregate. None of this exists in Android Models.kt or Repositories.kt. The savings/income feature cannot function on Android.
  - **Fix:** Port the Income models (with monthlyAmount logic) and IncomesRepo CRUD against /api/personal/incomes.
- 🟠 **[bug] DELETE bodies use empty JsonObject for routes iOS calls with no body — generally OK but settle uses POST vs PATCH**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:239-245` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:150-155`
  - iOS settles a split with PATCH /api/groups/splits/{splitId}/settle and body { memberId } (patchVoid). Android GroupsRepo.settleSplit uses POST to the same path. If the backend route only accepts PATCH, the Android settle action 404s/405s.
  - **Fix:** Change settleSplit to PATCH (add a patch helper to ApiClient) to match iOS/backend.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Groups update (PUT) repo method missing on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:226-228; native-ios/Solvio/Core/Models/Models.swift:344-352` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:134-156`
  - iOS GroupsRepo.update(id, GroupUpdate) PUTs /api/groups/{id} to edit a group (name/description/currency/emoji/mode/dates). Android GroupsRepo has list/detail/create/delete/receipts/settlements/createSplit/settleSplit but NO update, and there is no GroupUpdate model in Models.kt. Group editing is impossible on Android.
  - **Fix:** Add a GroupUpdate data class and GroupsRepo.update(id, body) using PUT /api/groups/{id}.
- 🟡 **[bug] ExpenseUpdate drops the `receiptId` field iOS sends**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Models/Models.swift:82-96` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/models/Models.kt:106-116`
  - iOS ExpenseUpdate includes `receiptId: String?` so a manual expense can be re-linked to / unlinked from a receipt on edit (backend UpdateExpenseSchema accepts it). Android ExpenseUpdate has no receiptId, so Android edits can never change the receipt link.
  - **Fix:** Add `receiptId: String? = null` to ExpenseUpdate.
- 🟡 **[crash-risk] MoneyString deserializer returns "0" for non-primitive instead of throwing/erroring**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Models/Money.swift:14-25` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/models/Models.kt:22-29`
  - iOS MoneyString.init(from:) throws typeMismatch if the JSON is neither a numeric string nor a number, surfacing a decode error. Android's deserializer silently returns MoneyString("0") for any non-JsonPrimitive (e.g. an object/array), masking malformed payloads as 0.00 instead of failing loudly. Could hide real backend/contract bugs as silent zero amounts.
  - **Fix:** Throw a SerializationException for the non-primitive branch (or at least log), matching iOS's typeMismatch behavior.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Missing computed helpers: monthlyAmount, displayPrice, displayItemCount, GroupMember.label, OcrReceiptResponse parity OK**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Models/Models.swift:131-132,156-158,307,951-962` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/models/Models.kt:124-151,318-327,940-963`
  - iOS exposes Income.monthlyAmount (normalizes weekly*52/12, yearly/12, oneoff→0, else raw), ReceiptItem.displayPrice (price ?? totalPrice), Receipt.displayItemCount (itemCount ?? items.count ?? 0), and GroupMember.label (name ?? displayName). Android Models.kt has none of these computed properties (only OcrReceiptResponse.firstSuccess was ported). UI that relies on these will need ad-hoc, possibly inconsistent logic. Also the entire Income model + IncomeCreate/IncomeUpdate/IncomeDelete/IncomesListResponse/IncomeWrap and IncomesRepo are absent on Android (see separate finding).
  - **Fix:** Add the computed properties as Kotlin extension vals/funcs on the data classes to centralize the same logic as iOS.
- 🟡 **[visual] Fmt.date uses 'd MMM yyyy' pattern instead of locale medium style; parse coverage narrower**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Formatters.swift:40-45,107-116` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/Formatters.kt:30-47`
  - iOS Fmt.date uses DateFormatter.dateStyle=.medium (e.g. PL '9 cze 2026', EN 'Jun 9, 2026' with locale ordering). Android hardcodes pattern 'd MMM yyyy' which forces day-month-year ordering and 'MMM' formatting that won't match iOS medium style for EN (which puts month first and adds a comma). The two apps will print different date strings, breaking visual parity.
  - **Fix:** Use DateFormat.getDateInstance(DateFormat.MEDIUM, locale) (or java.time medium localized) instead of the fixed 'd MMM yyyy' pattern.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Fmt missing intGrouped, qty, dayMonth, dayMonthShort, parseISO helpers**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Formatters.swift:85-103,107-125` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/Formatters.kt:15-52`
  - iOS Fmt provides intGrouped(Int) (quick-amount buttons), qty(Double) (locale-aware decimal, trims trailing zeros), dayMonth(iso), dayMonthShort(Date), and parseISO. Android Fmt only has amount/date/initials. Receipt/shopping quantity rows and quick-amount grids and short-date chips have no equivalent formatter and will render differently or with raw values.
  - **Fix:** Port intGrouped, qty (NumberFormat decimal, min 0 / max 3 fraction digits), dayMonth, dayMonthShort, and a public parseInstant/parseISO.
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] AppLocale.format uses naive single-token regex; fails multi-arg / %@ ordering vs iOS**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/AppLocale.swift:48-50,74-95` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/AppLocale.kt:45-54`
  - iOS uses real String(format:) with %@/%d and ships tPlural() with full CLDR Polish plural rules (one/few/many). Android format() does a naive replaceFirst per arg over %[@dDs] and has NO plural support at all. Polish pluralization (e.g. '%d paragon/paragony/paragonów') will be wrong on Android, and any format string using positional specifiers or repeated args will mis-substitute.
  - **Fix:** Implement tPlural with the Polish CLDR categories (one: n==1; few: n%10 in 2..4 and n%100 not in 12..14; many: else; EN: 1→one else many) and use String.format with proper locale for argument substitution.
- 🟡 **[bug] Cookie jar persists name/value only — drops domain/path/expiry/secure; can resend stale/expired cookies**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/ApiClient.swift:95-99 (HTTPCookieStorage full attributes)` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/ApiClient.kt:234-255`
  - iOS relies on HTTPCookieStorage which honors expiry, domain, path, Secure, HttpOnly. Android PersistentCookieJar.persist() flattens to 'host|name=value', discarding expiry/path/secure. On reload it rebuilds cookies with path '/' and no expiry — so an expired session cookie is treated as valid forever (no client-side expiry), and Secure/HttpOnly flags are lost. This can leave a logged-out-server / logged-in-client mismatch and send cookies that should have expired.
  - **Fix:** Persist full cookie attributes (expiresAt, path, domain, secure, httpOnly) and filter expired cookies in loadForRequest, or use a maintained persistent cookie jar library.
- 🟡 **[bug] ApiClient JSON encoder uses encodeDefaults=true (sends nulls/defaults) vs iOS omitting nil**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/ApiClient.swift:84-88 (default JSONEncoder omits nil optionals)` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/ApiClient.kt:32-37`
  - Swift's JSONEncoder omits nil optional properties from the body. Kotlin Json with encodeDefaults=true serializes every property including null defaults, so Android request bodies include explicit `"field": null` and default values that iOS never sends. Backend Zod schemas that use .strict() or distinguish 'absent' vs 'null' (e.g. partial updates like GroupUpdate/GoalUpdate/IncomeUpdate) may reject the Android payload or null out fields that should be untouched.
  - **Fix:** Set encodeDefaults=false (and explicitNulls=false) on the request encoder so absent/optional fields are omitted like iOS; keep encodeDefaults only where a default literal must be sent.
- 🟡 **[visual] Money formatted currency on Android uses default locale grouping (e.g. EN locale shows '1,234.56 zł') vs iOS currency formatter**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Models/Money.swift:41-48; native-ios/Solvio/Core/Formatters.swift:8-20,63-66` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/Formatters.kt:16-29`
  - iOS Fmt.amount uses a cached NumberFormatter with currencyCode set and Locale.current, producing e.g. '1234,56 zł' on PL. Android uses NumberFormat.getCurrencyInstance(Locale.getDefault()) then overrides the currency — when the device locale is EN but currency is PLN, Android prints 'PLN1,234.56' or 'zł1,234.56' with the symbol leading and US grouping, which won't match iOS for cross-locale cases (e.g. EN UI + PLN amounts). There's no formatter caching either (minor perf).
  - **Fix:** Mirror iOS: format using the currency's typical locale (or at least match iOS symbol placement/grouping for PLN), and cache NumberFormat instances per currency code.
- ⚪ **[bug] Expense model adds non-iOS `isRecurring` field**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Models/Models.swift:43-62` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/models/Models.kt:74-91`
  - Android Expense has an extra `isRecurring: Boolean?` field not present in iOS Expense. Backend may send it (schema has isRecurring) but iOS deliberately omits it. Harmless to decode but a parity drift; if any Android UI relies on it, iOS lacks the equivalent and the two apps render differently.
  - **Fix:** Either remove isRecurring to match iOS exactly, or add it to iOS too — keep the field set identical across platforms.
- ⚪ **[behavior] AuditPriceComparison/AnalysisInsight etc. lost their iOS stable `id` semantics**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Models/Models.swift:639-656,694-708,759-772,804-818,834-840,886-905` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/models/Models.kt:646-667,700-708,759-772,796-832,880-892`
  - iOS computes Identifiable ids (e.g. AuditTopStore.id=store, AuditTopProduct.id=name, AnalysisInsight.id="type-title", PriceComparison.id=productName, ProductSearchResult.id="store-productName"). Android models drop these — for LazyColumn keying the Compose UI must supply equivalent keys or risk recomposition/animation glitches and bad diffing. This is a behavior risk for list rendering parity.
  - **Fix:** Add matching stable id extension vals (or pass the same key expressions to LazyColumn `key=`) so list identity matches iOS exactly.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Settlement id-derivation models (SettlementPerPerson/Debt) lose Identifiable id**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Models/Models.swift:400-419` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/models/Models.kt:407-426`
  - iOS SettlementPerPerson.id = memberId and SettlementDebt.id = "fromId->toId-amount". Android lacks these; debt rows in particular have no unique key (a member can owe another in multiple rows), risking duplicate-key/animation issues in Compose lists.
  - **Fix:** Provide the same composite id as a computed property / list key.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Fmt.initials uppercases per-char and matches iOS but splits differently on multiple spaces**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Formatters.swift:127-133` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/Formatters.kt:48-51`
  - iOS uses split(separator:" ") which drops empty subsequences (so 'A  B' → 'AB'); Android uses split(' ') which keeps empty strings, so a double space could yield an empty first token and a different initials result. Minor but a parity drift in avatar initials.
  - **Fix:** Filter blank tokens: `name.split(' ').filter { it.isNotBlank() }.take(2)...`.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Accept-Language header derived from app locale, not always 2-letter; User-Agent string differs**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/ApiClient.swift:333-339` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/ApiClient.kt:115-118,136-138`
  - iOS sets Accept-Language from Locale.preferredLanguages.first prefix(2) (the *device* language) and User-Agent 'Solvio-iOS/<appVersion>'. Android sets Accept-Language from the app's PL/EN override (locale.language.value.code) and User-Agent 'Solvio-Android/1.0' (hardcoded version). Backend language seeding/AI-prompt language may differ when the user's app-locale override disagrees with the device language; UA version is wrong.
  - **Fix:** Decide intended source of Accept-Language (likely the app override is actually better, but match the documented contract) and inject the real BuildConfig.VERSION_NAME into the User-Agent.
- ⚪ **[behavior] No URL/HTTP response cache (ETag/304) like iOS**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/ApiClient.swift:95-114` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/ApiClient.kt:175-185`
  - iOS configures URLCache (8MB mem / 32MB disk) with .useProtocolCachePolicy so Vercel ETag/Last-Modified 304s avoid re-downloading unchanged dashboard payloads. Android's OkHttpClient has no Cache configured, so every refresh re-pulls the full JSON blob — slower and more data than iOS.
  - **Fix:** Add an OkHttp Cache(dir, ~32MB) to the client builder so conditional GETs/304s work like iOS.
- ⚪ **[behavior] ChallengesRepo.complete and challenge `id` route exist on Android but not iOS; ChallengeCreate parity OK**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:292-305` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:176-186`
  - Android ChallengesRepo adds delete(id) and complete(id) (POST /api/personal/challenges/{id}/complete) that iOS does not implement (iOS only has list + create). If these endpoints don't exist on the backend, Android calls will 404; if they do, the two apps offer different challenge actions (parity drift either way).
  - **Fix:** Confirm the backend supports challenge delete/complete; if iOS is source of truth, remove them on Android (or add to iOS) to keep behavior identical.
- ⚪ **[behavior] ShoppingOptimizeRequest/NearbyStores default radius differs from iOS**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:466-480` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:292-299`
  - iOS NearbyStoresRepo.search default radius is 5000 (meters); Android NearbyStoresRepo.fetch default radius is 1500. The two apps will query a different search area by default, returning different store sets. Also iOS sends `lang` in the nearby-stores body; Android sends none.
  - **Fix:** Use radius default 5000 to match iOS and pass `lang`.
- ⚪ **[missing-feature] ShoppingAdvisorResponse missing iOS `dataSource` field**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Models/Models.swift:842-856` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/models/Models.kt:834-846`
  - iOS ShoppingAdvisorResponse has `dataSource: String?` ('live_web_search' vs 'estimate') driving a trust badge. Android's ShoppingAdvisorResponse omits dataSource, so the Android advisor card cannot show the ŻYWE/ESTYMATA badge that iOS shows.
  - **Fix:** Add `dataSource: String? = null` to ShoppingAdvisorResponse.
- ⚪ **[behavior] MaintenanceRepo.recategorize default lang null vs iOS 'pl'; response field set narrower**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:574-597` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:327-339`
  - iOS RecategorizeBody defaults lang to 'pl' and decodes RecategorizeResult{ok, processed, itemsUpdated, itemsAttempted, expensesUpdated}. Android RecategorizeBody.lang defaults to null and RecategorizeResponse only has itemsUpdated/expensesUpdated. With lang null the backend may seed/recategorize in the wrong language, and dropped fields mean any UI showing processed/attempted counts can't.
  - **Fix:** Default lang to 'pl' and add ok/processed/itemsAttempted to RecategorizeResponse to match iOS.
- ⚪ **[behavior] SessionMe carries a non-iOS userId field; /me contract is email-only on iOS**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Models/Models.swift:5-8` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/models/Models.kt:34-35`
  - iOS SessionMe is just { email }. Android SessionMe adds userId and is overloaded as the return type for sign-in/demo (which actually return different shapes). This conflation is the root cause of the sign-in/demo decoding bugs above; the model should match the iOS /me contract and separate types used for login/demo.
  - **Fix:** Make SessionMe { email } only; use SessionLoginResponse / DemoLoginResponse for those endpoints as iOS does.

## Receipts+Scan — parity ~48% (32 findings)

_The Android Receipts+Scan port reproduces the static surfaces (receipts list, receipt detail, virtual-receipt form, scan-queue widget, FAB sheet, camera screen, queue manager) with good token/visual fidelity, but the entire scan capture pipeline is structurally dead: ReceiptsListScreen's two CTAs, the FloatingScanFab, and the ScanFabSheet only flip router.showingScanSheet, which is never observed — so ScanFabSheet, CameraScanScreen, the photo picker, and ScanQueueWidget are never actually presented anywhere in the navigation tree. That means a user can never scan a receipt, pick from library, or see upload progress on Android, even though all the building blocks exist as orphaned composables. On top of that, the receipt detail screen drops several iOS sections (receipt image, currency-converter tile, per-item category tags, share toolbar button, items-count plural), the list lacks pull-to-refresh / swipe-to-delete / context-menu-delete / undoable delete and the OCR confirm-and-edit sheet is entirely missing, while the virtual-receipt form regresses the native date picker to a free-text field and adds an unrequested search bar. There are also localization gaps (NBEyebrow/eyebrows not uppercased, hardcoded behaviors) and the post-scan savings-insight toast is absent. Visually solid, functionally far behind iOS._

- 🔴 **[bug] Scan flow is completely unwired — FAB / CTAs / sheet never presented**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Root/MainTabView.swift:100-119,178-188` → Android: `features/root/MainTabScreen.kt:108-109,158; features/root/AppRouter.kt:15`
  - On iOS, tapping the FAB or the Receipts 'Take photo' CTA sets router.showingScanSheet and MainTabView presents ScanFabSheet via .sheet, which then drives camera / photo picker / virtual / quick-split (MainTabView.swift:100-104, 178-188). On Android, ReceiptsListScreen (onScanReceipt/onCreateVirtual) and MainTabScreen's FloatingScanFab all just set router.showingScanSheet.value = true (MainTabScreen.kt:108-109,158), but NOTHING reads showingScanSheet anywhere (grep shows only writes). ScanFabSheet, CameraScanScreen, and the photo picker are never composed in MainTabScreen or RootScreen. Net effect: the user cannot scan a receipt, open the camera, or pick from library AT ALL on Android.
  - **Fix:** In MainTabScreen, observe router.showingScanSheet and present ScanFabSheet(onScanReceipt/onVirtualReceipt/onPickFromLibrary). Add a rememberLauncherForActivityResult(PickMultipleVisualMedia) for library, navigate to CameraScanScreen for camera, and present VirtualReceiptCreateScreen for virtual. Wire each ScanFabSheet option to the matching launcher exactly like MainTabView.handleScanSheetDismiss.
- 🔴 **[missing-feature] ScanQueueWidget never rendered — no background-upload progress UI**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Root/MainTabView.swift:58-59` → Android: `absent (core/ui/ScanQueueWidget.kt defined but uncalled)`
  - iOS pins ScanQueueWidget() above the tab bar in MainTabView (MainTabView.swift:58-59) so active OCR uploads show a floating progress chip on every tab. Android's ScanQueueWidget composable exists (core/ui/ScanQueueWidget.kt) but is never called anywhere (grep: only its own definition). Combined with the dead scan flow, the queue manager's items StateFlow is never surfaced to the user.
  - **Fix:** Render ScanQueueWidget() inside MainTabScreen's Box, stacked above NBTabBar in the BottomEnd/Bottom column, passing onOpenReceipt that navigates dashboardNav to ReceiptDetail.build(id). Match iOS placement (above tab bar, below FAB).
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Receipt image card missing on detail screen**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/ReceiptDetailView.swift:46,249-278` → Android: `features/receipts/ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:135-153`
  - iOS ReceiptDetailView renders imageCard(r) showing the receipt photo (AsyncImage with empty/success/failure states, 'imageUnavailable' fallback) between the e-receipt card and items list (ReceiptDetailView.swift:46,249-278). Android ReceiptDetailScreen never renders the image — it jumps from EReceiptCard straight to ItemsHeader (ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:138-153). receiptDetail.imageUnavailable key exists on Android but is unused.
  - **Fix:** Add an ImageCard composable using Coil AsyncImage on r.imageUrl with muted placeholder/failure state showing receiptDetail.imageUnavailable, clipped to Radius.md with widthThin border, inserted between EReceiptCard and ItemsHeader.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Currency-converter tile missing on receipt detail**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/ReceiptDetailView.swift:50-53,393-407` → Android: `absent (features/receipts/ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:135-168)`
  - iOS shows a CurrencyConverterCard (PLN/EUR/USD glance) for the receipt total when total>0 (ReceiptDetailView.swift:50-53,393-407, uses converter.* keys). Android ReceiptDetailScreen has no converter tile; converter.* keys exist on Android but are unused here.
  - **Fix:** Add the converter card after QrCard, gated on r.total?.toDouble() > 0, reusing the shared CurrencyConverterCard component with currency = r.currency ?: PLN and the converter.* localized strings, matching iOS targets logic (base PLN/EUR/USD else prepend source).
- 🟠 **[missing-popup] OCR confirm-and-edit sheet (OcrConfirmSheet) entirely missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/ReceiptsListView.swift:424-720` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS defines OcrConfirmSheet (ReceiptsListView.swift:424-720): vendor/date/total/currency fields, receipt-level category picker, editable line items with per-item category chips, Save and 'Split from receipt' actions, and a Discard (destructive) toolbar action. Although the current iOS list uses the background-queue flow, the confirm sheet is still part of the Receipts feature and wired via OcrDraft/onSplit. Android has no OcrConfirmSheet, no category pickers, and no save-and-split path — the ocrConfirm.* and quickSplit.fromReceipt keys exist on Android but are unused.
  - **Fix:** Port OcrConfirmSheet as a Compose screen/bottom-sheet with NBTextField fields, horizontal category chip rows (receipt + per-item), editable item rows, Save (persistEdits via ReceiptsRepo.updateItems then detail) and optional save-and-split, plus a Discard destructive action calling ReceiptsRepo.delete. Load categories from settings.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] List swipe-to-delete + context-menu delete missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/ReceiptsListView.swift:317-324,370-377` → Android: `features/receipts/ReceiptsListScreen.kt:231-274`
  - iOS receiptRow supports trailing swipe-to-delete (full swipe) and a long-press contextMenu delete, both routing through a destructive confirmationDialog and undoable optimistic delete (ReceiptsListView.swift:317-324,370-377,194-222). Android ReceiptRow is a plain clickable row with no swipe action, no context menu, and no delete affordance at all (ReceiptsListScreen.kt:231-274).
  - **Fix:** Add a SwipeToDismiss(Box) trailing delete on each ReceiptRow and a long-press dropdown 'Delete', both opening a confirm dialog (receipts.deleteReceipt) and performing an optimistic remove with a 5s undoable toast (toast.undoable) before committing ReceiptsRepo.delete.
- 🟠 **[behavior] Undoable optimistic delete + 5s undo window not implemented**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/ReceiptsListView.swift:194-222` → Android: `features/receipts/ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:178-193`
  - iOS scheduleReceiptDelete removes the row optimistically, shows an undoable toast ('common.itemDeleted' / 'common.undo'), and defers the real delete 5s with cancel-on-undo + restore-on-error (ReceiptsListView.swift:194-222). Android ReceiptDetailScreen.delete just deletes immediately with success/error toast (ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:178-193); the list has no delete at all. No undo anywhere.
  - **Fix:** Implement optimistic-remove + toast.undoable with a 5s coroutine delay that commits ReceiptsRepo.delete and restores the row on cancel/error, both on the list (after swipe) and consistent with detail delete.
- 🟠 **[behavior] Virtual receipt date field downgraded to free-text instead of date picker**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/VirtualReceiptCreateView.swift:42-47` → Android: `features/receipts/VirtualReceiptCreateScreen.kt:158-166`
  - iOS uses a native DatePicker (compact, .date) bound to a Date, formatting to yyyy-MM-dd on save (VirtualReceiptCreateView.swift:42-47,275-281). Android replaces it with a plain NBTextField where the user must type 'YYYY-MM-DD' by hand (VirtualReceiptCreateScreen.kt:158-166, explicit 'TODO: Compose date picker' comment). Bad UX and easy to enter an invalid date that the backend may reject.
  - **Fix:** Replace the date NBTextField with a clickable field that opens a Material3 DatePickerDialog, storing the selected LocalDate and formatting to yyyy-MM-dd, mirroring iOS.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Pull-to-refresh missing on list and detail**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/ReceiptsListView.swift:68-76; ReceiptDetailView.swift:80-89` → Android: `features/receipts/ReceiptsListScreen.kt:109-187; ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:125-171`
  - iOS ReceiptsListView and ReceiptDetailView both have .refreshable with light/ success haptics (ReceiptsListView.swift:68-76; ReceiptDetailView.swift:80-89). Android uses plain LazyColumn with no PullToRefresh / SwipeRefresh on either screen.
  - **Fix:** Wrap both LazyColumns in PullToRefreshBox (material3) calling vm.load() / vm.load(id); add haptic feedback on pull and on success.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Share toolbar button + ShareSheet missing on receipt detail**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/ReceiptDetailView.swift:64-78` → Android: `features/receipts/ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:108-171`
  - iOS detail has a top-bar trailing share button (square.and.arrow.up) that opens a ShareSheet for the public URL (ReceiptDetailView.swift:64-78,99-103), in ADDITION to the in-card Share button. Android only has the in-card Share ActionButton (ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:283-289); there is no top-bar share affordance and no nav title bar at all (it's a bare LazyColumn). receiptDetail.shareReceipt key exists but is unused.
  - **Fix:** Add a top app bar (or overlay icon) with a share action firing the same ACTION_SEND chooser, labeled via receiptDetail.shareReceipt. Also add the navigationTitle receiptDetail.title which Android currently omits entirely.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Per-item category tags missing in detail line items**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/ReceiptDetailView.swift:325-329` → Android: `features/receipts/ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:376-379`
  - iOS itemRow shows an NBTag for the item's category — resolved name via store.categories, falling back to first 6 chars of the UUID (ReceiptDetailView.swift:325-329). Android LineItemRow only renders quantity and unitPrice tags, never the category (ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:376-379). ReceiptItem.categoryId is parsed but never displayed.
  - **Fix:** Pass categories into ReceiptDetailScreen (load from settings/store) and add an NBTag for item.categoryId showing the resolved category name, falling back to categoryId.take(6), matching iOS.
- 🟡 **[behavior] Unrequested search bar added to Receipts list (not in iOS)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/ReceiptsListView.swift:296-327 (no search)` → Android: `features/receipts/ReceiptsListScreen.kt:107,148-177`
  - Android ReceiptsListScreen adds a search NBTextField and client-side filtering by vendor/date (ReceiptsListScreen.kt:107,148-156,173-177). iOS ReceiptsListView has no search field or filtering at all — the archive is a plain list. This is a feature divergence (extra control + receipts.search string surfaced) that breaks pixel/behavior parity.
  - **Fix:** Remove the search field and filtering to match iOS, OR confirm it as an intentional Android enhancement. For strict parity, remove it.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Post-scan savings-insight actionable toast absent**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/ScanQueueManager.swift:82-88,338-359; Features/Root/MainTabView.swift:69-81` → Android: `absent (core/ScanQueueManager.kt)`
  - iOS ScanQueueManager runs maybeAnalyzeSavings after a single-receipt scan and sets savingsInsight; MainTabView shows an actionable success toast deep-linking to the Deals tab's receipt analysis (ScanQueueManager.swift:82-88,338-359; MainTabView.swift:69-81). Android ScanQueueManager has no savingsInsight, no ReceiptAnalyzeRepo call, and no analysis enum (ScanQueueManager.kt) — and even if it did, there is no MainTabScreen observer. scanInsight.* keys are also absent on Android.
  - **Fix:** Add savingsInsight StateFlow + maybeAnalyzeSavings (ReceiptAnalyzeRepo.analyze) on single-item scans, add scanInsight.savingsTitle/savingsDesc/action keys, and an actionable toast in MainTabScreen that switches to Deals and sets a pendingAnalyzeReceiptId.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] ScanFabSheet options diverge: Android has 3, iOS has 4 (no Quick Split)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Root/MainTabView.swift:576-607,629-666` → Android: `features/root/ScanFabSheet.kt:89-109,115-156`
  - iOS ScanFabSheet offers 4 options: Camera (primary), Photo Library, Virtual receipt, and Quick Split (MainTabView.swift:576-607). Android ScanFabSheet offers only 3: Scan receipt, Virtual receipt, Pick from library — no Quick Split entry (ScanFabSheet.kt:89-109). The quickSplit.title / scanFab.quickSplitSub keys exist on Android but the option is missing. Also iOS uses camera.fill / photo.on.rectangle / square.and.pencil / person.2.fill icons and a chevron.right trailing affordance per row; Android uses DocumentScanner/Edit/PhotoLibrary with no chevron.
  - **Fix:** Add a 4th 'Quick Split' ActionTile (person icon) wired to the standalone quick-split sheet, reorder/relabel to match iOS, and add a trailing chevron.right per row to match iOS option layout.
- 🟡 **[behavior] ScanQueueManager: sequential (1) vs iOS parallel (maxConcurrent=2) + no aggressive 413 retry**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/ScanQueueManager.swift:92,197-211,247-320` → Android: `core/ScanQueueManager.kt:131-177`
  - iOS processes up to maxConcurrent=2 uploads in parallel (ScanQueueManager.swift:92,197-211) and on payloadTooLarge does an aggressive re-resize (1024px/2MB) + one retry before failing (swift:247-250,266-320). Android drains strictly one-at-a-time (single pumpJob + mutex, ScanQueueManager.kt:131-177) and on PayloadTooLarge immediately marks Failed with no aggressive-compression retry (kt:169-170). Batches of 10 receipts are ~2x slower and large images fail outright instead of retrying.
  - **Fix:** Process up to 2 items concurrently (launch 2 drain workers or a semaphore of 2). On PayloadTooLarge, decode bytes → scaleToMaxDimension(1024) → toJpegBytes(2MB) → retry once before Failed, mirroring iOS retryWithAggressiveCompression.
- 🟡 **[bug] ScanQueueManager: 'saved' items keep full JPEG bytes in memory (RAM regression)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/ScanQueueManager.swift:53-63` → Android: `core/ScanQueueManager.kt:46-63,151-161`
  - iOS nulls out imageData when an item reaches .saved to avoid holding ~4MB per receipt forever after a 10+ batch (ScanQueueManager.swift:53-63 didSet). Android's Item keeps imageBytes for the lifetime of the queue with no clearing on Saved (ScanQueueManager.kt:46-63,151-161) — only clearCompleted() frees them. A large batch can pin tens of MB of bitmaps+JPEGs.
  - **Fix:** On transition to Status.Saved, copy the item with imageBytes = ByteArray(0) (keep only thumbnail), matching iOS. Failed items must retain bytes so retry() still works.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Receipt list row thumbnail never loads remote image (placeholder only)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/ReceiptsListView.swift:380-405` → Android: `features/receipts/ReceiptsListScreen.kt:243-254`
  - iOS receiptRow thumbnail loads r.imageUrl via AsyncImage, falling back to a branded VendorLogo or doc icon (ReceiptsListView.swift:380-405). Android ReceiptRow always shows a static Description icon placeholder with an explicit 'TODO: AsyncImage via Coil if r.imageUrl != null' comment (ReceiptsListScreen.kt:243-254). Scanned receipts with photos show no thumbnail.
  - **Fix:** Use Coil AsyncImage on r.imageUrl with the muted/bordered box as placeholder and the Description icon as fallback; optionally add a vendor-logo fallback to match iOS VendorLogo.
- ⚪ **[copy-l10n] Detail items-count uses non-pluralized string + no item-row a11y compose**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/ReceiptDetailView.swift:115-134; Core/L10n.swift:731-734` → Android: `features/receipts/ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:218-221`
  - iOS hero uses tPlural('receiptDetail.itemsCount', count) with one/few/many variants (ReceiptDetailView.swift:131-134), and the list/hero append a pluralized 'pozycji/items'. Android Hero just renders '· $n ${receipts.itemsSuffix}' (ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:218-221) reusing receipts.itemsSuffix instead of the receiptDetail.itemsCount plural set that exists in iOS L10n. Polish plural correctness (pozycja/pozycje/pozycji) is lost.
  - **Fix:** Add receiptDetail.itemsCount plural handling to the Android locale and use it (with Polish one/few/many rules) in Hero and the list row instead of receipts.itemsSuffix.
- ⚪ **[visual] Receipts list empty state passes no icon (iOS uses doc.text.viewfinder)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/ReceiptsListView.swift:286-290` → Android: `features/receipts/ReceiptsListScreen.kt:164-167`
  - iOS NBEmptyState for receipts uses systemImage 'doc.text.viewfinder' (ReceiptsListView.swift:286-290). Android calls NBEmptyState(title, subtitle) without the icon arg (ReceiptsListScreen.kt:164-167); NBEmptyState renders no icon circle when icon==null, so the empty state is missing the framed icon iOS shows.
  - **Fix:** Pass icon = Icons.Filled.DocumentScanner (or equivalent) to NBEmptyState so the framed icon matches iOS.
- ⚪ **[visual] Archive eyebrow and other eyebrows not uppercased**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/ReceiptsListView.swift:180,298` → Android: `features/receipts/ReceiptsListScreen.kt:138,171`
  - iOS uppercases the archive eyebrow: NBEyebrow(text: locale.t('receipts.archive').uppercased()) (ReceiptsListView.swift:298) and elsewhere uses .textCase(.uppercase). Android passes the raw key NBEyebrow(text = locale.t('receipts.archive')) (ReceiptsListScreen.kt:171). Need to confirm NBEyebrow uppercases internally; if it does not, the archive header (and CTA virtualReceipt.eyebrow which iOS .capitalizes) will render with wrong casing.
  - **Fix:** Ensure NBEyebrow applies uppercase styling (textCase) like iOS; for the virtual CTA title iOS uses .capitalized — replicate capitalization. Verify against core/ui/NBComponents.kt NBEyebrow at line 58.
- ⚪ **[visual] ScanFabSheet header layout differs from iOS (eyebrow+close vs title+subtitle)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Root/MainTabView.swift:565-573,625` → Android: `features/root/ScanFabSheet.kt:75-87`
  - iOS sheet shows a pageTitle 'scanFab.title' and a caption 'scanFab.subtitle' header, with a Cancel toolbar button and .presentationDetents([.height(380), .medium]) (MainTabView.swift:568-573,616-626). Android shows an NBEyebrow 'scanFab.title' plus a small Close 'X' icon and no subtitle, in a fully-expanded ModalBottomSheet (ScanFabSheet.kt:75-87). The scanFab.subtitle key exists on Android but is unused; visual hierarchy (large title vs tiny eyebrow) differs.
  - **Fix:** Render scanFab.title as pageTitle/sectionTitle plus scanFab.subtitle caption; keep a cancel affordance. Consider a partially-expanded sheet height matching iOS 380pt.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Camera is single-shot only — no multi-capture / batch parity with library**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Root/MainTabView.swift:114-142` → Android: `features/root/ScanFabSheet.kt:103-109 (onPickFromLibrary unconnected)`
  - Both platforms treat the camera as single-shot and the library picker as multi-select (iOS maxSelectionCount 20; Android should use PickMultipleVisualMedia). This part matches in intent, but Android's library picker is not implemented at all (the onPickFromLibrary callback in ScanFabSheet is never connected to a launcher because the sheet is never shown). Flagging so the eventual wiring uses PickMultipleVisualMedia(maxItems=20) → toJpegBytes → ScanQueueManager.enqueue, matching iOS batch toasts (scanQueue.batchSaved / batchSavedSingle).
  - **Fix:** When wiring the sheet, register rememberLauncherForActivityResult(PickMultipleVisualMedia(maxItems=20)); for each uri decode→scaleToMaxDimension(1600)→toJpegBytes→enqueue; show scanQueue.batchSavedSingle for 1 image else scanQueue.batchSaved plural.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Library image-load failure feedback missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/ReceiptsListView.swift:107-145` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS ReceiptsListView walks picked items individually and surfaces a toast.error counting failed assets ('N receipts.imageLoadFailed') so the user isn't left wondering (ReceiptsListView.swift:107-145). The Android library path is unimplemented, and the receipts.imageLoadFailed key exists but is unused — when wired it must replicate the per-asset failure counting + error toast.
  - **Fix:** In the library launcher callback, decode each uri in a try/catch, count failures, and toast.error(toast.error title, '$failures ${receipts.imageLoadFailed}') if any failed, matching iOS.
- ⚪ **[behavior] ScanQueueManager: cancelled-upload requeue behavior absent on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/ScanQueueManager.swift:239-246` → Android: `core/ScanQueueManager.kt:171-172,183-191`
  - iOS catches ApiError.cancelled and requeues the item as .pending (so a transient URLSession cancel doesn't permanently fail it) (ScanQueueManager.swift:239-246). Android's drain has no ApiError.Cancelled branch — a cancelled upload falls into the generic ApiError handler and is marked Failed with friendly('Cancelled') text (ScanQueueManager.kt:171-172,183-191 — ApiError.Cancelled is exhaustive-matched to localized 'errors.cancelled' Failed).
  - **Fix:** Add a catch for ApiError.Cancelled that sets the item back to Status.Pending (and lets the pump retry) instead of marking it Failed.
- ⚪ **[behavior] ScanQueueManager.Status.Processing is dead state — chip never shows 'processing'**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/ScanQueueManager.swift:213-264 (uploading→processing→saved)` → Android: `core/ScanQueueManager.kt:141,151-167`
  - Android defines Status.Processing and the widget handles it (ScanQueueWidget.kt:245,253,267), but the drain loop only ever sets Uploading then Saved/Failed — Processing is never assigned (ScanQueueManager.kt:141,151-167). iOS likewise transitions uploading→processing→saved conceptually but Android skips Processing entirely. Minor: the 'processing'/'processingDetail' strings are unreachable.
  - **Fix:** Set Status.Processing after the upload request returns but before persisting the parsed result (or drop the state for parity). Keep behavior consistent with iOS staging.
- ⚪ **[behavior] VirtualReceiptCreate validation: priceInvalid shown but no qty/price field-level validation; toast type differs**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/VirtualReceiptCreateView.swift:84-88` → Android: `features/receipts/VirtualReceiptCreateScreen.kt:287-291`
  - iOS shows toast.warning('validation.vendorRequired') on a blocked save (VirtualReceiptCreateView.swift:86) and uses inline red 'validation.priceInvalid' when vendor present but cannot save. Android uses toast.error('validation.vendorRequired') (VirtualReceiptCreateScreen.kt:289) — warning vs error styling differs. Otherwise inline error logic matches.
  - **Fix:** Use toast.warning (if available) instead of toast.error for the blocked-save case to match iOS severity/color, and add haptic warning.
- ⚪ **[visual] VirtualReceiptCreate empty-items state differs (iOS shows NBEmptyState, Android always seeds 1 row)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/VirtualReceiptCreateView.swift:150-167` → Android: `features/receipts/VirtualReceiptCreateScreen.kt:106,201-211`
  - iOS allows items to be empty and renders an NBEmptyState with an 'Add item' action when so (VirtualReceiptCreateView.swift:150-162). Android seeds the list with one EditableItem and gates delete on items.size>1 (VirtualReceiptCreateScreen.kt:106,201-211,348), so the empty state (virtualReceipt.itemsEmptyTitle/Subtitle equivalents) is never shown. virtualReceipt.empty key exists but unused.
  - **Fix:** Allow items to be fully emptied and render NBEmptyState with an add-item action when empty, matching iOS; allow deleting the last row.
- ⚪ **[visual] VirtualReceiptCreate notes field is single-line NBTextField, iOS uses multi-line TextEditor**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/VirtualReceiptCreateView.swift:64-75` → Android: `features/receipts/VirtualReceiptCreateScreen.kt:264-272`
  - iOS notes uses a TextEditor with minHeight 72 and notes a11y hints (VirtualReceiptCreateView.swift:64-75). Android uses a single-line NBTextField with empty placeholder and no min height (VirtualReceiptCreateScreen.kt:264-272), and omits the notes a11y label/hint. Multi-line notes UX is lost.
  - **Fix:** Make the notes field multi-line (singleLine=false, minLines ~3 / minHeight 72.dp) and add contentDescription for accessibility.
- ⚪ **[visual] VirtualReceiptCreate header is custom row instead of nav title + cancel toolbar**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/VirtualReceiptCreateView.swift:105-114` → Android: `features/receipts/VirtualReceiptCreateScreen.kt:123-140`
  - iOS presents VirtualReceiptCreateView inside a NavigationStack with navigationTitle 'virtualReceipt.title' and a cancellationAction Cancel toolbar (VirtualReceiptCreateView.swift:105-114), as a medium/large detent sheet. Android draws a manual Row with Cancel text + centered title + a 56dp spacer hack to fake centering (VirtualReceiptCreateScreen.kt:123-140), full-screen rather than a sheet. Layout/structure diverges from iOS.
  - **Fix:** Present as a bottom sheet (or with a proper TopAppBar) with title and a Cancel action; remove the spacer-hack centering.
- ⚪ **[bug] Receipt detail back navigation / delete refresh signal is a no-op**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/ReceiptDetailView.swift:469-473` → Android: `features/receipts/ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:184-186`
  - After delete on Android, the code calls ScanQueueManager.get().let { /* reuse refresh signal */ } (ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:185) which does literally nothing — the list is not told to refresh. iOS calls store.didMutateReceipts() to invalidate caches (ReceiptDetailView.swift:471). Although ReceiptsListScreen reloads on queue.refreshNeeded, a manual delete never emits it, so returning to the list still shows the deleted receipt until a new scan saves.
  - **Fix:** Emit a refresh signal on delete — e.g. expose ScanQueueManager.signalRefresh() (tryEmit on refreshNeeded) or a shared receipts-invalidation flow, and call it after a successful delete so the list reloads.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Detail line-item price selection order differs (totalPrice-first vs displayPrice logic)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/ReceiptDetailView.swift:312-316` → Android: `features/receipts/ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:368`
  - iOS itemRow shows item.displayPrice (a model computed property) for the right-aligned price (ReceiptDetailView.swift:312-316). Android picks item.totalPrice?.raw ?: unitPrice?.raw ?: price?.raw (ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:368). If iOS displayPrice prefers a different field (e.g. price/total) the displayed amount can differ per item. Verify ReceiptItem.displayPrice semantics on iOS and match exactly.
  - **Fix:** Confirm iOS ReceiptItem.displayPrice resolution order (Models.swift) and replicate the exact precedence in LineItemRow.
- ⚪ **[visual] Delete confirm dialog uses custom Box overlay instead of native AlertDialog / confirmationDialog**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Receipts/ReceiptDetailView.swift:90-98` → Android: `features/receipts/ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:426-479`
  - iOS uses a system confirmationDialog (action sheet) for delete with destructive Delete + Cancel (ReceiptDetailView.swift:90-98). Android renders a hand-rolled full-screen scrim Box with a neobrutalist card (ReceiptDetailScreen.kt:426-479). This is a reasonable NB-styled choice but diverges from the iOS action-sheet presentation and from how delete confirms appear elsewhere; ensure consistency across the app.
  - **Fix:** Either standardize on the NB custom dialog app-wide or use a Material AlertDialog styled with NB tokens; keep destructive Delete + Cancel ordering and titles consistent with iOS.

## Deals/Okazje — parity ~35% (30 findings)

_The Android OkazjeHubScreen is a heavily simplified reimplementation of the iOS OkazjeHubView. It keeps the four core blocks (header, trending promos, AI shopping list, analyze receipt, launcher tiles) but is missing nearly all of the rich shell iOS added: the first-launch onboarding banner, the Apple-Wallet-style hero card (total savings, offer count, AI-powered badge, manual refresh, freshness pill), the entire filter bar (All/Personalized/Ending soon/Top discount/per-store) and its sort logic, the metadata footer, the collapsible disclosure-card pattern for the two AI tools, show-more/less expansion of promos, pull-to-refresh, the staged NBProgressCard loader, the persisted shopping list, the location toggle, submit-on-return row creation, urgency pills, promo-type badges, personalized card styling, and the deep-link-to-analyze flow. The trending card layout is also structurally different (horizontal row vs iOS's vertical card with vendor logo, large promo price, badges and urgency). On top of the missing features there are several concrete bugs: trending always shows max 3 with no expansion, NBEmptyState is double-wrapped in NBCard, the analyze result card omits per-item savings/cheaper-at formatting parity, the analyze hero uses a wrong eyebrow ("savings" instead of vendor/eyebrow), several L10n keys the iOS uses are completely absent from the Android L10n table, and the cheaperAtFmt key has a different argument arity. Roughly a third of the feature is present._

- 🔴 **[missing-feature] Hero card (savings/count/AI badge/refresh/freshness) missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:68,269-427` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS renders a dominant hero card at the top: eyebrow OKAZJE, AI-powered pill, manual refresh button, a large green totalPotentialSavings amount (or offer count fallback), savings label, an offers-count tag and a freshness pill (Live/X min ago/Stale). Android has none of this — it jumps straight from NBScreenHeader to the trending list, so totalPotentialSavings, cacheState, fetchedAt and the refresh control are never surfaced.
  - **Fix:** Add a heroCard Composable replicating iOS: gradient background (success.opacity .10 to background), thin border, Radius.lg, Shadow.md; row 1 = deals.heroEyebrow + AI badge + refresh button; row 2 = Fmt.amount(totalPotentialSavings) in black(34)/success with deals.heroSavingsLabel, fallback to deals.heroCountBig plural / deals.heroLoading; row 3 = offers-count NBTag + freshness pill computed from cacheState/fetchedAt. Add all deals.hero*/deals.freshness*/deals.aiPowered/deals.refresh L10n keys (currently absent).
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Filter bar and filtering/sort logic missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:69-71,106-194,434-486` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS shows a horizontal chip filter bar (All / Personalized / Ending soon / Top discount / per-store chips) when shouldShowFilters (>=4 promos and personalization or >=2 stores), and applies a default sort (personalized -> soonest expiry -> biggest discount). Android just takes the first 3 of personalizedDeals+promotions with no filtering, no sorting and no filter UI at all.
  - **Fix:** Port the OkazjeFilter enum, shouldShowFilters/filteredPromotions/discountPct/daysUntilExpiry logic and a scrollable Row of filter chips (active = foreground bg / background text, inactive = muted, capsule, thin border). Add deals.filterAll/filterPersonalized/filterEndingSoon/filterTopDiscount L10n keys (absent).
- 🟠 **[visual] Trending card layout structurally different**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:1052-1194` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/deals/OkazjeHubScreen.kt:256-314`
  - iOS trending card is a tall vertical card: top row vendor logo (VendorLogo 40 + tag.fill fallback) + store eyebrow + personalized 'sparkles for-you' tag + open-in-new arrow; then bold(17) product name (2 lines); optional promoDescription; price row with bold(22) green promo price + strikethrough regular + promo-type/discount badge; bottom row with urgency clock pill + valid-until date; personalized cards get an info-tinted gradient bg and info border. Android renders a compact horizontal Row: a 40dp emoji box (🏷), product name in bodyMedium, store as caption, and an inline price/discount/validUntil row, plus a ↗ glyph. No vendor logo, no personalized styling, no promoDescription, no urgency pill, no promo-type badge, much smaller promo price.
  - **Fix:** Rewrite TrendingCard as a vertical Column matching iOS: VendorLogo equivalent, store eyebrow (mono10 uppercase muted), personalized tag, productName bold(17), promoDescription caption, price row (promo bold(22) success + strikethrough regular mono(11) + promoTypeBadge/discount NBTag), urgency pill + validUntil row; apply info gradient + info border when matchesPurchases==true. Use Theme.card otherwise.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Trending hard-capped at 3, no show-more/less expansion**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:908-936,1024-1046` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/deals/OkazjeHubScreen.kt:238`
  - iOS shows 3 promos collapsed and a 'show more (+N) / show less' button expanding up to 10, with a 'X of Y shown' count next to the section header. Android always shows exactly take(3) with no count and no expansion control.
  - **Fix:** Add trendingExpanded state, cap at 3/10, render a full-width show-more/less button (deals.showMoreFmt/deals.showLess) when filteredPromotions.count>3, and a deals.countShownFmt count next to the section header. Add those L10n keys (absent).
- 🟠 **[visual] AI tools not collapsible disclosure cards**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:72,518-637` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/deals/OkazjeHubScreen.kt:318-389,667-724`
  - iOS wraps both AI features (shopping list, analyze receipt) under a single 'NARZEDZIA AI' section as two collapsible disclosure cards (icon circle + title + count badge + subtitle + rotating chevron), collapsed by default, with a divider when expanded. Android renders both sections always-expanded as plain sectioned columns with their own NBSectionHeader + subtitle text, no disclosure, no count badges, no collapsing.
  - **Fix:** Wrap shopping list and analyze bodies in a disclosureCard Composable (icon ZStack circle tinted, title + optional count badge, subtitle, animated chevron rotation, expand/collapse state) under a shared NBSectionHeader using deals.aiToolsEyebrow/deals.aiToolsTitle. Surface item/receipt counts as badges (shoppingList.itemsCountFmt / analyze.receiptsCountFmt). Add deals.aiToolsEyebrow/aiToolsTitle/shoppingList.itemsCountFmt/analyze.receiptsCountFmt L10n keys (absent).
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Location toggle missing; lat/lng never sent**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:1256-1277,2063-2105,2120-2183` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/deals/OkazjeHubScreen.kt:135-145,370-375`
  - iOS has a 'Uzyj mojej lokalizacji' toggle (default on) that fetches a one-shot CLLocation (5s timeout) and passes lat/lng to ShoppingRepo.optimize so the backend prefers nearby chains. Android has no toggle and runOptimize never sets lat/lng (ShoppingOptimizeRequest is built with only items/lang/currency), so nearby-store preference is permanently disabled.
  - **Fix:** Add a tappable toggle row (shoppingList.useLocation) defaulting on, request FINE_LOCATION, fetch a one-shot fused location with a 5s timeout, and pass lat/lng into ShoppingOptimizeRequest when enabled. shoppingList.useLocation key exists in Android L10n (741) but is unused.
- 🟠 **[visual] Analyze result hero uses wrong eyebrow and omits date/savings-label/paid-vs-best stats**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:732-767` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/deals/OkazjeHubScreen.kt:768-789`
  - iOS analyze result card header: eyebrow analyze.resultEyebrow (ANALIZA), vendor in cardTitle, date, dataSource badge on the left; right side shows analyze.savingsLabel + colored potentialSavings (warning if >0 else success). Then a two-stat row (analyze.paid / analyze.bestPossible) using AppFont.amount. Android instead uses NBEyebrow analyze.savings (the wrong key/text), shows vendor in muted bodyMedium (not a title), no date, then a big hero() potentialSavings always in success color, and a paid/best row in bodyMedium not amount. The savings color never turns warning when overpaying.
  - **Fix:** Match iOS: eyebrow analyze.resultEyebrow, vendor in cardTitle/foreground, render date, savings on the right with analyze.savingsLabel and color = if(potentialSavings>0) warning else success. Use SolvioFonts.amount for the paid/best stats. Add analyze.resultEyebrow if not wired (exists in L10n at 826 but unused).
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] First-time onboarding banner missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:65-67,217-259` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS shows a dismissible 'sparkles' onboarding banner the first time the user opens Okazje (gated by @AppStorage seenOkazjeOnboarding), explaining the AI scrapes leaflets every 6h. Android has no onboarding banner at all.
  - **Fix:** Add an onboarding banner Composable gated by a DataStore/SharedPreferences flag (e.g. solvio.seenOkazjeOnboarding), with sparkles icon, deals.onboardingTitle/deals.onboardingBody copy, an X dismiss button, warning-tinted background and thin border. Add the missing L10n keys deals.onboardingTitle and deals.onboardingBody.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Promo-type badge mapping missing on trending card**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:1130-1142,1199-1214` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/deals/OkazjeHubScreen.kt:299-301`
  - iOS maps offer.promoType to human badges (1+1, 2 ZA 1, 3 ZA 2, deals.promoBuyGet, deals.promoAppOnly, deals.promoMultipack, percent) via promoTypeBadge(); falls back to discount string. Android trending card only ever shows the raw offer.discount string and ignores promoType entirely.
  - **Fix:** Add a promoTypeBadge(offer) helper mirroring iOS and prefer it over the discount string when non-null. Add deals.promoBuyGet/deals.promoAppOnly/deals.promoMultipack L10n keys (absent).
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Urgency pill (ending-soon) missing on trending card**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:1146-1166,1216-1225` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS shows a clock-icon urgency pill on each promo when validUntil is within 3 days (Expired/Today/Tomorrow/N days) with destructive/warning tint. Android shows no urgency indicator, only the plain validUntil date string.
  - **Fix:** Add urgencyLabel(offer) using daysUntilExpiry and render a capsule pill with clock icon + tint when within 3 days. Add deals.urgencyExpired/urgencyToday/urgencyTomorrow/urgencyDaysFmt L10n keys (absent).
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Pull-to-refresh missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:98-103` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS has .refreshable that force-refetches promotions + receipts with haptics. Android LazyColumn has no pull-to-refresh; the user can only reload via the (also-missing) hero refresh button.
  - **Fix:** Wrap the LazyColumn in a PullToRefreshBox (material3) that calls vm.loadPromotions()+vm.loadReceipts(). Add haptic feedback on trigger to match iOS.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Staged NBProgressCard loader for shopping optimize missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:1303-1314` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/deals/OkazjeHubScreen.kt:366-376`
  - While optimizing, iOS shows an NBProgressCard with 4 cycling stage labels (Przegladam gazetki / Pobieram ceny / Porownuje / Finalizuje) and a 30s estimated progress bar. Android only shows a spinner inside the button (loading=isLoading) — no progress card, no stage copy, so a 12-50s call feels broken.
  - **Fix:** Render an NBProgressCard equivalent below the optimize button when OptimizeState.Loading, cycling shoppingList.stageLeaflets/stagePrices/stageCompare/stageFinalize over ~30s. Those stage keys exist in Android L10n (1367-1370) but are unused; wire a progress card Composable.
- 🟡 **[behavior] Shopping list not persisted across sessions**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:2003,2013-2043,2099` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/deals/OkazjeHubScreen.kt:171`
  - iOS persists the last successfully optimized shopping list to UserDefaults and restores it on next launch (ShoppingListVM.loadPersisted / persistItems). Android initializes items to a single empty row every time (remember { mutableStateOf(listOf(ShoppingItemDraft())) }) and never persists, so the list is also lost on rotation/process death.
  - **Fix:** Persist non-empty items to DataStore on successful optimize and restore them when the screen first composes; hoist the items state into the ViewModel (with SavedStateHandle or rememberSaveable) so it survives recomposition/config changes.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Deep-link to analyze a just-scanned receipt missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:92-97` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS reacts to router.pendingAnalyzeReceiptId (set by the post-scan savings toast): it expands the analyze section and immediately runs analysis for that receipt, then clears the pending id. Android has no equivalent navigation argument handling, so the 'analyze my last shop' deep link does nothing.
  - **Fix:** Accept a pendingAnalyzeReceiptId nav arg / shared state in OkazjeHubScreen; on first composition with a non-null id, expand the analyze disclosure and call vm.runAnalyze(id, lang.code), then consume it.
- 🟡 **[behavior] Rich loading/first-launch/filtered empty states missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:937-1022,969-991` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/deals/OkazjeHubScreen.kt:234-252`
  - iOS distinguishes: rich loading card (deals.loadingTitle/loadingHint + skeleton), first-launch cold-start empty (tag.slash, deals.emptyFirstTitle/Body + CTA), filtered-empty (line.3.decrease, deals.emptyFilteredTitle/Body + reset-filter action), and error card. Android only has generic NBLoadingCard, a single trendingEmpty card, and NBErrorCard — no first-launch CTA, no filtered-empty, no rich loading copy.
  - **Fix:** Add the three iOS empty/loading variants. The richLoadingCard hint copy (deals.loadingTitle/loadingHint) and empty-state copy (deals.emptyFirstTitle/emptyFirstBody/emptyFirstCTA/emptyFilteredTitle/emptyFilteredBody/deals.resetFilter) are all absent from Android L10n and must be added.
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] analyze.cheaperAtFmt argument arity mismatch (savings amount dropped)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:868-874` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/deals/OkazjeHubScreen.kt:854-866`
  - iOS analyze.cheaperAtFmt is '−%@ w %@' / '−%@ at %@' taking TWO args (savings amount + store) and is only shown when item.savings>0, in success color. Android's key is 'Taniej w %@' / 'Cheaper at %@' taking ONE arg (store) in muted color, and the savings amount is rendered separately as '−amount'. Result: the inline 'cheaper at' line never shows how much cheaper, and styling/placement differs.
  - **Fix:** Change Android analyze.cheaperAtFmt to two-arg form matching iOS and only render it when item.savings>0, formatting Fmt.amount(savings) + store, in success color; remove the separate −savings text or align it with iOS's layout.
- 🟡 **[visual] Multi-store strategy styling and eyebrow differ from iOS**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:1576-1696` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/deals/OkazjeHubScreen.kt:536-596`
  - iOS multiStoreSection uses a success-tinted container (success.opacity .06 bg + success .5 border), an arrow.triangle.branch icon, eyebrow shoppingList.multiStoreEyebrow with an inline '+savingsVsSingle' in green, a rationale line, per-store mini-cards (cardTitle store + subtotal + address + items with promo chips), and a footer with shoppingList.multiStoreGrandTotal + green grand total. Android MultiStoreSection uses a plain muted bg (no success tint/border), a 🏪 emoji, shoppingList.multiStore + multiStoreSubtitle, store partitions without promo chips on line items, and a grandTotal/savingsVsSingle footer. Header copy and visual emphasis diverge.
  - **Fix:** Restyle MultiStoreSection to a success-tinted card with success border, use the multiStoreEyebrow + inline +savings header, render rationale, and add promo chips on partition line items. Reconcile which keys to use (iOS uses multiStoreEyebrow/multiStoreGrandTotal; Android currently uses multiStore/grandTotal).
- 🟡 **[visual] Launcher tiles use different icons/order/layout and a wide advisor tile is missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:1802-1929` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/deals/OkazjeHubScreen.kt:890-954`
  - iOS launcher is a LazyVGrid of 4 square tiles (Product search/info, Nearby/warning, Audit/foreground, Prices/success — each a tinted SF-Symbol icon in a rounded square, minHeight 120) PLUS a full-width wide tile for Shopping advisor with chevron. Order: productSearch, nearbyStores, audit, prices, then advisor wide. Android renders a 2x2 grid of emoji tiles (💰 Prices, 📍 Nearby, 🛒 Audit, 🤖 Advisor) with no Product-search tile, no shopping-advisor wide tile, emoji instead of tinted icons, and a different first tile (prices vs productSearch). Section eyebrow/title also differ (iOS deals.toolsEyebrow/toolsTitle vs Android deals.launcherEyebrow/launcherTitle).
  - **Fix:** Rebuild LauncherSection as a 2-column grid of 5 entries matching iOS order and icons (use vector icons + tint, minHeight ~120), include a productSearch tile and a full-width advisor tile with chevron. Use deals.toolsEyebrow/toolsTitle and the nav.* + deals.*Sub copy iOS uses (deals.productsSub/storesSub/auditSub/pricesSub/advisorSub all exist in Android L10n).
- 🟡 **[visual] Trending card uses emoji 🏷 instead of vendor logo**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:1066` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/deals/OkazjeHubScreen.kt:272-279`
  - iOS uses VendorLogo(vendor:) to render the actual chain logo (with tag.fill fallback). Android renders a static 🏷 emoji in a foreground-tinted box for every promo, so all vendors look identical and brand recognition is lost.
  - **Fix:** Implement/port a VendorLogo Composable (async vendor logo with tag fallback) and use it in TrendingCard at 40dp like iOS.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Submit-on-return adds new row missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Deals/OkazjeHubView.swift:1336-1345` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/deals/OkazjeHubScreen.kt:403-409`
  - iOS shopping rows use submitLabel(.next) + onSubmit: pressing Return on the last non-empty row appends a new empty row. Android NBTextField rows have no IME action handling, so typing an item and pressing enter does nothing.
