  - **Fix:** Remove the priceRange/RANGE block entirely so Android matches iOS (iOS shows only cheapest + average).
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] Hardcoded non-localized 'RANGE' string**
  - iOS: `absent` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/productsearch/ProductSearchScreen.kt:182`
  - Even within the (extra) RANGE block, the label is the hardcoded English literal "RANGE" with no L10n lookup — would never localize to Polish. iOS has search.priceRange key but never uses it in this view.
  - **Fix:** If the RANGE block is kept (it should be removed per the previous finding), use locale.t("productSearch.priceRange")/"search.priceRange". Preferred: remove the block.
- 🟡 **[visual] Result price font size/weight differs (18 bold vs 14 monoBold)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ProductSearch/ProductSearchView.swift:322-325` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/productsearch/ProductSearchScreen.kt:209-211`
  - iOS result price is AppFont.bold(18). Android uses SolvioFonts.monoBold(14) — both smaller and a different (mono) typeface.
  - **Fix:** Use a bold(18)-equivalent style (SolvioFonts.bold(18) if available) to match iOS, not monoBold(14).
- 🟡 **[visual] Promo rendering differs: iOS inline badge, Android separate row + promoDetails**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ProductSearch/ProductSearchView.swift:301-309` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/productsearch/ProductSearchScreen.kt:213-224`
  - iOS shows a 'PROMOCJA/PROMO' pill inline next to the store name (mono(9), warning background, radius 4) and never shows promoDetails. Android renders the promo tag on a separate row below the title row AND appends result.promoDetails text — content and placement both differ from iOS.
  - **Fix:** Move the promo NBTag inline with the store name (same row, after store text) and remove the promoDetails text to match iOS, which does not display promoDetails.
- 🟡 **[visual] 'Estimated prices' shown as a HeroCard tag instead of inline info row after results**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ProductSearch/ProductSearchView.swift:273-281` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/productsearch/ProductSearchScreen.kt:155-161`
  - iOS shows isEstimated as a small info.circle + 'Ceny szacunkowe/Estimated prices' caption row appended at the bottom of the results section, muted color. Android shows isEstimated as an NBTag (warning background/foreground) in the top-right of the HeroCard. Placement, styling, and color all differ.
  - **Fix:** Remove the estimated tag from the (to-be-removed) HeroCard and append an inline Row(Icon info, caption muted text = productSearch.estimated) at the end of the results list when r.isEstimated == true.
- 🟡 **[visual] Section titles missing count badge and use different namespace**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ProductSearch/ProductSearchView.swift:266-268,334-336,404-416` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/productsearch/ProductSearchScreen.kt:125,128`
  - iOS sectionTitle is an HStack with the uppercased mono(11) tracked label on the left and a mono(11) count (results.count / alternatives.count) on the right. Android renders only a plain Text label (productSearch.results / productSearch.alternatives) with no trailing count and no Spacer/Row, so the count badge present on iOS for both Results and Alternatives is absent.
  - **Fix:** Create a sectionTitle composable: Row { Text(label, eyebrow style) ; Spacer ; Text(count.toString(), mono(11) muted) } and use it for both the results and alternatives headers.
- 🟡 **[visual] Alternative price missing '~' prefix and wrong color/font**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ProductSearch/ProductSearchView.swift:350-353` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/productsearch/ProductSearchScreen.kt:243-245`
  - iOS renders alternative avgPrice as '~<price>' in Theme.info color with bold(14). Android renders it without the '~' prefix, in foreground color, with mono(12). Prefix, color, and font all differ.
  - **Fix:** Render Text("~" + formattedPrice) with palette.info color and a bold(14)-equivalent style to match iOS.
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] Duplicate/divergent L10n namespaces: productSearch.* vs search.***
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:270-287` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/L10n.kt:705-722,2073-2090`
  - iOS uses the search.* namespace exclusively for this screen (search.title='Wyszukiwarka cen', search.results='Wyniki', search.cheapest='Najtaniej', etc.). Android maintains BOTH a full search.* set (matching iOS) and a separate productSearch.* set, and the screen reads only productSearch.*. The productSearch.* values diverge: e.g. productSearch.results='WYNIKI' (pre-uppercased) and productSearch.eyebrow='WYSZUKIWARKA CEN'. Maintaining two parallel namespaces risks drift and the screen is keyed off the non-iOS one. Also there is no productSearch.recent/clearHistory (history feature) and no productSearch.cheapestStore.
  - **Fix:** Point the screen at the search.* keys that already mirror iOS, and remove/deprecate the redundant productSearch.* set (or make productSearch.* alias search.*). Ensure recent/clearHistory keys exist for the history feature.
- 🟡 **[behavior] ensureDashboard()/currency prefetch on appear not performed**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ProductSearch/ProductSearchView.swift:73` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/productsearch/ProductSearchScreen.kt:66-104`
  - iOS calls store.ensureDashboard() in .task on appear so the sticky currency is available before the user searches. Android performs no on-appear prefetch and simply hardcodes PLN, so even if currency wiring is added it must be loaded on entry.
  - **Fix:** In ProductSearchViewModel init (or a LaunchedEffect on screen entry), prefetch settings/currency once (SettingsRepo.fetch) so the correct currency is ready for the request and formatting.
- ⚪ **[visual] Empty-results card missing magnifier icon and uses different component/radius**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ProductSearch/ProductSearchView.swift:385-400` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/productsearch/ProductSearchScreen.kt:114-122`
  - iOS emptyCard centers a 32pt bold magnifyingglass icon above the title (bodyMedium) and desc (caption) in an nbCard(Radius.md, Shadow.sm), lg padding. Android wraps NBEmptyState (which renders icon only if passed, defaults to none, uses Radius.lg/Shadow.md, cardTitle style) inside an NBCard(Radius.md) — so no magnifier icon shows and the radii/shadow/title style differ.
  - **Fix:** Either pass icon = Icons.Default.Search to NBEmptyState (and align radius/shadow to md/sm) or build a dedicated empty card with a centered search icon matching iOS sizing, instead of the default iconless NBEmptyState.
- ⚪ **[visual] Alternatives card layout differs (name+why stacked vs iOS name-then-why with price right)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ProductSearch/ProductSearchView.swift:337-358` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/productsearch/ProductSearchScreen.kt:231-251`
  - iOS alternative row: HStack with a leading VStack(name bodyMedium, whyBetter caption) and the price on the trailing edge of the same top row. Android puts name + price in the top Row and whyBetter on a second line below the whole row. The whyBetter placement relative to the price differs subtly from iOS (iOS keeps why under the name only, price stays top-right aligned to the name row).
  - **Fix:** Match iOS: Row { Column(weight 1f){ name; whyBetter } ; price }. Move whyBetter inside the left column rather than as a full-width line under the price.
- ⚪ **[visual] Tip card missing lightbulb icon**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ProductSearch/ProductSearchView.swift:364-381` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/productsearch/ProductSearchScreen.kt:253-262`
  - iOS tipCard is an HStack(top) with a 16pt bold lightbulb.fill icon in warning color, then a VStack of a 'WSKAZÓWKA/TIP' mono(10) uppercase muted label and the tip body. Android TipCard shows only the NBEyebrow label + tip text, with no lightbulb icon and no warning-colored accent.
  - **Fix:** Add a leading Icon(Icons.Filled.Lightbulb, tint = palette.warning, 16dp) in a Row(top alignment) before the eyebrow+tip column.
- ⚪ **[missing-feature] Navigation title missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ProductSearch/ProductSearchView.swift:71-72` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/productsearch/ProductSearchScreen.kt:66-87`
  - iOS sets navigationTitle(search.navTitle) with inline display mode ('Szukaj produktów'/'Search Products') shown in the nav bar above the screen header. Android's ProductSearchScreen renders no top app bar / nav title at all.
  - **Fix:** Ensure the screen is hosted with a top app bar showing locale.t("productSearch.navTitle")/"search.navTitle" (inline), consistent with how iOS shows it in the navigation bar.
- ⚪ **[copy-l10n] Screen header eyebrow uses uppercased key vs iOS uppercasing navTitle**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ProductSearch/ProductSearchView.swift:160-165` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/productsearch/ProductSearchScreen.kt:82-86`
  - iOS NBScreenHeader eyebrow = search.navTitle.uppercased() = 'SZUKAJ PRODUKTÓW'/'SEARCH PRODUCTS'. Android passes productSearch.eyebrow = 'WYSZUKIWARKA CEN'/'PRICE SEARCH', which is different text from the iOS eyebrow (it actually duplicates the title). The eyebrow copy does not match.
  - **Fix:** Set the header eyebrow to the uppercased nav title text (locale.t("search.navTitle").uppercase()) so it reads 'SZUKAJ PRODUKTÓW'/'SEARCH PRODUCTS' like iOS, instead of 'PRICE SEARCH'.

## Savings — parity ~22% (21 findings)

_The Android Savings screen is built against an obsolete design that iOS has explicitly retired. iOS SavingsHubView is now a single vertically-scrolling planner: NBScreenHeader, a planner-focused 2x2 KPI strip (Spent / Remaining / Health-or-SavingsTarget / Total-saved), a data-driven "smart hero insight" banner (8 prioritized states), a Goals preview section (sorted, deadline status, progress bars, empty CTA), a Challenges preview section (from store), a Deals teaser card deep-linking to the Deals tab, an Incomes quick-link, and a Planner card (income/budget/savings mini-facts + burn-rate pace indicator + savings-rate line + alerts + top categories) with a full BudgetEditSheet (3 inputs, presets, copy-last-month, live allocation bar, warnings) and a separate full IncomesView (multi-income editor with donut chart, period tags, add/edit sheet, delete confirm). Android instead renders the old 4-tab TabRow switcher (Goals / Budget / Challenges / Deals) — duplicating the Deals feature that already lives in its own bottom-nav tab, omitting the budget edit flow, the planner KPI strip, the smart insight engine, the goals/challenges/deals preview-with-navigation, the incomes link, and the entire IncomesView screen. Multiple wired callbacks are dead (onOpenGoal default no-op), and several iOS L10n keys are missing. Parity is low._

- 🔴 **[missing-feature] Entire Savings hub uses retired 4-tab design instead of single-scroll planner**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:22-82` → Android: `SavingsHubScreen.kt:140-222`
  - iOS SavingsHubView (SavingsHubView.swift:22-82) renders a single ScrollView/VStack: header, topKpiStrip, aiTipBanner, goalsPreviewSection, challengesPreviewSection, okazjeTeaserCard, plannerTab. The comment block (lines 1-13, 40-43) explicitly states the 4-tab roll-up (Planner/Products/Stores/Deals) was removed and the tab picker is 'intentionally hidden'. Android (SavingsHubScreen.kt:140-222) still implements a SavingsTab enum {GOALS,BUDGET,CHALLENGES,DEALS} with a TabSwitcher TabRow and lazily loads per-tab data. This is a fundamentally different screen structure.
  - **Fix:** Rewrite SavingsHubScreen as a single LazyColumn matching iOS: header, planner KPI strip, hero insight banner, goals preview, challenges preview, deals teaser, incomes link, planner card. Delete the SavingsTab enum, TabSwitcher, and the lazy per-tab loaders.
- 🔴 **[missing-feature] Planner-focused 2x2 KPI strip missing (wrong tiles shown)**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:101-127` → Android: `SavingsHubScreen.kt:240-263`
  - iOS topKpiStrip (SavingsHubView.swift:101-127) shows Spent-this-month, Budget-remaining, Health-score-tile (or Savings-target fallback), and Total-saved-across-goals — i.e. planner/budget KPIs from BudgetResponse. Android KpiStrip (SavingsHubScreen.kt:240-263) shows Total-saved, Active-goals-count, Monthly-needed, and Health — a goals-centric strip with no budget data (Spent/Remaining/SavingsTarget) at all. activeCount and monthlyNeeded tiles do not exist on iOS hub.
  - **Fix:** Replace KpiStrip with iOS tiles: savings.spentThisMonth (=budget.totalSpent), savings.budgetRemaining, healthScoreTile or savings.savingsTargetTile fallback, savings.totalSavedTile. Source values from BudgetResponse + goals, not from goals only.
- 🔴 **[missing-feature] Entire IncomesView screen (multi-income editor) absent on Android**
  - iOS: `IncomesView.swift:1-410` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS IncomesView.swift (1-410) is a full screen: NBScreenHeader, total-monthly summary KPI card, donut/bar share Chart (Charts framework), per-source list rows (emoji tile, name, period tag, inactive badge, monthly + amount), add/edit IncomeEditorSheet (name, amount, period NBSegmented monthly/weekly/yearly/oneoff, emoji picker), context-menu delete with confirm alert, refreshable. Android has NO IncomesView/IncomesScreen file, NO Income/IncomeCreate/IncomeUpdate model, NO IncomesRepo (grep confirms none exist), and NO incomes.* L10n keys.
  - **Fix:** Create IncomesScreen.kt + IncomeEditorSheet, add Income/IncomeCreate/IncomeUpdate data classes to Models.kt, add IncomesRepo to Repositories.kt (list/create/update/delete against the incomes API), add all incomes.* L10n keys (PL+EN), and a donut/bar share chart. Wire monthlyAmount normalization (weekly*52/12, yearly/12, oneoff, monthly).
- 🔴 **[missing-popup] BudgetEditSheet (full month-plan editor) completely missing**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:1032-1442` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS BudgetEditSheet (SavingsHubView.swift:1032-1442) is a presentationDetents([.medium,.large]) sheet with 3 inputs (income/budget/savings, each with %-of-income share line), 3 presets (50/30/20, 70/20/10, 80/20) gated on income>0, copy-last-month button, live allocation stacked bar + legend + savings-rate, warning cards (negative/overflow/no-income), cancel/save toolbar with canSave validation, and on save calls BudgetRepo.upsert + toast + store.didMutateBudget. Android has no budget editor at all — there is no edit button, no sheet, and BudgetRepo.upsert (Repositories.kt:245) is never called. The planner is read-only.
  - **Fix:** Build a BudgetEditSheet composable (ModalBottomSheet) with the 3 fields + share lines, 3 preset rows, copy-last-month, allocation bar + legend, warning cards, and Save -> BudgetRepo.upsert(BudgetUpsert(month, totalIncome, totalBudget, savingsTarget)) + toast(savings.budgetSaved). Use all budgetSheet.* keys (already present in L10n).
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Smart data-driven hero insight banner replaced by raw AI-tip string**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:185-376` → Android: `SavingsHubScreen.kt:303-333`
  - iOS heroInsight (SavingsHubView.swift:185-283) computes one of 8 prioritized insights locally (overspending category >=90%, near-goal >=80%, overdue goal, on-track encouragement with monthly amount, plus 4 no-data setup fallbacks), each with tone-based bg/stroke/title color (warn/good/info), emoji, title, body — and ALWAYS shows something. Android AiTipBanner (SavingsHubScreen.kt:303-333) just renders health.tips.firstOrNull() as a collapsible ✨ row; if no tips, nothing shows. None of the computed insight states, tone coloring, or no-data CTAs exist.
  - **Fix:** Port heroInsight logic (priority order + 8 states) and aiTipBanner styling (tone -> bg .opacity(0.10)/.muted, stroke, titleColor; emoji chip 36x36, title bodyMedium, body) into Compose. Drive from budget.categoryBreakdown + goals, not health.tips.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Goals preview section (sorted, deadline status, progress) missing**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:531-693` → Android: `SavingsHubScreen.kt:374-429`
  - iOS goalsPreviewSection (SavingsHubView.swift:531-679) is a titled section ('savings.goalsTitle' + 'savings.viewAllGoals' chevron pushing .more(.goals)) showing top-3 active goals sorted by closest deadline then progress, each row with emoji tile, name, pct, NBProgressBar, current/target amounts, and deadline status (overdue/today/tomorrow/dayMonth via hubDeadlineStatus). Empty state is a tappable CTA card. Android GoalsTabBody (SavingsHubScreen.kt:374-429) only renders inside the GOALS tab: take(5) unsorted goals, GoalRow lacks deadline status entirely and shows no section header / view-all link / empty CTA card.
  - **Fix:** Add a GoalsPreviewSection composable: title row + 'Wszystkie' link navigating to goals, top-3 sorted (deadline asc then pct desc), GoalRow with deadline-status pill (goals.overdue/today/tomorrow or Fmt.dayMonth), and an empty-state CTA card.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Challenges preview section missing (only a CHALLENGES tab exists)**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:390-489` → Android: `SavingsHubScreen.kt:501-541`
  - iOS challengesPreviewSection (SavingsHubView.swift:390-489) is an always-visible section: title 'savings.challengesTitle' + 'savings.viewAllChallenges' link to .more(.challenges); top-3 active challenges as rows (emoji 40x40, name, pct, chevron) sourced from store.challenges; an empty CTA card ('savings.challengesEmptyTitle/Sub') when none. Android renders challenges only inside a tab (ChallengesTabBody, SavingsHubScreen.kt:501-541) with no section title, no view-all navigation, no pct (shows c.type.uppercase() instead of progress %), and a plain NBEmptyState instead of the tappable CTA card.
  - **Fix:** Add ChallengesPreviewSection: title + view-all link to challenges route, top-3 active rows with emoji tile + name + pct(target/current) + chevron, and a tappable empty CTA. Show progress % (currentProgress/targetAmount) not challenge type.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Deals teaser card missing; Android duplicates the whole Deals feature**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:496-529` → Android: `SavingsHubScreen.kt:543-594`
  - iOS okazjeTeaserCard (SavingsHubView.swift:496-529) is a single tap-card (tag.fill icon, 'savings.dealsTeaserTitle'/'savings.dealsTeaserSub', chevron) that deep-links to router.selectedTab = .deals — it does NOT render deals here because they live in the dedicated Deals bottom-nav tab. Android DealsTabBody (SavingsHubScreen.kt:543-594) actually re-fetches PromotionsRepo and renders deal rows inside Savings, duplicating OkazjeHubScreen which already exists as the Deals tab (MainTabScreen.kt:147).
  - **Fix:** Delete DealsTabBody/DealRow/loadPromotions and the DEALS tab. Add a single OkazjeTeaserCard that sets router.selectedTab = Deals. Use savings.dealsTeaserTitle/Sub keys (must be added to L10n).
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Incomes quick-link card missing from planner**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:722-752` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS plannerTab (SavingsHubView.swift:722-752) includes a 'banknote' quick-link card ('incomes.title' + 'incomes.linkSub') that pushes .more(.incomes) to open the multi-income editor. Android has no equivalent card and no navigation to an incomes screen anywhere (grep: no IncomesScreen/IncomesView, no 'incomes' route).
  - **Fix:** Add a banknote-styled link card (success-tinted 36x36 icon, incomes.title + incomes.linkSub) navigating to a new IncomesScreen route.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Planner summary card lacks income/budget/savings mini-facts, pace indicator, savings-rate**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:781-844` → Android: `SavingsHubScreen.kt:450-466`
  - iOS budgetSummaryCard (SavingsHubView.swift:781-844) shows a 3-up miniFact grid (Income/Budget/SavingsTarget), a Spent line with totalSpent/totalBudget, NBProgressBar(over: pct>1), a pctUsed + burn-rate pace indicator (colored dot comparing pct vs b.monthProgress, 'X% of month'), and a savings-rate line (budgetSheet.savingsRateFmt, green if >=20%). Android BudgetTabBody (SavingsHubScreen.kt:432-499) only shows 2 StatTiles (Spent/Remaining), a progress bar, and pctUsed text. No income/savings facts, no monthProgress pace dot, no savings-rate line.
  - **Fix:** Rebuild the planner card: 3-up mini-facts (income/budget/savingsTarget), spent/total line, ProgressBar(over), pctUsed + pace dot (onPaceColor from pct - monthProgress thresholds) + '{monthProgress*100}% miesiąca', and savings-rate line when income>0 && savingsTarget>0.
- 🟠 **[bug] Goal taps are dead — onOpenGoal default no-op, never wired**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:606-614` → Android: `SavingsHubScreen.kt:143, MainTabScreen.kt:149`
  - Android SavingsHubScreen(onOpenGoal: (String) -> Unit = {}) (SavingsHubScreen.kt:143) defaults to a no-op, and MainTabScreen.kt:149 calls SavingsHubScreen() without passing a handler. So tapping a goal row (GoalRow onClick -> onOpenGoal(g.id)) does nothing. iOS pushes router.push(.goalDetail(id:)) (SavingsHubView.swift:609).
  - **Fix:** Pass a real navigation lambda from MainTabScreen that routes to the goal detail screen, matching iOS .goalDetail(id:).
- 🟠 **[copy-l10n] Missing L10n keys for insights, previews, teaser, incomes**
  - iOS: `L10n.swift:818-908` → Android: `L10n.kt:506-540,1236-1286 (incomplete)`
  - iOS L10n.swift defines keys Android L10n.kt lacks: savings.insightOverBudget/Body, insightNearGoal/Body, insightOverdue/Body, insightOnTrack/Body, insightSetupTitle/Body, insightAddBudgetTitle/Body, insightAddGoalTitle/Body (818-832); savings.goalsTitle, viewAllGoals, goalsEmptyTitle, goalsEmptySub, challengesTitle, viewAllChallenges, challengesEmptyTitle, challengesEmptySub, dealsTeaserTitle, dealsTeaserSub, totalSavedTile, monthShort (834-860); and the entire incomes.* set (883-908). Android instead has stale keys for the old tabs (segProducts/segStores/segLoyalty/productsTitle/storesTitle etc.).
  - **Fix:** Add all missing savings.insight*, savings.goalsTitle/viewAll*/Empty*, savings.dealsTeaser*, savings.totalSavedTile, savings.monthShort, and incomes.* keys to both PL and EN maps in L10n.kt. Remove dead old-tab keys after the rewrite.
- 🟡 **[visual] Budget alerts rendered without icon, total-label, or amounts**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:858-880` → Android: `SavingsHubScreen.kt:467-478`
  - iOS alertsSection (SavingsHubView.swift:858-880) renders each alert with a severity icon (octagon.fill for critical else triangle.fill, destructive/warning tint), a title that maps category=='__total__' to 'savings.totalBudget', a spent/budgeted amount line + pct, each in its own nbCard. Android (SavingsHubScreen.kt:467-478) renders alerts as bare 'category: pct%' text lines inside one card — no icon, no __total__ mapping, no spent/budgeted amounts, no per-alert card.
  - **Fix:** Render each alert as a row: severity icon (a.type=='critical'?octagon:triangle, tinted), title (category=='__total__'?savings.totalBudget:category), '{spent}/{budgeted} — {pct}%' caption, wrapped in nbCard(Radius.sm).
- 🟡 **[visual] Top categories rows lack progress bar and budgeted/spent ratio**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:882-908` → Android: `SavingsHubScreen.kt:479-494`
  - iOS topCategoriesSection (SavingsHubView.swift:882-908) filters rows to budgeted>0, takes 5, and renders each with name + '{spent}/{budgeted}' (red if pct>1) + an NBProgressBar(over: pct>1). Android (SavingsHubScreen.kt:479-494) takes 5 unfiltered categoryBreakdown rows showing only 'icon name' + Fmt.amount(spent) — no budgeted comparison, no progress bar, no >0 filter, no over-budget red.
  - **Fix:** Filter categoryBreakdown to budgeted>0, take 5, render name + '{spent}/{budgeted}' (destructive if pct>1) + ProgressBar(value=spent/budgeted, over=pct>1).
- 🟡 **[behavior] Planner empty/error/loading states differ from iOS**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:754-777` → Android: `SavingsHubScreen.kt:437-449`
  - iOS planner (SavingsHubView.swift:754-777) uses NBSkeletonHero + NBSkeletonList(rows:3) while loading, NBErrorCard with retry, and a rich NBEmptyState (dollarsign.circle.fill icon, savings.emptyPlanner/Sub, action 'savings.setBudget' opening the edit sheet) when no budget. Android budget tab (SavingsHubScreen.kt:437-449) uses NBLoadingCard (not skeletons) and an NBEmptyState with savings.emptyBudget/Sub and NO action button (can't set a budget since the editor is missing).
  - **Fix:** Use skeleton loaders, and an empty state with a 'Ustaw budżet' (savings.setBudget) action that opens the BudgetEditSheet. Prefer savings.emptyPlanner/emptyPlannerSub keys per iOS.
- 🟡 **[bug] Currency derived from goals only — wrong for budget/planner values**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:947-950` → Android: `SavingsHubScreen.kt:183, 456-457`
  - Android derives currency = goals.firstOrNull()?.currency ?: "PLN" (SavingsHubScreen.kt:183) and uses it for KPI tiles. iOS uses store.currency (settings-driven) for all planner/KPI formatting (SavingsHubViewModel.currency, SavingsHubView.swift:948-950). If the user has no goals, or goal currency differs from the settings currency, Android formats budget amounts in the wrong currency. Budget StatTiles on Android (lines 456-457) call Fmt.amount(...) with no currency at all, defaulting to PLN regardless of settings.
  - **Fix:** Source currency from app settings/store (as iOS does), and pass it to every Fmt.amount call including the budget StatTiles.
- 🟡 **[behavior] Stale per-tab data loaders fire expensive endpoints iOS deliberately removed**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:995-1023` → Android: `SavingsHubScreen.kt:115-137,152-159`
  - iOS loadAll (SavingsHubView.swift:995-1005) deliberately fires ONLY budget + financialHealth, and the comment notes the old design's per-tab LLM price-comparison/audit calls were removed because the views weren't rendered. Android still has loadChallenges/loadPromotions (SavingsHubScreen.kt:115-137) triggered by tab selection, re-fetching ChallengesRepo and PromotionsRepo inside Savings even though iOS surfaces challenges from the shared store and deals only via the dedicated tab.
  - **Fix:** Remove the DEALS/promotions loader from Savings. Source challenges from a shared store (like iOS store.challenges) for the preview instead of a Savings-local fetch. Keep budget + financialHealth loaders.
- ⚪ **[behavior] No pull-to-refresh on Savings hub**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:51` → Android: `SavingsHubScreen.kt:161`
  - iOS SavingsHubView has .refreshable { await vm.loadAll() } (SavingsHubView.swift:51) and IncomesView has .refreshable (IncomesView.swift:62). Android SavingsHubScreen LazyColumn (SavingsHubScreen.kt:161-221) has no pull-to-refresh; it only loads once via LaunchedEffect(Unit).
  - **Fix:** Wrap the LazyColumn in a PullToRefreshBox that re-runs vm.loadHeader()/budget loaders.
- ⚪ **[visual] Health gauge tile font size and label key differ**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:151-153` → Android: `SavingsHubScreen.kt:298`
  - iOS healthScoreTile (SavingsHubView.swift:130-160) uses bold(18) for the '{score}/100' value and label key 'savings.health'. Android HealthGaugeTile (SavingsHubScreen.kt:296-299) uses bold(16) and a separate key 'savings.kpiHealth'. Also iOS gauge arc trims to min(100,score)/100 of a full circle drawn over a muted full circle; Android matches the arc but value font/label key differ. Minor but a pixel diff.
  - **Fix:** Use bold(18) for the score value to match iOS; consolidate to a single 'savings.health' key.
- ⚪ **[behavior] monthlyNeeded month math differs from iOS (per-goal vs days/30)**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:301-319` → Android: `SavingsHubScreen.kt:224-238`
  - iOS monthlyNeededForActive (SavingsHubView.swift:301-319) skips overdue goals, computes months = max(1, ceil(days/30)) then remaining/months, summed. Android computeMonthlyNeeded (SavingsHubScreen.kt:224-238) does NOT skip overdue goals (days coerced to >=1) and computes (remaining/days)*30, producing very large 'monthly' figures for near/overdue deadlines. Note this tile shouldn't exist at all per the KPI-strip finding, but if retained the math must match.
  - **Fix:** If a monthly-needed value is kept anywhere, skip overdue goals and use months=max(1,ceil(days/30)); remaining/months. Otherwise remove (not in iOS KPI strip).
- ⚪ **[visual] AI tip banner uses ✨ icon + collapse interaction not present on iOS**
  - iOS: `SavingsHubView.swift:321-376` → Android: `SavingsHubScreen.kt:303-333`
  - iOS hero banner is always-expanded, tone-colored, emoji+title+body, non-collapsible (SavingsHubView.swift:321-376). Android AiTipBanner (SavingsHubScreen.kt:303-333) is a collapsible row with a ✨ chip, 'AI TIP' label, and a ▲/▼ toggle that hides the tip by default — extra interaction and a different visual that iOS does not have.
  - **Fix:** Replace with the always-visible tone-colored hero insight banner (emoji chip + title + body), removing the collapse toggle and AI TIP label.

## ShoppingAdvisor — parity ~58% (21 findings)

_The Android ShoppingAdvisorScreen is a fairly faithful structural port of the iOS ShoppingAdvisorView (same card stack: header, generate CTA, summary, hero stats, recommendations, weekly plan, insights), and the data models/repo signatures line up. However there are serious gaps. The single biggest one is that the screen is not wired into navigation at all: there is no AppDestination.Advisor, no composable("advisor"){...} in MainTabScreen's NavHost, and OkazjeHubScreen is invoked at MainTabScreen:147 without an onNavigate handler, so the "advisor" tile (and prices/nearby/audit tiles) are dead and ShoppingAdvisorScreen is unreachable. Beyond that: the data-source honesty badge (LIVE/ESTIMATE) is completely missing on Android (and the dataSource field is missing from the Android model), the rich staged NBProgressCard loading experience is downgraded to a generic NBLoadingCard, the nav title is never shown, pull-to-refresh is gone, currency is hardcoded to "PLN" instead of loaded from settings, iOS' no_data error mapping is missing, and several visual details diverge (SF Symbol icons replaced by emoji/arrows, section eyebrows missing the "// " prefix and uppercasing, eyebrow colors wrong, FlowLayout product chip behavior). Android also ADDS an alternativeStores expandable section that iOS does not render, which is a divergence in the other direction._

- 🔴 **[missing-feature] ShoppingAdvisorScreen is not wired into navigation — unreachable**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:54` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/root/AppRouter.kt:20-44; android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/root/MainTabScreen.kt:97-135`
  - On iOS the advisor is a navigable screen (navigationTitle advisor.navTitle). On Android there is no AppDestination.Advisor and no composable("advisor") { ShoppingAdvisorScreen() } in any NavHost. The Deals hub tile calls onNavigate("advisor") (OkazjeHubScreen.kt:928) but MainTabScreen:147 invokes OkazjeHubScreen() with NO onNavigate argument (defaults to {} no-op). So ShoppingAdvisorScreen() is referenced nowhere except its own definition and can never be opened.
  - **Fix:** Add `object Advisor : AppDestination("advisor")` to AppRouter, register `composable(AppDestination.Advisor.route) { ShoppingAdvisorScreen() }` in the dashboard-tab NavHost in MainTabScreen, and pass `onNavigate = { route -> dashboardNav.navigate(route) }` (with tab-switch + pendingRoute handling like MoreSheet) to OkazjeHubScreen() at MainTabScreen:147 so the advisor/prices/nearby/audit tiles actually navigate.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Data-source honesty badge (LIVE/ESTIMATE) entirely missing on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:33,111-125` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS renders a dataSourceRow above the summary: an NBTag colored success (advisor data is 'live_web_search' -> shoppingList.badgeLive 'ŻYWE/LIVE') or warning ('estimate' -> shoppingList.badgeEstimate 'ESTYMATA/ESTIMATE'), with .opacity(0.15) background. This is an explicit no-fabricated-data signal. Android never renders any data-source badge in the Loaded branch.
  - **Fix:** Add a DataSourceRow composable that switches on r.dataSource and renders NBTag(text=locale.t("shoppingList.badgeLive"), background=palette.success.copy(alpha=0.15f), foreground=palette.success) for "live_web_search" and the warning variant for "estimate", placed as the first item in the Loaded branch (before SummaryBlock), matching iOS line 33.
- 🟠 **[bug] ShoppingAdvisorResponse.dataSource field missing from Android model**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Models/Models.swift:852-854` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/models/Models.kt:835-846`
  - iOS ShoppingAdvisorResponse has `let dataSource: String?` ('live_web_search' | 'estimate'). The Android data class omits dataSource entirely, so even if the badge UI were added there is no decoded field to drive it. (kotlinx ignoreUnknownKeys will silently drop the server's data_source/dataSource value.)
  - **Fix:** Add `val dataSource: String? = null,` to ShoppingAdvisorResponse in Models.kt to mirror iOS line 854.
- 🟠 **[behavior] Loading state downgraded from staged NBProgressCard to generic NBLoadingCard**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:16-28` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/shoppingadvisor/ShoppingAdvisorScreen.kt:111`
  - iOS shows NBProgressCard(title=advisor.runningTitle, stages=[progress.preparingRequest, progress.aggregating, progress.contactingAI, progress.formattingResults, progress.almostDone], estimatedSeconds=15) — a titled card with rotating stage label, animated progress bar capped at 95%, and elapsed/ETA seconds. Android shows NBLoadingCard() — a plain spinner + generic common.loading text. Android already HAS NBProgressCard (NBComponents.kt:705) so this is just not used here.
  - **Fix:** Replace `item { NBLoadingCard() }` at line 111 with `item { NBProgressCard(title = locale.t("advisor.runningTitle"), stages = listOf(locale.t("progress.preparingRequest"), locale.t("progress.aggregating"), locale.t("progress.contactingAI"), locale.t("progress.formattingResults"), locale.t("progress.almostDone")), estimatedSeconds = 15) }` to match iOS.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Navigation title (advisor.navTitle) never displayed on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:54-55` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS sets .navigationTitle(advisor.navTitle 'Doradca zakupów'/'Shopping advisor') with inline display mode, giving the screen a top bar title and a back affordance. Android's ShoppingAdvisorScreen renders only a LazyColumn with no Scaffold/TopAppBar, so the localized nav title (which IS defined in L10n.kt:656/2024) is never shown and there is no back button.
  - **Fix:** Wrap ShoppingAdvisorScreen content in a Scaffold with a neobrutalist top bar showing locale.t("advisor.navTitle") plus a back button (pop), matching the pattern used by other detail screens, or host it inside a NavHost destination that supplies the title bar.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Pull-to-refresh missing on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:57` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS attaches .refreshable { await run() } so pulling down re-runs the advisor. Android's LazyColumn has no pull-to-refresh, so re-analysis is only possible via the Generate button.
  - **Fix:** Wrap the LazyColumn in a PullToRefreshBox (material3 pull-refresh) whose onRefresh calls vm.run(lang, currency), mirroring iOS .refreshable.
- 🟡 **[behavior] Currency hardcoded to PLN instead of loaded from user settings**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:9,56,60-65` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/shoppingadvisor/ShoppingAdvisorScreen.kt:81`
  - iOS initializes currency='PLN' but on .task loads SettingsRepo.fetch() and overrides currency with bundle.settings.currency, then passes it both to the analyze request and all Fmt.amount formatting. Android does `val currency = "PLN"` (hardcoded) and never reads settings, so a user whose currency is EUR/USD will see wrong currency in the request and in every formatted amount (savings, prices, totals).
  - **Fix:** Load the user's currency from SettingsRepo (e.g. in a LaunchedEffect or the ViewModel) defaulting to 'PLN' when empty, store it in state, and use it for vm.run and all Fmt.amount calls, mirroring loadCurrency() in iOS lines 60-65.
- 🟡 **[behavior] no_data error not mapped to friendly advisor.noDataSub message**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:399-406` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/shoppingadvisor/ShoppingAdvisorScreen.kt:62-64`
  - iOS: when result.error == 'no_data' it shows the localized advisor.noDataSub copy; any other error string is shown raw. Android just does `_state.value = UiState.Error(r.error)` for any non-blank error, so a server-returned 'no_data' token is shown verbatim to the user instead of the friendly localized explanation. (advisor.noDataSub IS defined in Android L10n.)
  - **Fix:** In ShoppingAdvisorViewModel.run, when r.error == "no_data" set the Error message to locale.t("advisor.noDataSub"); otherwise use r.error. Pass an AppLocale into the ViewModel or map the token in the composable before rendering NBErrorCard.
- 🟡 **[visual] Hero-stat icons: SF Symbols on iOS vs emoji/arrow glyph on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:145-156,160-163` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/shoppingadvisor/ShoppingAdvisorScreen.kt:157-170,184`
  - iOS savings badge uses SF Symbol 'arrow.down.circle.fill' (size 20, success) and best-store badge uses 'building.2.fill' (size 20, accent color). Android renders the savings icon as the text glyph '↓' (bold(20), success) and the store icon as the emoji '🏪' (bold(20), tint=foreground). Result: different iconography, different shape weight, and the store-badge tint is foreground on Android vs accent on iOS.
  - **Fix:** Use Material icons matching iOS semantics — e.g. Icons.Filled.ArrowCircleDown (tint success) and Icons.Filled.Apartment/Storefront (tint accent) at 20.dp — and set the store badge tint to palette.accent, not palette.foreground.
- 🟡 **[visual] Section eyebrows (Recommendations / Insights) bypass NBEyebrow — missing '// ' prefix, uppercasing, letter-spacing, wrong color**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:183,363` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/shoppingadvisor/ShoppingAdvisorScreen.kt:122,131`
  - iOS uses NBEyebrow(text:) for ALL four section headers (summary, recommendations, weekly plan, insights). NBEyebrow renders '// ' + UPPERCASED text in foreground with 2pt tracking. Android uses NBEyebrow only for Summary and Weekly Plan; for Recommendations (line 122) and Insights (line 131) it uses raw Text(locale.t(...), style=SolvioFonts.eyebrow.copy(color=mutedForeground)). So those two titles lack the '// ' prefix, are not uppercased, lack letter-spacing, and are mutedForeground instead of foreground.
  - **Fix:** Replace the raw Text at lines 122 and 131 with NBEyebrow(text = locale.t("advisor.recommendationsTitle")) and NBEyebrow(text = locale.t("advisor.insightsTitle")) respectively (no color override) to match iOS.
- 🟡 **[behavior] alternativeStores expandable section: Android renders it, iOS does not**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:190-268` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/shoppingadvisor/ShoppingAdvisorScreen.kt:250-284`
  - Android's RecommendationCard adds a tappable 'advisor.alternatives' row with a ▼/▲ chevron expanding a muted panel listing alternativeStores (store, price, deal). The iOS recommendationCard never references rec.alternativeStores and has no such expander or 'advisor.alternatives' L10n key (iOS L10n has no advisor.alternatives; Android added it). Since iOS is source of truth, this extra section is a divergence — Android shows data/UI iOS hides.
  - **Fix:** To match iOS source-of-truth, remove the alternativeStores expander (lines 250-284) and the altsExpanded state, OR escalate to add the same expander to iOS. Per audit rules (iOS is truth), remove it on Android.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Failure toast (advisor.failed) not surfaced on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:407-410` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/shoppingadvisor/ShoppingAdvisorScreen.kt:67-69`
  - iOS, on a thrown error (network/parse), sets the inline error AND fires toast.error(advisor.failed). Android only sets UiState.Error(e.message) with no toast, so the user gets the inline card but not the toast feedback iOS provides.
  - **Fix:** On the catch branch emit a toast via the app's ToastCenter equivalent using locale.t("advisor.failed") in addition to the Error state.
- ⚪ **[visual] Weekly Plan eyebrow color is mutedForeground vs iOS foreground**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:296` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/shoppingadvisor/ShoppingAdvisorScreen.kt:315`
  - iOS NBEyebrow(text: advisor.weeklyPlanTitle) uses the default color (foreground). Android passes color = palette.mutedForeground, so the Weekly Plan header is a paler grey than on iOS. (Summary eyebrow on Android correctly uses the default, so this is an inconsistency.)
  - **Fix:** Remove the `color = palette.mutedForeground` argument from the NBEyebrow call at line 315 so it defaults to foreground, matching iOS.
- ⚪ **[visual] 'best deal' tag icon: SF Symbol tag.fill (warning) vs '🏷' emoji**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:244-257` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/shoppingadvisor/ShoppingAdvisorScreen.kt:234-246`
  - iOS shows the best-deal chip with SF Symbol 'tag.fill' tinted Theme.warning at size 10. Android uses the emoji '🏷' (SolvioFonts.body size, multicolor, not tinted warning). Visual + tint mismatch.
  - **Fix:** Use Icons.Filled.Sell or LocalOffer at ~10-12.dp tinted palette.warning instead of the '🏷' emoji glyph.
- ⚪ **[visual] Store-plan card icon: SF Symbol storefront.fill (accent) vs '🏪' emoji**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:322-325` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/shoppingadvisor/ShoppingAdvisorScreen.kt:345-346`
  - iOS storePlanCard header uses SF Symbol 'storefront.fill' tinted Theme.accent. Android uses the '🏪' emoji (no accent tint, fixed multicolor) plus a manual 6dp spacer. Icon style and color differ.
  - **Fix:** Replace the '🏪' Text with Icons.Filled.Storefront tinted palette.accent (matching iOS), keeping the spacing.
- ⚪ **[visual] Weekly-savings row icon: SF Symbol arrow.down.circle.fill vs '↓' glyph**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:300-301` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/shoppingadvisor/ShoppingAdvisorScreen.kt:325`
  - iOS weekly-savings row uses SF Symbol 'arrow.down.circle.fill' (success). Android uses the bold text glyph '↓' (success). Same color, different icon shape/weight.
  - **Fix:** Use Icons.Filled.ArrowCircleDown tinted palette.success instead of the '↓' Text glyph.
- ⚪ **[visual] Recommendation 'your price -> best price' arrow: icon vs glyph + size mismatch**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:225-228` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/shoppingadvisor/ShoppingAdvisorScreen.kt:225`
  - iOS uses Image(systemName: 'arrow.right') at default body size, centered between two equal-width columns with Spacers on both sides. Android uses a Text '→' at bold(16) and places the two price columns with weight(1f) each and the arrow between (no Spacers). Slightly different arrow weight and the iOS layout has explicit Spacer padding around the arrow vs Android relies on Arrangement.spacedBy(md). Minor alignment/glyph difference.
  - **Fix:** Use Icons.AutoMirrored.Filled.ArrowForward tinted mutedForeground at ~16-18.dp instead of the '→' glyph for a consistent icon, and verify the centering matches iOS.
- ⚪ **[visual] Insight icon size mismatch (22pt iOS vs bold(20) Android)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:372-373` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/shoppingadvisor/ShoppingAdvisorScreen.kt:392`
  - iOS insightCard renders insight.icon (emoji, default '💡') at .system(size: 22). Android renders it at SolvioFonts.bold(20). The hero-stat emoji is the same. Insight emoji is rendered 2pt smaller on Android.
  - **Fix:** Render the insight icon at font size 22 (e.g. SolvioFonts of 22 or a 22.sp TextStyle) to match iOS line 373.
- ⚪ **[visual] Hero-stat value lacks minimumScaleFactor — long store names may truncate instead of shrinking**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:168-172` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/shoppingadvisor/ShoppingAdvisorScreen.kt:186`
  - iOS statBadge value uses .lineLimit(1).minimumScaleFactor(0.7), so a long bestOverallStore or savings string shrinks to fit. Android uses maxLines=1 with no autosize/overflow ellipsis specified, so a long value will hard-clip (default Clip overflow) rather than scale down, and may also be cut without an ellipsis.
  - **Fix:** Add overflow = TextOverflow.Ellipsis and ideally autosize (BasicText autoSize or a fontSizeRange ~0.7x) to the StatBadge value Text to mirror minimumScaleFactor(0.7).
- ⚪ **[visual] Product chips: SwiftUI custom FlowLayout vs Compose FlowRow — spacing semantics differ**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:343-352,416-453` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/shoppingadvisor/ShoppingAdvisorScreen.kt:356-379`
  - iOS lays out store-plan product chips with a custom FlowLayout (spacing 4 horizontal and 4 added to row height between wrapped rows). Android uses ExperimentalLayoutApi FlowRow with horizontalArrangement and verticalArrangement both spacedBy(4.dp). Behavior is close but FlowRow's max-lines/overflow defaults and last-row alignment can differ; also FlowRow is experimental. Functionally near-equivalent, low risk, flag for visual verification.
  - **Fix:** Verify chip wrapping/spacing visually matches iOS; acceptable as-is, but confirm no extra trailing gap and identical inter-row spacing.
- ⚪ **[visual] Category pill uses background-then-clip ordering and padding parity check**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/ShoppingAdvisor/ShoppingAdvisorView.swift:201-209` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/shoppingadvisor/ShoppingAdvisorScreen.kt:207-216`
  - Both render the category as a mono(10) muted-background pill with Radius.sm, horizontal 6 / vertical 2 padding. Values match. Minor: iOS applies background then clipShape; Android clips then backgrounds then pads — visually equivalent here. No change strictly required; included only because it is part of the recommendation card region.
  - **Fix:** No change needed — matches iOS; listed for completeness.

## Goals — parity ~58% (20 findings)

_Android Goals covers the same broad structure as iOS (list with KPI strip, AI tip banner, active section, completed disclosure, FAB, create sheet, deposit sheet, detail hero + KPI row + AI tips + deposits history). However it diverges substantially in behavior and data: the list has NO delete path at all (iOS has swipe-to-delete, swipe-to-add-funds, context menu, and a delete confirmation alert — all absent), the AI tip banner reads from a different data source (FinancialHealthRepo instead of each goal's aiTips) and shows only one tip with no summary line, the deposit sheet is missing the smart quick-amount buttons, the goal card omits the Today/Tomorrow/Overdue deadline urgency states, and the create sheet hard-codes Polish category labels, replaces the SwiftUI date picker with a raw yyyy-MM-dd text field, and drops the "retirement" category. There are also several visual mismatches (hero amount font/size, hero "of X · %" copy, double-card empty state, missing empty-state CTA, full-date vs day-month deadline format) and missing L10n keys. No crash risks, but parity is materially incomplete on the most-used interactions (delete + quick deposit)._

- 🔴 **[missing-feature] List delete path entirely missing (swipe + context menu + confirm alert)**
  - iOS: `GoalsListView.swift:159-185, 259-273` → Android: `GoalsListScreen.kt:170-172 (GoalCard has only onClick)`
  - iOS lets the user delete a goal from the list via trailing swipe action, via a long-press context menu, both routed through a delete confirmation .alert presenting the goal name. The ViewModel even has a delete(id) function, but it is never wired to any UI. On Android there is no way to delete a goal from the list at all — no swipe, no long-press, no menu, no confirmation dialog.
  - **Fix:** Add SwipeToDismiss (or a long-press dropdown / overflow) on GoalCard with a destructive 'delete' action and a 'add funds' leading action. Route delete through an AlertDialog confirmation showing g.name, then call vm.delete(g.id). Wire up the existing GoalsListViewModel.delete().
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Swipe-to-add-funds (quick deposit) from list missing**
  - iOS: `GoalsListView.swift:167-185, 274-289 (depositTarget sheet)` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS exposes a leading swipe action and context-menu item 'Add funds' that opens DepositSheet for that goal directly from the list (depositTarget state + .sheet(item:)). Android list has no quick-deposit affordance; the user must open the detail screen first.
  - **Fix:** Add a depositTarget state to GoalsListScreen and a swipe/context action that opens DepositSheet(goal=...) with onSubmit calling GoalsRepo.deposit + vm.load(), mirroring iOS.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Deposit quick-amount buttons missing in DepositSheet**
  - iOS: `GoalsListView.swift:733-815 (quickAmounts grid, roundQuick, fallback [50,100,200,500])` → Android: `DepositSheet.kt:83-84 (only amount + note fields)`
  - iOS DepositSheet renders a 4-column grid of smart quick-amount chips computed as 5/10/25/50% of remaining, rounded to clean denominations and deduped, with a fixed fallback of [50,100,200,500] when remaining is unknown. Selecting a chip fills the amount field and highlights (inverted colors). Android DepositSheet has no quick-amount section at all.
  - **Fix:** Add the quickAmounts logic (port roundQuick + 5/10/25/50% of remaining + fallback) and a 4-column FlowRow/Grid of selectable chips above/around the note field, with selected-state inverted background like iOS. Add 'goals.quickAmounts' label.
- 🟠 **[behavior] AI tip banner uses wrong data source and shows only one tip**
  - iOS: `GoalsListView.swift:311-401 (roundRobinTips from activeGoals.aiTips, summary line, up to 3 bullets)` → Android: `GoalsListScreen.kt:62-77, 155-163, 274-306`
  - iOS builds the tip list by round-robin across each active goal's own aiTips array, and when expanded shows a summary line ('active · perMonth') plus up to 3 bulleted tips drawn across goals. Android instead fetches a completely separate FinancialHealthRepo.tips list and, when expanded, renders only s.tips.first() with no summary line and no bullet list. The banner is also only shown when health tips exist, not when goals have aiTips.
  - **Fix:** Drop the FinancialHealthRepo dependency for this banner; compute roundRobinTips from active goals' aiTips (port the iOS 0..2 x perGoal loop, cap 3). When expanded render the '<active> · <perMonth>' summary line then the bulleted tips, matching iOS layout.
- 🟠 **[behavior] Goal card deadline urgency states (Today/Tomorrow/Overdue + color) missing**
  - iOS: `GoalsListView.swift:456-536 (DeadlineStatus, deadlineLabel: red overdue, amber today, dayMonth normal)` → Android: `GoalsListScreen.kt:346-348 (always Fmt.date(d))`
  - iOS computes deadline status and renders 'Overdue' (destructive/red), 'Today' (warning/amber), 'Tomorrow' (muted), or a short day-month date for normal deadlines. Android always renders the full date via Fmt.date (e.g. '12 lip 2026') with no urgency states or coloring.
  - **Fix:** Port deadlineStatus()/deadlineLabel(): parse yyyy-MM-dd in UTC, branch to t('goals.overdue') in destructive, t('goals.today') in warning, t('goals.tomorrow') muted, else a day-month formatted date. Requires adding a Fmt.dayMonth helper and the missing L10n keys.
- 🟠 **[copy-l10n] Create sheet category labels hardcoded in Polish (not localized)**
  - iOS: `GoalsListView.swift:598-610 (locale.t('goals.catVacation') etc.)` → Android: `GoalCreateSheet.kt:62-71 (Triple(..., "Wakacje"), "Samochód", ...)`
  - iOS localizes every category label via goals.catVacation/catCar/catElectronics/catHome/catEmergency/catEducation/catWedding/catRetirement/catOther. Android hardcodes Polish strings ('Wakacje','Samochód','Elektronika','Dom','Awaria','Nauka','Ślub','Inne') so EN users see Polish and the strings bypass L10n.
  - **Fix:** Replace hardcoded labels with locale.t('goals.catVacation') ... using the existing keys (all are present in Android L10n: catCar, catEducation, catElectronics, catEmergency, catHome, catOther, catRetirement, catVacation, catWedding).
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Pull-to-refresh missing on list and detail**
  - iOS: `GoalsListView.swift:211-220; GoalDetailView.swift:51-59` → Android: `absent (GoalsListScreen.kt LazyColumn, GoalDetailScreen.kt LazyColumn)`
  - Both iOS screens support .refreshable (pull to refresh with light/success haptics). Neither Android screen wraps its LazyColumn in a pull-to-refresh container.
  - **Fix:** Wrap each LazyColumn in a PullToRefreshBox (material3 pulltorefresh) that calls vm.load()/vm.load(goalId).
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Create sheet missing 'Retirement' category**
  - iOS: `GoalsListView.swift:607 (retirement, 🌴)` → Android: `GoalCreateSheet.kt:62-71 (8 categories, no retirement)`
  - iOS offers 9 categories including ('retirement','🌴'). Android lists only 8 — the retirement option is absent entirely.
  - **Fix:** Add Triple("retirement", "🌴", locale.t("goals.catRetirement")) to categoryChoices, preserving iOS ordering (after wedding, before other).
- 🟡 **[behavior] Deadline picker replaced by raw text field instead of date picker**
  - iOS: `GoalsListView.swift:654-665 (Toggle + DatePicker default = now+90 days)` → Android: `GoalCreateSheet.kt:118-133 (clickable chip toggle + NBTextField placeholder '2026-12-31')`
  - iOS uses a Toggle plus a native DatePicker pre-seeded to today+90 days, guaranteeing a valid yyyy-MM-dd. Android uses a chip toggle plus a free-text NBTextField where the user must manually type a yyyy-MM-dd string; the submit only accepts it if length>=10, and any malformed entry silently yields a wrong/empty deadline. Also no default date is provided.
  - **Fix:** Use a DatePickerDialog (material3) gated by the hasDeadline toggle, defaulting to now+90 days, and format the chosen date as yyyy-MM-dd, removing the raw text field.
- 🟡 **[visual] Empty state has no 'New goal' CTA and is double-wrapped in a card**
  - iOS: `GoalsListView.swift:105-117 (NBEmptyState with systemImage 'target' + action label goals.new)` → Android: `GoalsListScreen.kt:131-138 (NBCard { NBEmptyState(...) } no icon, no action)`
  - iOS empty state shows a target icon and a primary action button ('New goal') that opens the create sheet. Android calls NBEmptyState without icon, without actionLabel/onAction (so no CTA), AND wraps it inside an extra NBCard even though NBEmptyState already draws its own nbCard — producing a nested double border/shadow.
  - **Fix:** Call NBEmptyState directly (no outer NBCard) with icon = a target/flag ImageVector, actionLabel = locale.t('goals.new'), onAction = { showCreate = true }.
- 🟡 **[visual] Detail hero current-amount font wrong (mono 28 vs Inter Black 34)**
  - iOS: `GoalDetailView.swift:134-136 (AppFont.black(34))` → Android: `GoalDetailScreen.kt:232 (SolvioFonts.amountLarge = monoBold(28))`
  - iOS renders the big current amount in Inter Black at size 34. Android renders it in monospaced bold at size 28 (amountLarge). Different typeface family and 6pt smaller.
  - **Fix:** Use SolvioFonts.black(34) for the hero current amount to match iOS (not amountLarge).
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] Detail hero progress sub-line copy diverges from iOS ofFmt**
  - iOS: `GoalDetailView.swift:137 (goalDetail.ofFmt = 'of %@ • %@%%' / 'z %@ • %@%%')` → Android: `GoalDetailScreen.kt:233-236 ('${Fmt.amount(target)} · $pctLabel%')`
  - iOS formats the line via the localized goalDetail.ofFmt key, producing 'of 3000 PLN • 50%' (EN) / 'z 3000 PLN • 50%' (PL) with a leading word and a bullet '•'. Android hardcodes the string without the leading 'of'/'z' and uses a middle dot '·' instead of '•', bypassing the existing goalDetail.ofFmt key.
  - **Fix:** Use String.format(locale.t('goalDetail.ofFmt'), Fmt.amount(target, g.currency), pctLabel.toString()) instead of the hardcoded concatenation.
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] Missing L10n keys used by iOS Goals not present in Android L10n**
  - iOS: `GoalsListView.swift:172,177,241,242,158,400,518,522,527,531,785; GoalDetailView.swift:262,272` → Android: `L10n.kt (keys absent)`
  - Android L10n is missing several keys iOS uses: goals.overdue, goals.today, goals.tomorrow, goals.quickAmounts, goals.newHint, goals.openDetailHint, goals.tipsCollapseHint, goals.tipsExpandHint, goalDetail.addFundsHint, goalDetail.deleteHint. (Note goals.addFunds IS present.) These are needed once the deadline states, quick amounts, and accessibility hints are ported.
  - **Fix:** Add the missing goals.* and goalDetail.* keys (PL+EN) to L10n.kt matching iOS values before wiring the corresponding features.
- ⚪ **[visual] Goal card normal deadline uses full date instead of day-month**
  - iOS: `GoalsListView.swift:531 (Fmt.dayMonth(iso))` → Android: `GoalsListScreen.kt:347 (Fmt.date(d) = 'd MMM yyyy')`
  - iOS shows a compact day+month (no year) on the goal card for normal deadlines. Android shows the full 'd MMM yyyy' date, which is longer and includes the year.
  - **Fix:** Add Fmt.dayMonth (parse yyyy-MM-dd, format 'd MMM' / day-month) and use it for the card deadline label (and in the new deadline-status logic).
- ⚪ **[behavior] Detail screen does not seed from cached goal (extra spinner)**
  - iOS: `GoalDetailView.swift:41-50 (seeds vm.goal from store.goals before network load)` → Android: `GoalDetailScreen.kt:107 (always Loading until GoalsRepo.list() returns)`
  - iOS pops the goal out of the in-memory AppDataStore so the detail renders instantly, then refreshes from network. Android always starts in Loading state and re-fetches the full list, showing a spinner every time the user opens a goal.
  - **Fix:** Pass the already-loaded goal (or read from a shared store) into GoalDetailScreen as initial state so it renders immediately, then refresh via vm.load(goalId).
- ⚪ **[copy-l10n] Header subtitle uses sectionCompleted instead of statCompleted; missing screen header on detail differs**
  - iOS: `GoalsListView.swift:77 (uses goals.sectionActive + goals.statCompleted)` → Android: `GoalsListScreen.kt:121 (uses goals.sectionActive + goals.sectionCompleted)`
  - iOS builds the header subtitle from goals.sectionActive lowercased + goals.statCompleted lowercased; Android uses goals.sectionCompleted. The two completed keys currently resolve to identical text so the output matches today, but the key choice is inconsistent and will drift if either string changes.
  - **Fix:** Use locale.t('goals.statCompleted').lowercase() to match iOS exactly.
- ⚪ **[visual] Detail KPI/priority icons and per-month icon missing**
  - iOS: `GoalDetailView.swift:179-224, 314-320 (miniKPI with SF Symbols: calendar, arrow.down.circle, flame/equal.circle/leaf)` → Android: `GoalDetailScreen.kt:269-287 (plain StatTile, no leading icons)`
  - iOS detail KPI tiles each show a leading icon (calendar for per-month, arrow.down.circle for deposits, and a priority-specific icon flame/equal.circle/leaf). Android reuses the list StatTile which has no icon, so all three KPI tiles are icon-less.
  - **Fix:** Either add a leading icon variant to the KPI tiles on detail (calendar, download, and priority icon) or accept a minor deviation; to match iOS, render an Icon + label row above the value.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Detail deposits not reverse/ordered and amount type handling differs**
  - iOS: `GoalDetailView.swift:276-312 (Fmt.amount(dep.amount) on MoneyString, divider tinted foreground)` → Android: `GoalDetailScreen.kt:310-341 (Fmt.amount(dep.amount.toDouble()), divider border.alpha 0.4)`
  - Both render deposits in API order (no explicit sort on either side, so OK). Minor visual: iOS divider uses Theme.foreground (solid); Android uses border at 40% alpha — a lighter divider than iOS.
  - **Fix:** Use palette.foreground (full opacity) for the deposit-row divider to match iOS, or confirm border-thin is intended.
- ⚪ **[visual] Create/Deposit sheets use bottom-sheet with inline buttons vs iOS NavigationStack toolbar**
  - iOS: `GoalsListView.swift:612-711, 754-848 (NavigationStack, nav title, Cancel/Save toolbar items, medium+large detents)` → Android: `GoalCreateSheet.kt:73-162, DepositSheet.kt:50-101 (ModalBottomSheet, title Text, inline Cancel/Save buttons)`
  - iOS presents these as multi-detent sheets with a navigation bar title and Cancel/Save in the toolbar. Android uses a ModalBottomSheet with the title as a plain Text and Cancel/Save as inline buttons at the bottom. Functionally close, but the chrome (nav title placement, detent behavior, Save in top-right) differs. Acceptable platform adaptation but note for pixel parity.
  - **Fix:** Acceptable as platform idiom; if strict parity desired, render a top bar inside the sheet with title centered and Save aligned trailing. Otherwise leave as-is.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Create sheet does not allow priority selection (iOS keeps default 'medium' too — parity OK, note)**
  - iOS: `GoalsListView.swift:595 (priority defaults 'medium', no UI to change)` → Android: `GoalCreateSheet.kt:149 (priority = 'medium' hardcoded)`
  - Neither platform exposes priority selection in the create sheet; both default to 'medium'. This matches. Flagged only to confirm it is intentional and not a missing control.
  - **Fix:** No change needed — parity confirmed.

## L10n (localization string tables — PL/EN) — parity ~52% (20 findings)

_The Android L10n.kt is a single-file map (PL + EN, 1290 keys each) intended to mirror iOS, which is split across L10n.swift (PL, 1401 keys) and L10n+EN.swift (EN, 1371 keys). Three classes of divergence exist and they are severe. (1) STRUCTURAL: 219 iOS keys are entirely absent from Android — including the ENTIRE Deals hub hero/freshness/filters/onboarding block, the ENTIRE Incomes feature, the ENTIRE Welcome onboarding carousel, the Settings About/danger-zone/delete-account/export block, savings smart-insight banners, scanInsight post-scan banner, dozens of a11y hints, and ALL CLDR plural variants (.one/.few/.many). (2) BEHAVIORAL: iOS has a full Polish CLDR plural engine (tPlural with one/few/many) and a PL→EN→key fallback chain; Android has NO plural support at all and NO EN fallback (lookup returns the raw key immediately), and its naive format() does not collapse "%%" or handle "%.2f"/"%.0f", so several format strings render broken literals. (3) CONTENT: 175 PL values and 159 EN values that exist on both sides have DIFFERENT text — eyebrows differ (iOS "OKAZJE" vs Android "// OKAZJE"), titles/subtitles fully rewritten, status tags differ in case, and Android invents key names (nearbyStores.*, productSearch.*, scanFab.scanReceipt, analyze.summary/savings/verdict*) that do not exist in the iOS source of truth. This is far from parity._

- 🔴 **[bug] No CLDR plural engine on Android (iOS tPlural absent) + all plural keys missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/AppLocale.swift:90-150 (tPlural, pluralCategory, polishCategory)` → Android: `android/.../core/AppLocale.kt:45-54 (only t/format, no plural); core/L10n.kt (all .one/.few/.many keys absent)`
  - iOS picks the correct Polish form (one/few/many) for every counted string via tPlural + the Polish CLDR rule (one: n==1; few: n%10∈2..4 and n%100∉12..14; many: else), and the dictionaries carry .one/.few/.many variants. Android has NO tPlural / no PluralCategory / no polishCategory anywhere, and the map is missing EVERY plural variant key (e.g. analysis.insightsCountFmt.one/few/many, audit.txnsFmt.*, dashboard.dayBreakdownTxns.*, deals.freshnessDayFmt.*, deals.heroCountFmt.*, deals.heroCountBig.*, deals.heroOffersFmt.*, deals.urgencyDaysFmt.*, prices.productsCountFmt.*, prices.comparisonsCountFmt.*, receiptDetail.itemsCount.*, savings.seeAllGoalsFmt.*, scanQueue.batchSaved.*, virtualReceipt.itemsCount.*, analyze.receiptsCountFmt.*). On Android Polish counts will always render the grammatically wrong base form (e.g. '2 paragonów' / '2 transakcji' instead of '2 paragony' / '2 transakcje').
  - **Fix:** Add a tPlural(baseKey, count) to AppLocale.kt that implements the Polish CLDR rule (port polishCategory) and the same one→[.one,base], few→[.few,.many,base], many→[.many,base] candidate fallback as iOS; add all *.one/.few/.many key/value pairs to both pl and en maps in L10n.kt; route every counted string through tPlural.
- 🔴 **[missing-feature] Entire Incomes feature strings missing (24 keys)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:883-908 + L10n+EN.swift:853-878 (incomes.*)` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS defines a full Incomes module (incomes.title/eyebrow/subtitle/linkSub/totalEyebrow/shareEyebrow/perMonth/emptyTitle/emptySubtitle/addFirst/addAnother/add/addTitle/editTitle/name/namePlaceholder/amount/period/emoji/periodMonthly/periodWeekly/periodYearly/periodOneoff/inactive/deleteConfirm/deleteConfirmMsg). None of these exist in the Android map, so the entire Incomes screen would be untranslated (raw keys).
  - **Fix:** Add all incomes.* keys (PL + EN) to L10n.kt with the exact iOS values.
- 🟠 **[behavior] L10n.lookup has no EN fallback (iOS falls back PL→EN→key)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/AppLocale.swift:50-52 (t: strings[language]?[key] ?? strings[.en]?[key] ?? key)` → Android: `android/.../core/L10n.kt:13-14 (lookup returns map[key] ?: key — no cross-language fallback)`
  - iOS t() tries the active language, then English, then the raw key. Android lookup() returns the raw key the instant it is missing in the active language. Because the Android pl/en maps currently have identical key sets the divergence is latent today, but the moment any key is added to only one side (which the iOS source already does for several keys) Android shows the dotted key on screen instead of the English string.
  - **Fix:** Change L10n.lookup to: (table[key]) ?: en[key] ?: key — i.e. fall back to the EN map before returning the raw key, matching iOS t().
- 🟠 **[bug] format() does not collapse %% and breaks %.2f/%.0f → wrong rendered text**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:579,1216,1035; L10n+EN.swift:557,1180,1005 (uses %%, %.2f%%, %.0f via String(format:))` → Android: `android/.../core/AppLocale.kt:47-53 (replaceFirst(Regex("%[@dDs]")) — no %% handling, no float specifier)`
  - iOS String(format:) collapses %% to %. Android format() only replaceFirst's %@/%d/%D/%s and never touches %%, so: goalDetail.ofFmt 'z %@ • %@%%' renders a literal '%%'; savings.pctUsed '%d%% wykorzystane' renders '5%% wykorzystane'; budgetSheet.savingsRateFmt '...: %d%%' renders '...: 90%%'. Additionally audit.timesBoughtFmt is '%.0f×' on iOS but Android stores '%@×' and its regex has no float specifier; quickSplit.mustSum100 is '...%.2f%%' on iOS vs Android '...100% (jest %@)'. Each produces visibly wrong copy.
  - **Fix:** Rewrite format() to use a proper printf-style pass: replace %% with % after substitution (or use Java String.format with a converted template), and support %.Nf. Normalize the numeric format keys so the %d count and trailing %% render as one percent sign exactly like iOS.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Entire Welcome onboarding carousel strings missing (15 keys)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:1472-1486 + L10n+EN.swift:1434-1448 (welcome.*)` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS has a 3-page first-launch onboarding carousel: welcome.skip/next/getStarted/page1.title/page1.body/page2.title/page2.body/page3.title/page3.body/skipHint/swipeHint/nextHint/getStartedHint/pageOfFmt. All absent on Android.
  - **Fix:** Add all welcome.* keys (PL + EN) to L10n.kt with iOS values; ensure the Android onboarding screen reads them.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Settings About / Danger-zone / Delete-account / Export-data strings missing (24 keys)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:1393-1443 + L10n+EN.swift:1355-1405` → Android: `absent`
  - Missing: settings.aboutEyebrow, settings.aboutTitle, settings.version, settings.terms, settings.privacy, settings.support, settings.dangerZone, settings.copyEmail, settings.emailCopied, settings.addCategory, settings.addBudget, settings.noBudgetsTitle, settings.noCategoriesTitle, settings.noRulesTitle, settings.deleteAccount, settings.deleteAccountDesc, settings.deleteAccountConfirmTitle, settings.deleteAccountConfirmMsg, settings.deletingAccount, settings.deleteAccountDone, settings.deleteAccountFailed. The iOS Settings About section and the entire Danger Zone (delete account, export) would be untranslated on Android.
  - **Fix:** Add all the missing settings.* keys (PL + EN) to L10n.kt with iOS values.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Savings smart-insight banner + goals/challenges preview strings missing (~30 keys)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:818-843 + L10n+EN.swift:790-815` → Android: `absent`
  - Missing savings.insightOverBudget(+Body), insightNearGoal(+Body), insightOverdue(+Body), insightOnTrack(+Body), insightSetupTitle/Body, insightAddBudgetTitle/Body, insightAddGoalTitle/Body, savings.goalsTitle/goalsEmptyTitle/goalsEmptySub/viewAllGoals/challengesTitle/challengesEmptyTitle/challengesEmptySub/viewAllChallenges/dealsTeaserTitle/dealsTeaserSub/totalSavedTile/monthShort/seeAllGoalsFmt. The Savings hub's dynamic AI hero banner and the goals/challenges/deals preview sections have no Android strings.
  - **Fix:** Add all the missing savings.* keys (PL + EN) to L10n.kt with iOS values. Note these use {cat}/{pct}/{name}/{amount} named placeholders, not %@, so the Android substitution must support named-token replacement too.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Deals hub hero / freshness / filters / onboarding / promo-badge strings missing (~60 keys)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:76-167 + L10n+EN.swift:67-146` → Android: `absent (Android has only deals.navTitle/eyebrow/headerTitle/headerSubtitle/trending*/validUntilFmt/openLeaflet/runningTitle/launcher*/tile*)`
  - Android is missing the whole Deals visual surface: deals.heroTitle/heroLoading/heroCountFmt(+plurals)/heroCountBig(+plurals)/heroEyebrow/heroSavingsLabel/heroOffersFmt(+plurals)/aiPowered/refresh/aiToolsEyebrow/aiToolsTitle/showLess/showMoreFmt/countShownFmt/onboardingTitle/onboardingBody/filterAll/filterPersonalized/filterEndingSoon/filterTopDiscount/resetFilter/loadingTitle/loadingHint/emptyFirstTitle/emptyFirstBody/emptyFirstCTA/emptyFilteredTitle/emptyFilteredBody/personalized/urgencyExpired/urgencyToday/urgencyTomorrow/urgencyDaysFmt(+plurals)/promoBuyGet/promoAppOnly/promoMultipack/freshnessLive/freshnessNow/freshnessMinFmt/freshnessHrFmt/freshnessDayFmt(+plurals)/freshnessStale/freshnessLoading/freshnessEmpty/freshnessUnknown/footerLive/footerEstimate/footerSchedule.
  - **Fix:** Add all the missing deals.* keys (PL + EN, including plural variants) to L10n.kt with iOS values.
- 🟠 **[behavior] Android invents non-iOS key namespaces (nearbyStores.*, productSearch.*) instead of iOS nearby.*/search.***
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:247-287 (nearby.*) and 270-288 (search.*) + EN equivalents` → Android: `android/.../core/L10n.kt:684-722 (nearbyStores.*, productSearch.*)`
  - iOS uses the key prefixes nearby.* (nearby.navTitle/title/subtitle/searching/found/stores/knownBrands/distance/meters/km/openingHours/noStores/noStoresDesc/locationNeeded/locationNeededDesc/openSettings + nearby.radius/allStores/knownOnly/phone/navigate) and search.* (search.navTitle/title/subtitle/placeholder/searching/results/cheapest/average/promo/available/unavailable/alternatives/tip/noResults/noResultsDesc/estimated/perUnit/priceRange). Android renamed these to nearbyStores.* and productSearch.* — different keys entirely. The iOS source-of-truth keys nearby.* and search.* are therefore MISSING from Android, and Android's nearbyStores.*/productSearch.* are extra keys that don't exist on iOS. iOS-derived UI code expecting nearby.*/search.* would render raw keys.
  - **Fix:** Rename Android keys to the iOS namespaces (nearby.*, search.*) and add the iOS keys missing on Android (nearby.radius/allStores/knownOnly/phone/navigate; search.available/unavailable/perUnit/priceRange). Drop the duplicate nearbyStores.*/productSearch.* set.
- 🟠 **[copy-l10n] 175 Polish + 159 English shared-key VALUES differ from iOS**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift + L10n+EN.swift (passim)` → Android: `android/.../core/L10n.kt (passim)`
  - For 175 PL and 159 EN keys that exist on BOTH platforms the text differs, so the same screen reads differently on Android. High-visibility examples: eyebrows — iOS 'OKAZJE'/'WYDATKI'/'GRUPY'/'PANEL'/'PODZIAŁY' vs Android prefixes with '// ' ('// OKAZJE','// WYDATKI','// GRUPY','// PULPIT','// PODZIAŁY'); nav.dashboard iOS 'Panel' vs Android 'Pulpit'; nav.loyalty iOS 'Lojalność' vs Android 'Karty'; dashboard.totalSpent iOS 'Wydano łącznie' vs 'Łącznie wydane'; dashboard.yourMoney iOS 'Twoje pieniądze' vs 'Twoje finanse'; dashboard.rolling30 iOS '30-dniowe okno kroczące' vs 'Ostatnie 30 dni'; dashboard.perDayShort iOS '/dzień' vs ' /dzień' (leading space); expenses.title iOS 'Wydatki' vs 'Twoje wydatki'; expenses.confirmDelete/deleteConfirmMsg swapped meaning (iOS title 'Potwierdź usunięcie' + msg 'Usunąć ten wydatek?' vs Android title 'Usunąć wydatek?' + msg 'Tej operacji nie można cofnąć.'); group settlement status tags iOS Title-case ('Rozliczono'/'Oczekujące'/'Odrzucone') vs Android ALL-CAPS ('ROZLICZONE'/'OCZEKUJE'/'ODRZUCONE'); login.email iOS 'Email' vs 'E-mail', login.emailPlaceholder iOS 'ty@example.com' vs 'imie@domena.pl', login.privacyNote completely different copy; splash.tagline iOS 'Inteligentne finanse' vs 'Mądre finanse'; toast.error iOS 'Błąd' vs 'Coś poszło nie tak' (and common.error inverted). Many subtitles/descriptions are fully rewritten (deals/savings/shoppingList/analyze/advisor headers). EN side also has en-GB vs en-US spelling drift (Analysing/Analyse/Personalised/Colour) vs iOS en-US (Analyzing/Analyze/Personalized/Color).
  - **Fix:** Treat iOS L10n.swift/L10n+EN.swift as source of truth and overwrite the 175 PL + 159 EN diverging Android values verbatim (including the eyebrow style — decide one canonical form and apply identically on both platforms; iOS does NOT use the '// ' prefix for these eyebrows). Standardize EN spelling to match iOS en-US.
- 🟡 **[missing-popup] scanInsight post-scan savings banner strings missing (3 keys)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:1446-1448 + L10n+EN.swift:1408-1410` → Android: `absent`
  - Missing scanInsight.savingsTitle ('Mogłeś zaoszczędzić %@'), scanInsight.savingsDesc, scanInsight.action ('ZOBACZ'). iOS shows a savings-insight banner/toast right after a scan; Android has no strings for it.
  - **Fix:** Add scanInsight.* keys (PL + EN) to L10n.kt with iOS values and wire the post-scan banner.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] groupDetail menu + count-format strings missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:538-544 + L10n+EN.swift:516-522` → Android: `absent (Android instead has groupDetail.tabSplits/tabReceipts/tabSettlements which do NOT exist on iOS)`
  - Missing groupDetail.menu/menuHint/quickSplitHint/receiptsHint/settlementsHint and the count formatters groupDetail.sharesCountFmt ('%d udziałów') / unsettledCountFmt ('%d nierozliczonych'). Android additionally invented groupDetail.tabSplits/tabReceipts/tabSettlements that have no iOS counterpart — indicating the Android group-detail layout uses tabs while iOS uses section headers, a structural divergence.
  - **Fix:** Add the missing groupDetail.* keys (with plural variants for the count formatters). Reconcile the tab vs section structure — if iOS uses section eyebrows, drop the Android tab keys and match.
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] common.dismiss and common.clear missing on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:47,152 + L10n+EN.swift:38,131` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS defines common.clear ('Wyczyść'/'Clear') and common.dismiss ('Zamknij'/'Dismiss'). Android has neither; any caller will render the raw key.
  - **Fix:** Add common.clear and common.dismiss (PL + EN) to L10n.kt.
- 🟡 **[behavior] Android invents analyze.* result keys (summary/savings/sources/tip/verdict*) not in iOS**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:210-218 (analyze.resultEyebrow/savingsLabel/paid/bestPossible/cheaperAtFmt/overpaid/fair/underpaid/noData)` → Android: `android/.../core/L10n.kt:776-787 (analyze.savings/paid/bestPossible/cheaperAtFmt/tip/sources/summary/verdictOverpaid/verdictFair/verdictUnderpaid/verdictNoData)`
  - iOS verdict keys are analyze.overpaid/fair/underpaid/noData and label analyze.savingsLabel; Android uses analyze.verdictOverpaid/verdictFair/verdictUnderpaid/verdictNoData and analyze.savings, plus extra analyze.tip/sources/summary that iOS does not have for this screen. The values also differ (analyze.paid iOS 'ZAPŁACONO' all-caps vs Android 'Zapłacono'; analyze.bestPossible iOS 'MOŻNA BYŁO' vs Android 'Możliwy minimum'; analyze.cheaperAtFmt iOS '−%@ w %@' vs Android 'Taniej w %@'). The receipt-audit result UI will look different.
  - **Fix:** Align Android analyze.* keys and values to iOS: use analyze.overpaid/fair/underpaid/noData, analyze.savingsLabel, restore the all-caps labels and the '−%@ w %@' / '−%@ at %@' format, and drop the non-iOS analyze.tip/sources/summary unless iOS adds them.
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] scanFab key names and values diverge from iOS**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:558-563 + L10n+EN.swift:536-541 (scanFab.title/subtitle/cameraSub/librarySub/virtualSub/quickSplitSub)` → Android: `android/.../core/L10n.kt:332-338 (scanFab.title/scanReceipt/scanReceiptSub/virtualReceipt/virtualReceiptSub/pickFromLibrary/pickFromLibrarySub)`
  - iOS scan FAB sheet keys are scanFab.title/subtitle/cameraSub/librarySub/virtualSub/quickSplitSub. Android uses scanFab.scanReceipt/scanReceiptSub/virtualReceipt/virtualReceiptSub/pickFromLibrary/pickFromLibrarySub and lacks scanFab.subtitle/quickSplitSub.
