Also scanFab.title iOS 'Nowy paragon'/'New receipt' vs Android 'Dodaj paragon'/'Add receipt'. The action-sheet rows and header differ.
  - **Fix:** Adopt iOS scanFab.* key names and values; add scanFab.subtitle and scanFab.quickSplitSub; set scanFab.title to 'Nowy paragon'/'New receipt'.
- 🟡 **[bug] Format-specifier content mismatch: audit.timesBoughtFmt and quickSplit.mustSum100**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:1216,579 + L10n+EN.swift:1180,557` → Android: `android/.../core/L10n.kt:424,236 + 1981(approx),2236(approx)`
  - iOS audit.timesBoughtFmt is '%.0f× kupione' / '%.0f× bought' (float, no decimals); Android stores '%@× kupione' / '%@× bought' and its format() regex %[@dDs] cannot render a float anyway. iOS quickSplit.mustSum100 is 'Musi sumować się do 100%% — obecnie %.2f%%' (two-decimal float + literal %); Android is 'Suma musi wynosić 100% (jest %@)' — different copy, single %@, and the bare '100%' literal won't be affected by format() but the message structure differs. Result: the multiplier/percentage rendering differs from iOS.
  - **Fix:** Either keep %@ on Android and pass a pre-formatted string from the call site, OR add %.Nf support to format() and store '%.0f×' / '%.2f%%' matching iOS; align quickSplit.mustSum100 copy and placeholder with iOS.
- ⚪ **[missing-feature] Accessibility hint strings missing (a11y hints, ~20 keys)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:998-1002,1051-1053,1088-1091 + corresponding EN; goalDetail.*Hint, goals.*Hint, challenges.*Hint, login.*Hint, groupDetail.*Hint, quickSplit.saveHint, groupEdit.saveHint, receipts.openReceiptHint, loyalty.openCardHint` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS provides accessibility hints for VoiceOver: goals.newHint/openDetailHint/tipsExpandHint/tipsCollapseHint, goalDetail.addFundsHint/deleteHint, challenges.newHint/expandHint/collapseHint/saveHint, login.emailHint/continueHint/tryDemoHint/signingInHint, groupDetail.menu/menuHint/quickSplitHint/receiptsHint/settlementsHint, groupEdit.saveHint, quickSplit.saveHint, receipts.openReceiptHint, loyalty.openCardHint. None on Android, so TalkBack reads nothing meaningful.
  - **Fix:** Add all *Hint keys (PL + EN) to L10n.kt and apply them via Modifier.semantics { contentDescription/stateDescription } in the Compose UI.
- ⚪ **[copy-l10n] analysis.weekdayNote describes a different data source than iOS**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:1288 + L10n+EN.swift:1250` → Android: `android/.../core/L10n.kt:395 + EN side`
  - iOS: 'Szacowane z danych bankowych — liczba transakcji w każdym dniu.' / 'Estimated from bank activity — transactions per weekday.' Android: 'Szacowane z anomalii — sumy w danym dniu tygodnia.' / 'Estimated from anomalies — sums per weekday.' This is not just wording — it claims a different source (bank activity vs anomalies) and a different metric (transaction count vs sums), which is misleading.
  - **Fix:** Use the iOS wording verbatim in both languages.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Extra Android keys with no iOS counterpart (login.welcomeBack, expenses.recurring, savings.kpiHealth, savings.seeChallenges, common.continue/tryAgain, receipts.empty/search, deals.openLeaflet, shoppingList.* extras)**
  - iOS: `absent on iOS` → Android: `android/.../core/L10n.kt (login.welcomeBack:50, expenses.recurring, savings.kpiHealth:802, savings.seeChallenges:533, common.continue:22, common.tryAgain:28, receipts.empty:253, receipts.search:256, deals.openLeaflet:729, shoppingList.bestStore/total/savings/summary/grandTotal/savingsVsSingle/multiStore/multiStoreSubtitle/itemEmpty/itemEmptySub/promoMulti/promoX2/promoBundle/promoLoyalty/freshAt/cacheRefresh/badgeUnknown/qty/itemNamePh/sources/tip/alternatives)`
  - 108 Android keys have no iOS equivalent. Some are harmless aliases (common.continue vs login.continue, common.tryAgain vs common.retry) but they signal the Android UI was built against a different key contract than iOS. For shoppingList the Android keys (bestStore/total/savings/summary/grandTotal/savingsVsSingle/multiStore) replace the iOS keys (bestStoreEyebrow/totalLabel/savingsLabel/alternativesTitle/multiStoreGrandTotal/multiStoreEyebrow), so the same screen uses incompatible key names and slightly different copy.
  - **Fix:** Reconcile to the iOS key contract: prefer iOS key names (e.g. login.continue not common.continue, common.retry not common.tryAgain, receipts.emptyTitle not receipts.empty, shoppingList.totalLabel/savingsLabel/bestStoreEyebrow/multiStoreEyebrow/multiStoreGrandTotal). Remove the redundant Android-only aliases once UI references are updated.
- ⚪ **[copy-l10n] Header comments overstate parity ('mirrored 1:1 from iOS')**
  - iOS: `n/a` → Android: `android/.../core/L10n.kt:3-11 (doc comment claims 1:1 mirror)`
  - The Android file header says it is 'mirrored 1:1 from iOS L10n.swift' and that missing keys return the raw key 'so dev catches it instantly'. In reality 219 iOS keys are missing, 175 PL + 159 EN values differ, and 108 extra keys exist — so the claim is inaccurate and there is no plural support. This will mislead the next agent.
  - **Fix:** Update the header to state the actual coverage and the known gaps (no plural engine, no EN fallback) until parity is achieved.

## Groups — parity ~66% (19 findings)

_Android's Groups area covers the same five surfaces as iOS (list, detail, settlements, receipts, quick-split + create sheets) and the heavy data-flow logic (split math, validation, settlement resolution, receipt assignment grouping) is faithfully ported and model field names line up. However there are several real parity gaps: (1) the entire group Edit + Delete flow is missing on Android — there is no overflow menu, no GroupEditSheet, no delete confirmation, and GroupsRepo lacks an update() method, so groups can never be renamed/edited or deleted from mobile; (2) the standalone group-less Quick Split flow (QuickSplitStandaloneSheet) is completely absent — Android's Quick Split CTA just navigates into the first group or shows a toast, the opposite of the iOS fix that intentionally opens a groupless sheet; (3) Android restructured Group Detail into a 3-tab control (Splits/Receipts/Settlements) whereas iOS shows splits inline with two nav buttons, changing layout, navigation and section ordering; (4) swipe-to-delete on list rows, pull-to-refresh on every screen, the in-detail mark-as-settled progress affordance parity, and several copy strings differ. Quick-split equal-mode residual distribution is also a behavioral/financial bug on Android. Visual atoms (cards, borders, shadows, colors, spacing) are otherwise close._

- 🔴 **[missing-feature] Group Edit flow entirely missing (no edit sheet, no overflow menu, no repo method)**
  - iOS: `GroupDetailView.swift:52-75 (toolbar Menu), :93-109 (showEdit sheet), :848-941 (GroupEditSheet)` → Android: `absent (GroupDetailScreen.kt has no menu/edit; Repositories.kt GroupsRepo has no update())`
  - iOS GroupDetailView has a top-bar ellipsis Menu with Edit and Delete. Edit opens GroupEditSheet (name/emoji/currency/mode/description) which calls GroupsRepo.update(id:body:GroupUpdate). Android GroupDetailScreen has only a back button in DetailTopBar — no menu, no GroupEditSheet exists, the GroupUpdate model is absent, and GroupsRepo (Repositories.kt:134-151) exposes only list/detail/create/delete/receipts/settlements/createSplit/settleSplit, with NO update(). Groups cannot be edited at all on Android.
  - **Fix:** Add a GroupUpdate data class to Models.kt; add GroupsRepo.update(id, body: GroupUpdate) (PUT /api/groups/{id}); add a GroupEditSheet composable mirroring GroupCreateSheet pre-filled from the group; add an overflow menu (Edit/Delete) to DetailTopBar that opens it and the delete confirm.
- 🔴 **[missing-popup] Delete group flow missing from detail screen (no menu, no confirmation dialog)**
  - iOS: `GroupDetailView.swift:61-66 (Delete menu item), :110-130 (deleteConfirm alert + popToRoot + store.didMutateGroups)` → Android: `absent (GroupDetailScreen.kt)`
  - iOS shows a destructive Delete in the detail overflow menu, then a confirmation alert (title groupDetail.deleteConfirm, message groupDetail.deleteMsg) that on confirm pops to root, calls GroupsRepo.delete, toasts groupEdit.deleted and syncs the list. Android GroupDetailScreen offers no way to delete a group and shows no confirmation dialog. (GroupsRepo.delete exists but is never wired into detail.)
  - **Fix:** Add a Delete entry to the detail overflow menu and an AlertDialog (groupDetail.deleteConfirm/deleteMsg) that calls GroupsRepo.delete(groupId), pops back, and toasts groupEdit.deleted.
- 🔴 **[missing-feature] Standalone group-less Quick Split sheet not implemented; CTA behaves like the removed iOS behavior**
  - iOS: `QuickSplitStandaloneSheet.swift (whole file), GroupsListView.swift:93-97 + :152-176 (Quick Split opens standalone sheet)` → Android: `GroupsListScreen.kt:168-177 (Quick Split navigates to first group / shows toast)`
  - iOS Quick Split on the list opens QuickSplitStandaloneSheet — a groupless ad-hoc flow (inline participants 'Ja' + others, amount, paid-by, equal preview) that silently creates an ad-hoc group (emoji ⚡️, mode 'adhoc') + split in one round-trip. The iOS comment explicitly says routing into the first group was the OLD confusing behavior that was fixed. Android does exactly that removed behavior: navigates to groups/{firstId} when a group exists, else toasts groups.quickSplitUnavailable. The entire standalone sheet (header eyebrow/title/subtitle, participants section, paid-by chips, preview, ad-hoc create+split submit) is absent.
  - **Fix:** Port QuickSplitStandaloneSheet as a Compose ModalBottomSheet with participant rows, paid-by chips, equal preview, and the ad-hoc create→detail→createSplit submission; wire the list Quick Split CTA to open it (remove the navigate-to-first-group / quickSplitUnavailable fallback).
- 🟠 **[behavior] Group Detail restructured into 3-tab control vs iOS inline layout + 2 nav buttons**
  - iOS: `GroupDetailView.swift:19-39 (body order), :216-236 (tabsRow = 2 secondary buttons Receipts+Settlements), :240-256 members, :285-321 quickSplitCTA, :325-349 splits inline` → Android: `GroupDetailScreen.kt:120-160 (Hero → KpiStrip → QuickSplitCTA → TabRow(Splits/Receipts/Settlements) → per-tab content)`
  - iOS detail shows hero → KPI → a row of TWO secondary buttons (Receipts, Settlements) that PUSH to those screens → members strip → quick-split CTA → splits list, all on one scroll. Android replaced this with a 3-segment TabRow (Splits/Receipts/Settlements); Splits tab shows members+splits inline, Receipts/Settlements tabs show a single secondary button that navigates. Section ordering also differs (Android puts QuickSplitCTA above the tab/members, iOS puts it below members). This is a structural layout + navigation-model divergence and introduces extra L10n keys (groupDetail.tabSplits/tabReceipts/tabSettlements) that don't exist on iOS.
  - **Fix:** Replace the TabRow with iOS's tabsRow: a Row of two NBSecondaryButtons (nav.receipts + groupDetail.settlements) that navigate directly; render MembersStrip then QuickSplitCTA then the splits list inline (matching iOS order hero→kpi→tabsRow→members→quickSplitCTA→splits). Drop the tab enum.
- 🟠 **[bug] Quick Split equal-mode does not distribute rounding residual to last member (sum may not equal total)**
  - iOS: `GroupDetailView.swift:794-810 (each = round(total/n); last gets total - baseSum)` → Android: `QuickSplitSheet.kt:251-256 (each = round(total/size); every member gets each)`
  - iOS deliberately computes each = round(total/n) for the first n-1 members and gives the LAST member the residual (total - baseSum) so portions sum exactly to total (the comment notes 100/3 → 33.33×3 = 99.99 ≠ 100 was rejected by the backend). Android gives every selected member the same rounded `each`, so for 100/3 the portions sum to 99.99 and the backend can reject the split (or balances drift). Financial correctness bug.
  - **Fix:** In the SplitMode.Equal branch, compute each = round(total/n*100)/100, baseSum = each*(n-1), last = round((total-baseSum)*100)/100, and assign the last selected member `last` (mirror iOS submit()).
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Swipe-to-delete on group list rows missing**
  - iOS: `GroupsListView.swift:294-301 (.swipeActions delete → pendingDelete) + :115-140 delete confirm alert` → Android: `absent (GroupsListScreen.kt GroupCard has only clickable open)`
  - iOS list rows have a trailing destructive swipe action that sets pendingDelete and shows a delete confirmation alert (title common.delete, message = group name, destructive Delete → GroupsRepo.delete + toast.deleted). Android GroupCard supports tap-to-open only; there is no swipe action and no per-row delete confirmation, so the ONLY way deletion exists on iOS-from-list is entirely absent on Android (and combined with finding #2, deletion is impossible anywhere on Android).
  - **Fix:** Wrap each GroupCard in a SwipeToDismiss/anchored swipe revealing a destructive Delete that opens an AlertDialog (common.delete / group name) calling GroupsRepo.delete then vm.load().
- 🟡 **[behavior] Pull-to-refresh missing on all four Groups screens**
  - iOS: `GroupsListView.swift:84-89; GroupDetailView.swift:44-51; GroupSettlementsView.swift:36; GroupReceiptsView.swift:44` → Android: `absent (GroupsListScreen.kt:150, GroupDetailScreen.kt:115, GroupSettlementsScreen.kt:134, GroupReceiptsScreen.kt:94 — plain LazyColumns)`
  - Every iOS Groups screen is .refreshable (list does light haptic + force reload + success haptic; detail/settlements/receipts reload on pull). None of the Android screens wrap their LazyColumn in a pull-refresh container, so users cannot manually refresh.
  - **Fix:** Wrap each LazyColumn in PullToRefreshBox (material3) tied to vm.load(); on the list add the light/success haptic pattern to match iOS.
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] Settlements header eyebrow hardcoded "GROUP" instead of localized groupSettlements.groupEyebrow**
  - iOS: `GroupSettlementsView.swift:54 (NBEyebrow text: locale.t("groupSettlements.groupEyebrow")) → PL "GRUPA"/EN "GROUP"` → Android: `GroupSettlementsScreen.kt:200 (NBEyebrow(text = "GROUP"))`
  - iOS uses the localized key groupSettlements.groupEyebrow (PL 'GRUPA', EN 'GROUP'). Android hardcodes the English literal 'GROUP', so Polish users see 'GROUP' in the settlements header.
  - **Fix:** Replace NBEyebrow(text = "GROUP") with NBEyebrow(text = locale.t("groupSettlements.groupEyebrow")) (the key exists on iOS; add it to L10n.kt if missing).
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] groups.subtitle copy differs and the count subtitle drops the "active" suffix**
  - iOS: `GroupsListView.swift:241-249 (subtitle = "{count} {challenges.active lowercased}"); L10n groups.subtitle PL "Podziel wydatki z innymi" / EN "Split expenses with others"` → Android: `GroupsListScreen.kt:156-160 (subtitle = "{groups.size}"); L10n.kt groups.subtitle PL "Wspólne wydatki" / EN "Shared expenses"`
  - Two mismatches: (a) the screen title text — iOS groups.subtitle = 'Split expenses with others' / 'Podziel wydatki z innymi' but Android = 'Shared expenses' / 'Wspólne wydatki'. (b) the header subtitle line — iOS shows '<count> active' (count + challenges.active lowercased), Android shows just the bare number with no word.
  - **Fix:** Change L10n.kt groups.subtitle to match iOS wording; change NBScreenHeader subtitle to "${groups.size} " + locale.t("challenges.active").lowercase().
- 🟡 **[visual] Group list empty state uses generic card instead of NBEmptyState with icon + action button**
  - iOS: `GroupsListView.swift:269-281 (NBEmptyState systemImage person.3.fill, title, subtitle, action label groups.new → showCreate)` → Android: `GroupsListScreen.kt:203-211 (plain NBCard with EYEBROW + title + subtitle, no icon, no action button)`
  - iOS empty groups state is a full NBEmptyState: person.3.fill icon, title, subtitle AND a primary action button ('New' → opens create sheet). Android renders a bare NBCard with a hardcoded 'EMPTY' eyebrow, title and subtitle — no icon and, crucially, no call-to-action button to create the first group.
  - **Fix:** Use the shared NBEmptyState component (or replicate it) with a person icon, groups.emptyTitle/emptySubtitle, and an action button (groups.new) that sets showCreate = true.
- 🟡 **[behavior] Members strip rendered only inside Splits tab; not always visible like iOS**
  - iOS: `GroupDetailView.swift:31 + 240-256 (membersSection always shown when members exist)` → Android: `GroupDetailScreen.kt:137-147 (MembersStrip only rendered under DetailTab.Splits)`
  - iOS always shows the horizontal members strip in detail (above the quick-split CTA). Android only renders MembersStrip when the Splits tab is selected, so switching to the Receipts/Settlements tab hides members. Combined with finding #4 (tabs shouldn't exist), members visibility is incorrect.
  - **Fix:** When restructuring detail to match iOS (finding #4), render MembersStrip unconditionally (when members non-empty) regardless of section.
- ⚪ **[copy-l10n] Detail hero NBEyebrow falls back to literal "GROUP" instead of mode; group-card mode badge differs**
  - iOS: `GroupDetailView.swift:149 (NBEyebrow text: (g.mode ?? "group").uppercased())` → Android: `GroupDetailScreen.kt:246 (NBEyebrow text: (group.mode ?: "GROUP").uppercase())`
  - Minor: both fall back to a non-localized 'GROUP'/'group' literal for the hero eyebrow when mode is null (matches iOS, acceptable). Note though that the raw mode value (e.g. 'ongoing','trip') is shown un-localized as an eyebrow on BOTH platforms here — flagged only for awareness, not a divergence.
  - **Fix:** No change required for parity (both match); optionally localize via modeLabel() on both platforms in a later pass.
- ⚪ **[visual] Detail empty-splits and settlements/receipts empty states use ad-hoc cards instead of NBEmptyState (missing icons/actions)**
  - iOS: `GroupDetailView.swift:336-342 (NBEmptyState square.stack.3d.up + action); GroupSettlementsView.swift:114-118 (checkmark.seal.fill); GroupReceiptsView.swift:24-28 (doc.text.viewfinder)` → Android: `GroupDetailScreen.kt:469-485 EmptySplits; GroupSettlementsScreen.kt:258-271 EmptyDebtsCard; GroupReceiptsScreen.kt:149-163 EmptyReceiptsCard`
  - iOS uses NBEmptyState (centered icon + title + subtitle, plus an action button for empty splits) for these three empty states. Android replaces each with a left-aligned NBCard carrying a hardcoded 'EMPTY' eyebrow and no icon; the empty-debts and empty-receipts variants also lack the iOS icon. EmptySplits does keep an action button.
  - **Fix:** Replace EmptySplits/EmptyDebtsCard/EmptyReceiptsCard with the shared NBEmptyState (matching icons square.stack.3d.up / checkmark.seal.fill / doc.text.viewfinder) and keep the create action on empty splits.
- ⚪ **[visual] Quick Split create sheet uses bottom-sheet save buttons; iOS uses Cancel/Save toolbar with Save disabled on empty name — minor but description field type differs**
  - iOS: `GroupDetailView.swift (GroupCreateSheet) lives in GroupsListView.swift:544-799 — description uses multi-line TextEditor (minHeight 72)` → Android: `GroupCreateSheet.kt:137-142 (description uses single-line NBTextField)`
  - iOS GroupCreate description field is a multi-line TextEditor (~72pt min height) with its own label; Android uses a single-line NBTextField for description, so multi-line descriptions are awkward to enter and the visual block height differs.
  - **Fix:** Use a multi-line text field (NBTextField with singleLine=false / minLines, or a TextField with min height) for the description in GroupCreateSheet to match iOS TextEditor.
- ⚪ **[copy-l10n] Create sheet name placeholder hardcoded "Trip to Berlin" instead of localized groups.namePlaceholder**
  - iOS: `GroupsListView.swift:576 (placeholder: locale.t("groups.namePlaceholder"))` → Android: `GroupCreateSheet.kt:88 (placeholder = "Trip to Berlin")`
  - iOS uses the localized groups.namePlaceholder for the group-name field. Android hardcodes the English literal 'Trip to Berlin', which won't localize to Polish.
  - **Fix:** Replace the literal with placeholder = locale.t("groups.namePlaceholder") (add the key to L10n.kt if absent).
- ⚪ **[visual] Detail hero missing the members-count and date leading icons (Label glyphs)**
  - iOS: `GroupDetailView.swift:163-177 (Label person.2 for member count, Label calendar for created date)` → Android: `GroupDetailScreen.kt:253-267 (plain Text for member count and date, no leading icons)`
  - iOS hero meta row shows a person.2 icon before the member count and a calendar icon before the created date (SwiftUI Labels). Android renders both as plain text with no leading icons, so the hero meta row looks visually sparser than iOS.
  - **Fix:** Add small leading Icons (Person/Group icon + Calendar icon, ~caption size, mutedForeground) before the member-count and date texts in Hero.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Receipt card item-quantity guard differs (× shown for q==1 risk vs iOS q != 1)**
  - iOS: `GroupReceiptsView.swift:177-181 (if let q = item.quantity, q != 1 → show × qty)` → Android: `GroupReceiptsScreen.kt:278-280 (q.takeIf { it != 1.0 && it > 0 } → show × qty)`
  - Minor behavioral nuance: iOS shows the ×quantity chip whenever quantity != 1 (including fractional like 0.5 or negative). Android additionally requires q > 0, so it would hide a quantity of e.g. 0.5? No — 0.5 > 0 passes. The only divergence is q <= 0 (Android hides, iOS shows). Practically harmless but a behavioral mismatch.
  - **Fix:** Drop the `&& it > 0` to exactly match iOS (show whenever quantity != 1.0), or accept as intentional guard.
- ⚪ **[copy-l10n] groupReceipts.eyebrow / groupSettlements.perPerson copy shortened on Android**
  - iOS: `L10n: groupReceipts.eyebrow PL "PARAGONY GRUPY"/EN "GROUP RECEIPTS"; groupSettlements.perPerson PL "BILANS NA OSOBĘ"/EN "PER PERSON BALANCE"` → Android: `L10n.kt: groupReceipts.eyebrow PL "// PARAGONY"/EN "// RECEIPTS"; groupSettlements.perPerson PL "// SALDA"/EN "// PER PERSON"`
  - Several eyebrow strings are abbreviated on Android vs iOS: receipts eyebrow 'GROUP RECEIPTS'→'RECEIPTS' / 'PARAGONY GRUPY'→'PARAGONY'; per-person 'PER PERSON BALANCE'→'PER PERSON' / 'BILANS NA OSOBĘ'→'SALDA'. (paidShare also differs slightly: iOS 'Paid %@ · Share %@' vs Android 'Paid %@ · share %@' — capitalization of 'Share'.)
  - **Fix:** Align L10n.kt eyebrow/paidShare values to the iOS wording (GROUP RECEIPTS / PER PERSON BALANCE / 'Share' capitalized, and the Polish equivalents).
- ⚪ **[visual] Recent Activity card omits group emoji size/structure parity — acceptable; but disclosure background border alpha matches**
  - iOS: `GroupsListView.swift:513-538 (recentActivityCard: emoji 18 + name semibold(12) + balance monoBold(13))` → Android: `GroupsListScreen.kt:523-552 (matches: emoji regular(18), semibold(12), monoBold(13))`
  - RecentActivity disclosure and cards are a close match (background surface@0.5, border@0.3, emoji+name+balance). One subtle gap: iOS recentActivityCard balance uses the same +/− sign logic and colors which Android mirrors. No actionable divergence found here — included only to confirm parity.
  - **Fix:** No change required (matches).

## Challenges — parity ~66% (19 findings)

_Android Challenges reproduces the overall structure of the iOS screen (header + KPI strip + active cards with emoji/type/progress/days-left + collapsible completed section + create sheet + FAB) and the Challenge/ChallengeCreate models and i18n key set are present. However parity is moderate: there is a real KPI bug (Android sums currentProgress across ALL challenges, inflating "Saved", while iOS deliberately sums only active+completed), the create sheet uses raw text fields for dates instead of iOS's native graphical DatePicker (with no end>=start constraint and format-leaking labels), pull-to-refresh is missing, the loading state uses a single spinner instead of the iOS skeleton list, and the empty state drops the trophy icon and "New challenge" action button while double-nesting cards. On top of these, there is a large batch of copy/L10n mismatches (Android translations diverge from iOS for ~13 EN and ~11 PL keys, and 5 iOS keys are missing entirely) plus several visual deltas (date shows year, KPI tiles use a smaller goals StatTile, active-card vertical spacing is md vs xs, progress-bar height/radius differ, emoji/FAB glyph sizes differ). No crash risks; models and helpers resolve correctly._

- 🟠 **[bug] "Saved" KPI sums all challenges instead of active+completed (iOS bug already fixed)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Challenges/ChallengesView.swift:31-34` → Android: `android/.../features/challenges/ChallengesScreen.kt:128`
  - iOS computes totalSaved = (activeChallenges + completedChallenges).reduce(...) and the inline comment explicitly notes that summing across ALL challenges double-counts archived/inactive ones and inflates the KPI. Android does challenges.sumOf { it.currentProgress?.toDouble() ?: 0.0 } over the full list, so inactive/archived challenges inflate the Saved tile.
  - **Fix:** Change line 128 to sum only active + completed: val totalSaved = (active + completed).sumOf { it.currentProgress?.toDouble() ?: 0.0 } (compute after active/completed are derived).
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Date pickers replaced by raw ISO text fields in create sheet**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Challenges/ChallengesView.swift:408-422` → Android: `android/.../features/challenges/ChallengeCreateSheet.kt:139-150`
  - iOS uses native graphical DatePicker for start and end dates (labelsHidden), with the end picker constrained to startDate... so the user cannot pick an end before start. Android renders two NBTextFields where the user must hand-type 'YYYY-MM-DD' (placeholders '2026-01-01'/'2026-12-31'), with no calendar UI and no start<=end constraint.
  - **Fix:** Replace the startDate/endDate NBTextFields with a Compose DatePicker (e.g. Material3 DatePickerDialog triggered by a tappable field) storing LocalDate; enforce endDate >= startDate as iOS does; keep ISO serialization on submit.
- 🟡 **[behavior] Pull-to-refresh missing on Challenges list**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Challenges/ChallengesView.swift:79-83` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS attaches .refreshable to the ScrollView (light haptic, force refresh via store.awaitChallenges(force:true), success haptic on no error). Android's LazyColumn has no pull-to-refresh; the only way to reload is the error-card retry.
  - **Fix:** Wrap the LazyColumn in a PullToRefreshBox (Material3 pullRefresh) that calls vm.load() on refresh.
- 🟡 **[behavior] Loading state uses single spinner card instead of skeleton list**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Challenges/ChallengesView.swift:48-49` → Android: `android/.../features/challenges/ChallengesScreen.kt:110`
  - iOS shows NBSkeletonList(rows: 4) (4 shimmer rows) while loading. Android shows NBLoadingCard() (one row: small spinner + 'common.loading' text). NBSkeletonList already exists in Android NBComponents.kt:673 but is not used here.
  - **Fix:** Replace NBLoadingCard() in the Loading branch with NBSkeletonList(rows = 4).
- 🟡 **[visual] Empty state missing trophy icon, missing action button, and double-wrapped in NBCard**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Challenges/ChallengesView.swift:52-58` → Android: `android/.../features/challenges/ChallengesScreen.kt:117-124`
  - iOS NBEmptyState passes systemImage 'trophy.fill' AND an action (label challenges.new -> showCreate=true). Android calls NBEmptyState with only title+subtitle (no icon param, no actionLabel/onAction) so the icon badge and primary CTA are absent. It also wraps NBEmptyState in an outer NBCard, but NBEmptyState already renders its own nbCard (radius lg, shadow md), producing a card-inside-a-card with the wrong radius.
  - **Fix:** Remove the surrounding NBCard wrapper and call NBEmptyState(title=..., subtitle=..., icon=Icons.Filled.EmojiEvents (or a trophy vector), actionLabel=locale.t("challenges.new"), onAction={ showCreate = true }).
- 🟡 **[visual] Date range on active card formatted with year (Fmt.date) instead of day+month (Fmt.dayMonth)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Challenges/ChallengesView.swift:207` → Android: `android/.../features/challenges/ChallengesScreen.kt:251-253`
  - iOS prints the start–end range with Fmt.dayMonth (template 'dMMM', e.g. '5 Jun – 5 Jul', NO year). Android uses Fmt.date which formats 'd MMM yyyy' (e.g. '5 Jun 2026 – 5 Jul 2026'), showing the year and a longer string.
  - **Fix:** Add a Fmt.dayMonth(iso) helper (template 'd MMM', no year) in Formatters.kt and use it for the start/end range instead of Fmt.date.
- 🟡 **[visual] KPI strip uses goals StatTile (value 16, no label tracking) instead of NBStatTile (value 22)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Challenges/ChallengesView.swift:124-133` → Android: `android/.../features/challenges/ChallengesScreen.kt:131-135`
  - iOS KPI strip uses NBStatTile (value font bold 22, label mono 10 with letter-spacing, vertical spacing Spacing.xs). Android imports StatTile from features/goals (GoalsListScreen.kt:261) which renders value at bold 16 with maxLines=1, label mono 10 with NO letter-spacing, vertical spacing 2.dp. The Saved/Active/Completed tiles therefore look smaller and tighter than iOS.
  - **Fix:** Use the shared NBStatTile (core/ui/NBComponents.kt:344) for the three KPI tiles instead of goals' StatTile.
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] EN copy mismatches across many challenge keys**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n+EN.swift:1033-1057` → Android: `android/.../core/L10n.kt:1966-1992`
  - Android EN values diverge from iOS source of truth: headerTitle 'Financial challenges' vs iOS 'Spending challenges'; statSaved 'Progress' vs 'Saved'; dayLeft 'day' vs 'day left'; daysLeft 'days' vs 'days left'; emptyTitle 'No challenges' vs 'No challenges yet'; emptySub 'Start a challenge to save faster.' vs 'Start a challenge and track progress automatically as you spend.'; createFailed 'Couldn\'t create challenge' vs 'Create failed'; namePh 'e.g. 30 days no coffee' vs 'e.g. No-coffee week'; typeNoSpend 'No spend' vs 'No-spend'; categoryLabel 'Category' vs 'Category (optional)'; targetLabel 'Target amount' vs 'Target amount (optional)'; startDate 'Start (YYYY-MM-DD)' vs 'Start date'; endDate 'End (YYYY-MM-DD)' vs 'End date'.
  - **Fix:** Update the EN values in L10n.kt to exactly match iOS L10n+EN.swift for all listed keys.
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] PL copy mismatches across many challenge keys**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:1063-1091` → Android: `android/.../core/L10n.kt:598-625`
  - Android PL values diverge from iOS: headerTitle 'Wyzwania finansowe' vs iOS 'Wyzwania oszczędnościowe'; statSaved 'Postęp' vs 'Zebrano'; typeSavings 'Oszczędzanie' vs 'Oszczędności'; typeStreak 'Seria' vs 'Passa'; emptySub 'Rozpocznij wyzwanie, by oszczędzać szybciej.' vs 'Rozpocznij wyzwanie i automatycznie śledź postęp podczas wydawania.'; createFailed 'Nie udało się utworzyć wyzwania' vs 'Tworzenie nieudane'; namePh 'np. 30 dni bez kawy' vs 'np. Tydzień bez kawy'; categoryLabel 'Kategoria' vs 'Kategoria (opcjonalnie)'; targetLabel 'Cel kwotowy' vs 'Kwota docelowa (opcjonalnie)'; startDate 'Start (RRRR-MM-DD)' vs 'Data początku'; endDate 'Koniec (RRRR-MM-DD)' vs 'Data końca'.
  - **Fix:** Update the PL values in L10n.kt to exactly match iOS L10n.swift for all listed keys.
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] Missing L10n keys: categoryPlaceholder (hardcoded 'Food'), newHint, saveHint, expandHint, collapseHint**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n+EN.swift:1054,1058-1061` → Android: `android/.../features/challenges/ChallengeCreateSheet.kt:130`
  - iOS defines challenges.categoryPlaceholder ('e.g. Food' / 'np. Jedzenie'), challenges.newHint, challenges.saveHint, challenges.expandHint, challenges.collapseHint. Android L10n.kt has none of these. The create sheet hardcodes the category placeholder as the literal English 'Food' (placeholder = "Food") instead of using a localized key, so it stays English in PL. The four *Hint keys back iOS accessibility hints (FAB, save, expand/collapse completed) which Android does not provide.
  - **Fix:** Add challenges.categoryPlaceholder, challenges.newHint, challenges.saveHint, challenges.expandHint, challenges.collapseHint to both PL and EN maps in L10n.kt with iOS values; replace placeholder = "Food" with locale.t("challenges.categoryPlaceholder"); add contentDescription/semantics for FAB, save, and the completed disclosure toggle.
- ⚪ **[visual] Active challenge cards spaced by md (16) instead of xs (8)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Challenges/ChallengesView.swift:63` → Android: `android/.../features/challenges/ChallengesScreen.kt:97,146`
  - iOS wraps active cards in LazyVStack(spacing: Theme.Spacing.xs) so cards are 8dp apart. Android emits each active card as a separate LazyColumn item, and the LazyColumn verticalArrangement is spacedBy(Spacing.md)=16dp, so active cards are 16dp apart (and equally spaced from the section header), unlike iOS's tighter 8dp grouping.
  - **Fix:** Either group the active cards in a single item{} containing a Column(verticalArrangement = spacedBy(Spacing.xs)) or reduce inter-card spacing to xs to match iOS grouping.
- ⚪ **[visual] Progress bar height/radius differ from iOS (8/4 vs 10/3)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Dashboard/DashboardView.swift:755-776` → Android: `android/.../features/goals/GoalsListScreen.kt:355-372`
  - iOS NBProgressBar is height 10 with corner radius 3 and a hairline border. Android Challenges reuses goals' ProgressBar (height 8, radius 4, border). The bar is 2px shorter and rounder than iOS. (Note the shared NBProgressBar in Android NBComponents.kt:784 is even further off at height 6 with no border.)
  - **Fix:** Adjust the progress bar used here to height 10.dp, RoundedCornerShape(3.dp), keep the widthThin border, to match iOS NBProgressBar.
- ⚪ **[visual] Active-card emoji glyph size 24 instead of 28**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Challenges/ChallengesView.swift:157-159` → Android: `android/.../features/challenges/ChallengesScreen.kt:220`
  - iOS renders the active card emoji at .font(.system(size: 28)) inside the 44x44 muted tile. Android uses SolvioFonts.bold(24), so the emoji is visibly smaller within the same tile.
  - **Fix:** Use SolvioFonts.bold(28) (or a 28sp text style) for the active card emoji.
- ⚪ **[visual] FAB plus glyph size 28 instead of 22**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Challenges/ChallengesView.swift:90-91` → Android: `android/.../features/challenges/ChallengesScreen.kt:179`
  - iOS FAB '+' is Image(systemName: 'plus').font(.system(size: 22, weight: .bold)). Android draws Text('+') at SolvioFonts.bold(28), so the plus glyph is larger than iOS. (iOS also applies Theme.Shadow.md to the FAB; verify Android FAB has the same hard offset shadow — current Android FAB uses plain background+border without nbShadow.)
  - **Fix:** Use a 22sp bold glyph (or an Add icon at ~22dp) for the FAB and apply the md hard-offset shadow to match iOS.
- ⚪ **[visual] Create-sheet emoji tile glyph size 20 instead of 22; grid is fixed 8-wide (overflow risk)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Challenges/ChallengesView.swift:351-359` → Android: `android/.../features/challenges/ChallengeCreateSheet.kt:81-98`
  - iOS lays emoji choices in a LazyVGrid adaptive(minimum: 52) so they reflow across rows, glyph size 22 inside 44x44 tiles. Android uses chunked(8) -> all 8 emojis forced into a single non-wrapping Row of 44dp tiles (8*44 + 7*6 ≈ 394dp), which can overflow on narrow screens, and the glyph is SolvioFonts.bold(20) (smaller than iOS 22).
  - **Fix:** Use a wrapping/adaptive grid (e.g. FlowRow or LazyVGrid with adaptive min ~52dp) and set the emoji glyph to 22sp.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Emoji choice list uses plain star ⭐ vs iOS ⭐️ (variation selector)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Challenges/ChallengesView.swift:331` → Android: `android/.../features/challenges/ChallengeCreateSheet.kt:51`
  - iOS emojiChoices contains '⭐️' (U+2B50 + U+FE0F emoji variation selector); Android uses '⭐' (U+2B50 without selector). Selection comparison is self-consistent on each platform, but the value persisted to the backend differs between the two clients for the star choice.
  - **Fix:** Use '⭐️' in the Android emojiChoices list to match iOS exactly.
- ⚪ **[visual] Create sheet chrome: bottom-sheet inline title + bottom buttons vs iOS nav title + toolbar Cancel/Save**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Challenges/ChallengesView.swift:342-461` → Android: `android/.../features/challenges/ChallengeCreateSheet.kt:59-178`
  - iOS presents the create form in a NavigationStack with an inline navigationTitle ('New challenge'), a drag indicator, [.medium,.large] detents, Cancel in the leading toolbar and Save in the trailing toolbar. Android uses a ModalBottomSheet (skipPartiallyExpanded=true so no medium detent) with a left-aligned section-title Text and a Cancel/Save button Row pinned at the bottom of the scroll content. Section spacing also differs: iOS form VStack spacing is Spacing.md; Android column spacing is Spacing.sm.
  - **Fix:** Acceptable platform idiom, but align spacing (use Spacing.md between form sections) and consider allowing a partially-expanded detent; ensure the title styling matches iOS inline nav title.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Default end date is 1 calendar month vs iOS fixed 30 days**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Challenges/ChallengesView.swift:329` → Android: `android/.../features/challenges/ChallengeCreateSheet.kt:49`
  - iOS default endDate = now + 30 days (addingTimeInterval(60*60*24*30)). Android default endDate = LocalDate.now().plusMonths(1) (28-31 days depending on month). The prefilled end date can differ by up to a few days.
  - **Fix:** Use LocalDate.now().plusDays(30) to match iOS exactly.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Haptic feedback absent on create/save/toggle/FAB**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Challenges/ChallengesView.swift:87,246,432,438` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS fires haptics: medium impact on FAB tap and on Save, selection on emoji/type pick and on completed-section toggle and Cancel, light impact + success on pull-to-refresh. Android (entire app — no Haptics utility exists) provides none of these. Listed once here as a Challenges-area instance of a global gap.
  - **Fix:** Introduce a shared haptics helper (View.performHapticFeedback / HapticFeedback) and fire it on FAB tap, Save, emoji/type selection, and completed toggle to mirror iOS.

## Auth/Login — parity ~38% (19 findings)

_The Android Auth area is a substantial downgrade from iOS. iOS ships TWO screens — a 3-page WelcomeOnboarding carousel (shown once per install before login, with swipeable pages, animated capsule page indicator, Next/Get-started CTA, Skip button, haptics, persisted via UserDefaults `solvio.seenWelcome`) and a richly detailed LoginView (44x44 bordered+shadowed "S" logo tile with spring pop-in, eyebrow/title/subtitle, email field inside a neobrutalism card with hard shadow, live email-regex validation with inline error, a continue button that is DISABLED until the email is valid plus a "securely signing you in" progress hint, a demo button with its OWN spinner, a privacy footer block, and haptics on every action). Android implements ONLY a flat login screen: plain text brand (no logo tile), no card wrapper, no shadow on the field, no email validation (only `isBlank`), a single shared `loading` flag that disables BOTH buttons and only animates the primary one, no signing-in hint, no haptics, no logo animation. The entire WelcomeOnboarding flow is absent on Android — RootScreen has no `seenWelcome` branch and ALL `welcome.*` L10n keys are missing. Several other login hint L10n keys are also missing, the page title key and subtitle copy diverge, and the email field lacks email-specific capitalization/autocorrect handling. Visual structure (no card, no logo, no inline validation) and the missing onboarding make this far from pixel/behavior parity._

- 🔴 **[missing-feature] WelcomeOnboarding 3-page carousel is entirely missing on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Auth/WelcomeOnboardingView.swift:11-186; native-ios/Solvio/Features/Root/RootView.swift:17-19` → Android: `absent (android/.../features/auth has no Welcome screen; features/root/RootScreen.kt:37-41 has no seenWelcome branch)`
  - iOS shows a swipeable 3-page value-prop carousel to first-time users (doc.text.viewfinder / person.2.fill / target icons with tinted glow circles, title+body per page, animated capsule page indicator, Next→Get-started CTA, top-right Skip, haptics on swipe/tap, persisted once via UserDefaults solvio.seenWelcome). Android RootScreen goes straight splash→login→main; there is no onboarding screen, no composable, and no first-launch persistence flag at all.
  - **Fix:** Create features/auth/WelcomeOnboardingScreen.kt using a HorizontalPager (3 pages, icons + tint backgrounds matching success/info/warning), a custom animated pill page indicator (active 24x8, inactive 8x8), NBPrimaryButton showing Next vs Get-started on last page, a Skip text button top-right, and persist a `seenWelcome` boolean in DataStore/SharedPreferences (key e.g. solvio.seenWelcome). Add the branch in RootScreen.kt: when user==null && !seenWelcome show onboarding, else LoginScreen().
- 🟠 **[copy-l10n] All welcome.* localization keys missing from Android L10n**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Auth/WelcomeOnboardingView.swift:25-49,57,117,125,149,171-177` → Android: `absent (android/.../core/L10n.kt has 0 occurrences of welcome.skip, welcome.skipHint, welcome.swipeHint, welcome.pageOfFmt, welcome.getStarted, welcome.next, welcome.getStartedHint, welcome.nextHint, welcome.page1..3.title/body)`
  - iOS uses 16 welcome.* keys (page1/2/3 title+body, skip, skipHint, swipeHint, pageOfFmt, getStarted, next, getStartedHint, nextHint). None exist in Android L10n.kt for either PL or EN.
  - **Fix:** Add all welcome.* keys to both the PL and EN maps in core/L10n.kt with values mirroring native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift and L10n+EN.swift. pageOfFmt is a format string ('Strona %d z %d' / 'Page %d of %d').
- 🟠 **[visual] Login logo tile (bordered, shadowed, animated 'S' badge) missing on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Auth/LoginView.swift:20-48` → Android: `android/.../features/auth/LoginScreen.kt:50-53`
  - iOS renders a 44x44 RoundedRectangle (Radius.sm) filled with Theme.foreground, a centered 'S' in AppFont.black(20) in Theme.background, a 2px border, an nbShadow(Shadow.sm), and a spring scale/opacity pop-in on appear, with the brand text 'Solvio' in black(22) beside it. Android renders only a plain Text(login.brand) in black(40) with no logo tile, no border, no shadow, no animation, and the brand sits alone on its own line.
  - **Fix:** Add a Row with a 44x44 Box (clip Radius.sm, background palette.foreground, 2px palette.border, nbShadowOnly Shadow.sm) containing centered Text('S', SolvioFonts.black(20), color=palette.background), followed by Text(login.brand, SolvioFonts.black(22)). Optionally add an entrance scale/alpha animation via animateFloatAsState on first composition.
- 🟠 **[visual] Email form is not wrapped in a neobrutalism card**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Auth/LoginView.swift:64-160 (.padding(Theme.Spacing.md).nbCard(radius: Theme.Radius.lg, shadow: Theme.Shadow.lg))` → Android: `android/.../features/auth/LoginScreen.kt:65-110 (no card; controls placed directly in the page Column)`
  - iOS groups the email label/field, continue button, signing-in hint, and demo button inside a single nbCard with Radius.lg corners and Shadow.lg hard offset shadow, padded Spacing.md. Android places these controls bare in the outer scroll Column with no card container, so the whole signature card visual (border + lg shadow + grouped padding) is lost.
  - **Fix:** Wrap the email label/NBTextField, NBPrimaryButton, signing-in hint, and NBSecondaryButton in an NBCard(radius=Radius.lg, shadow=Shadow.lg) with internal padding Spacing.md, matching iOS.
- 🟠 **[behavior] Continue button not disabled on invalid/empty email (iOS gates on regex validity)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Auth/LoginView.swift:118-119,188-195 (.disabled(isSubmitting || !isValidEmail))` → Android: `android/.../features/auth/LoginScreen.kt:73-96 (NBPrimaryButton has no enabled gating; click handler only checks !email.isBlank() && !loading)`
  - iOS disables the Continue button whenever the email fails an RFC-5322-simplified regex (or while submitting), so the CTA is visibly inert until a valid address is entered. Android always renders the button enabled and only blocks the action on isBlank inside onClick — a single space or 'foo@bar' will pass isBlank and attempt submit.
  - **Fix:** Add an isValidEmail computed value using the same regex (^[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+@[A-Za-z0-9.-]+\.[A-Za-z]{2,}$, case-insensitive, trimmed) and pass enabled = isValidEmail && !loading to NBPrimaryButton; keep the guard in onClick too.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Inline email validation error message missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Auth/LoginView.swift:90-108,199-202 (emailErrorVisible when trimmed.count>=4 && !isValidEmail; red exclamationmark.circle.fill + validation.emailInvalid; field border turns destructive)` → Android: `absent (LoginScreen.kt:65-71 uses plain NBTextField, no error state, no destructive border)`
  - iOS shows an inline destructive-colored error row (icon + 'validation.emailInvalid' text) once the user has typed 4+ chars without a valid email, and turns the email field's border red. Android's NBTextField has no error parameter and the login screen shows no validation feedback whatsoever.
  - **Fix:** Add an isError/errorText parameter to NBTextField (red border via OutlinedTextFieldDefaults error colors) and, in LoginScreen, render a destructive Row (Icons.Filled.Error + Text(locale.t('validation.emailInvalid'))) when trimmed.length>=4 && !isValidEmail; set the field's error border accordingly.
- 🟠 **[behavior] Demo button shares the single loading flag and shows no independent spinner**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Auth/LoginView.swift:10,142-152,224-241 (separate isDemoLoading; demo button shows its OWN ProgressView tinted foreground; only the demo button is disabled while demo runs)` → Android: `android/.../features/auth/LoginScreen.kt:37,73-110 (one `loading` flag drives both buttons; NBSecondaryButton has no loading param and shows no spinner)`
  - iOS tracks isSubmitting and isDemoLoading separately: pressing Continue spins only the primary button; pressing Try Demo spins only the secondary button (foreground-tinted ProgressView) and leaves Continue interactive. Android uses one `loading` boolean for both, so a demo tap disables/greys the primary button and the demo button itself shows no progress indicator at all.
  - **Fix:** Split into two states (submitting, demoLoading). Gate the primary button on submitting and the demo button on demoLoading; add a `loading` param to NBSecondaryButton (foreground-tinted CircularProgressIndicator) and pass demoLoading to it.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] 'Securely signing you in' progress hint missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Auth/LoginView.swift:126-140 (lock.shield icon + login.signingInHint shown while isSubmitting)` → Android: `absent (LoginScreen.kt shows no hint; button label just swaps to login.signingIn)`
  - iOS displays a muted hint row (lock.shield icon + 'login.signingInHint') beneath the Continue button while the network call runs, fading in via opacity transition. Android only swaps the button label to login.signingIn and shows the inline spinner.
  - **Fix:** While submitting, render a muted Row (Icons.Outlined.Lock + Text(locale.t('login.signingInHint'), caption, mutedForeground)) under the primary button with a fade-in (AnimatedVisibility). Requires adding the login.signingInHint key (see separate finding).
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] Missing login hint L10n keys (emailHint, continueHint, signingInHint, tryDemoHint)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Auth/LoginView.swift:88,121,132,152` → Android: `absent (core/L10n.kt has 0 occurrences of login.emailHint, login.continueHint, login.signingInHint, login.tryDemoHint)`
  - iOS references login.emailHint, login.continueHint, login.signingInHint and login.tryDemoHint (accessibility hints + the signing-in hint text). None exist in Android L10n.kt for PL or EN.
  - **Fix:** Add login.emailHint, login.continueHint, login.signingInHint, login.tryDemoHint to both PL and EN maps in core/L10n.kt mirroring the iOS values; wire them into contentDescription/semantics and the signing-in hint Text.
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] Page title uses login.welcomeBack with diverging PL copy instead of login.title**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Auth/LoginView.swift:52 (locale.t("login.title")); native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:1451 ("login.title": "Witaj z powrotem")` → Android: `android/.../features/auth/LoginScreen.kt:59 (locale.t("login.welcomeBack")); core/L10n.kt:50 ("login.welcomeBack" to "Witaj ponownie")`
  - iOS renders the page title from login.title = 'Witaj z powrotem' (PL). Android renders login.welcomeBack = 'Witaj ponownie' (PL) — different wording for the same heading. (login.title also exists in Android L10n as 'Witaj z powrotem' but is unused here.)
  - **Fix:** Change LoginScreen to use locale.t("login.title") for the heading so the PL copy matches iOS ('Witaj z powrotem'); or align login.welcomeBack's value to the iOS string. Prefer reusing login.title to avoid a duplicate key.
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] Subtitle copy diverges from iOS for the shared login.subtitle key**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:1453 ("login.subtitle": "Wpisz email, aby kontynuować. Stworzymy konto, jeśli to Twój pierwszy raz."); L10n+EN.swift:1415 ("Enter your email to continue. We'll create an account if this is your first time.")` → Android: `core/L10n.kt:51 ("login.subtitle" to "Wpisz e-mail, by zalogować się do aplikacji."); :1426 ("Enter your email to sign in.")`
  - Same key login.subtitle but Android's value (PL: 'Wpisz e-mail, by zalogować się do aplikacji.', EN: 'Enter your email to sign in.') drops iOS's 'we'll create an account if this is your first time' messaging, which communicates the account-creation-on-first-login behavior.
  - **Fix:** Update login.subtitle in both PL and EN maps in core/L10n.kt to match the iOS strings (include the first-time account-creation sentence).
- 🟡 **[visual] Email field lacks neobrutalism shadow and uses 44dp vs iOS 48pt with Shadow.sm**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Auth/LoginView.swift:78-86 (height 48, background Theme.card, Radius.md, 2px Theme.Border.width border, nbShadow Shadow.sm)` → Android: `android/.../features/auth/LoginScreen.kt:65-71 via core/ui/NBComponents.kt:443-461 (OutlinedTextField, heightIn min 44dp, hairline Border.widthThin border, NO shadow)`
  - The iOS login email field is a bespoke 48pt-tall field with a full 2px border and a hard nbShadow(Shadow.sm). Android reuses the generic NBTextField which is 44dp tall, has only a hairline (widthThin) unfocused border, and renders no shadow — so the field looks visibly flatter and thinner-bordered than iOS.
  - **Fix:** Either give the login NBTextField a Shadow.sm hard shadow and full Border.width 2px border at 48dp height to match iOS, or add variant parameters to NBTextField; ensure focused/unfocused borders are 2px and add nbShadowOnly(Shadow.sm).
- 🟡 **[behavior] Email field missing email-specific autocapitalization/autocorrect settings**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Auth/LoginView.swift:71-74 (textContentType .emailAddress, keyboardType .emailAddress, autocapitalization .never, autocorrectionDisabled true)` → Android: `android/.../features/auth/LoginScreen.kt:65-71 + core/ui/NBComponents.kt:447 (only KeyboardType.Email set; no capitalization=None, no autoCorrect=false)`
  - iOS forces no autocapitalization and disables autocorrect for the email field. Android's NBTextField KeyboardOptions only sets keyboardType=Email; capitalization defaults (often Sentences) and autoCorrect defaults to on, so the IME may capitalize/autocorrect the address.
  - **Fix:** In NBTextField (or a login override), set KeyboardOptions(keyboardType = Email, capitalization = KeyboardCapitalization.None, autoCorrect = false). Consider only doing this when keyboardType==Email.
- 🟡 **[bug] Email is trimmed but not lowercased before sign-in**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Auth/LoginView.swift:211 (let trimmed = email.trimmingCharacters(in: .whitespaces).lowercased())` → Android: `android/.../features/auth/LoginScreen.kt:67,80 (onChange trims; signIn(email) sends as-typed casing)`
  - iOS lowercases the trimmed email before calling session.login so the deterministic userId hash (sha256(email)) is case-insensitive. Android only trims (in onChange) and passes the original casing to session.signIn, so 'Foo@Bar.com' and 'foo@bar.com' would hash to different userIds, splitting a user's data across sessions.
  - **Fix:** Lowercase before submitting: session.signIn(email.trim().lowercase()). Ideally do the trim+lowercase at submit time rather than mutating state in onChange (mutating in onChange also blocks the user from typing spaces mid-edit).
- ⚪ **[behavior] No haptic feedback on login/demo actions or onboarding**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Auth/LoginView.swift:209,216,229,235 + WelcomeOnboardingView.swift:58,79,165-166 (Haptics.impact/.success/.error/.selection)` → Android: `absent (LoginScreen.kt has no haptic calls)`
  - iOS fires medium impact on Continue, light impact on Demo, success/error notification haptics on result, and selection haptics throughout onboarding. Android performs no haptic feedback for any auth action.
  - **Fix:** Use LocalHapticFeedback / a Haptics helper: medium impact on Continue press, light impact on Demo press, and success/error haptics on the coroutine result; add selection haptics in the onboarding screen when built.
- ⚪ **[visual] Brand-only header omits eyebrow/title ordering and shows tagline instead of subtitle structure**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Auth/LoginView.swift:39-60 (logo row, then NBEyebrow signInEyebrow, then title, then subtitle — all in one leading VStack)` → Android: `android/.../features/auth/LoginScreen.kt:50-61 (brand+splash.tagline in one block, then a separate block with eyebrow+welcomeBack+subtitle)`
  - iOS keeps the brand row and the eyebrow/title/subtitle in a single tightly-spaced (Spacing.md) heading group with the logo tile first. Android renders the brand in one Column with splash.tagline (a tagline string not used in the iOS login heading), then a separate spaced block, producing different vertical grouping/spacing and an extra tagline line that iOS does not show on login.
  - **Fix:** Restructure to mirror iOS: logo tile row + brand, then NBEyebrow(login.signInEyebrow), Text(login.title), Text(login.subtitle) in one VStack with Spacing.md; drop the splash.tagline line from the login heading (tagline belongs to the splash screen only).
- ⚪ **[visual] Android splash differs from iOS (bare spinner vs branded SplashLogo)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Root/RootView.swift:31-52 (SplashView: 96x96 SplashLogo with 22 radius, 2px border, Shadow.lg, brand black(32), tagline eyebrow)` → Android: `android/.../features/root/RootScreen.kt:44-55 (SplashScreen: just a centered CircularProgressIndicator on the background)`
  - While restoring the session, iOS shows a branded splash (logo image, brand name, tagline). Android shows only a centered spinner. This is the gate that precedes login and is part of the auth/launch experience.
  - **Fix:** Build a branded SplashScreen: 96dp logo (rounded 22, 2px border, Shadow.lg), Text(login.brand, black(32)), NBEyebrow(splash.tagline), matching iOS RootView.SplashView.
- ⚪ **[behavior] RootScreen splash gating relies on a fixed 450ms delay rather than an isRestoring flag**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Root/RootView.swift:13-15 (if session.isRestoring { SplashView } — driven by actual restore state)` → Android: `android/.../features/root/RootScreen.kt:27-41 (var bootDone via delay(450); splash shown only while !bootDone && user==null)`
  - iOS shows the splash precisely while the session restore is in flight (session.isRestoring). Android approximates this with a hardcoded 450ms delay and a null-user check, so on slow restores the login screen can flash before the cookie check completes, and on fast restores the splash is held an arbitrary minimum.
  - **Fix:** Expose an isRestoring StateFlow from SessionStore (set true during restore(), false when done) and gate SplashScreen on it instead of the delay(450) heuristic, matching iOS.
- ⚪ **[copy-l10n] Eyebrow strings stored with '// ' prefix then stripped in LoginScreen (inconsistent L10n)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:1461 ("login.signInEyebrow": "Logowanie" — raw, NBEyebrow adds '// ' + uppercases); native-ios/Solvio/Core/Theme/Theme.swift:321-330` → Android: `core/L10n.kt:49,57 ("login.signInEyebrow" to "// LOGOWANIE"); features/auth/LoginScreen.kt:58,115 (.removePrefix("// ")); core/ui/NBComponents.kt:58-71 (NBEyebrow re-adds '// ' + uppercases)`
  - iOS stores eyebrow values raw ('Logowanie'/'Sign in') and NBEyebrow formats them. Android stores them pre-formatted ('// LOGOWANIE') and the LoginScreen calls .removePrefix('// ') before passing to NBEyebrow, which then re-adds '// ' and re-uppercases. The output happens to match, but the storage convention diverges from iOS and the removePrefix hack is fragile (any consumer that forgets it gets a doubled '// // '). 
  - **Fix:** Store eyebrow L10n values raw (e.g. login.signInEyebrow to 'Logowanie' / 'Sign in', login.privacyEyebrow to 'Prywatność' / 'Privacy') and remove the .removePrefix("// ") calls in LoginScreen, letting NBEyebrow do the formatting — matching the iOS convention.

## NearbyStores — parity ~42% (18 findings)

_The Android NearbyStores screen is a heavily reduced port of the iOS view. It renders the screen header, a brands row, store cards (name/category/distance/address/hours), and permission/empty/loading/error states. But it is missing roughly half of the iOS feature surface: the radius slider control, the All/Known-only filter, the two-stat summary bar, the per-store Navigate (maps) and Phone call action buttons, the staged progress card, pull-to-refresh, and the location-denied "Open Settings" deep link. On top of that there is a real wiring bug: iOS calls the endpoint via POST with a JSON body (lat/lng/radius/lang) while Android calls it via GET with query params and omits both the user-controlled radius and the lang param, so the request will likely fail against the production POST-only route and never localizes results. There is also a permission/threading bug in the retry path. Layout, palette and atom usage of the parts that ARE present mostly match. Overall parity is low._

- 🔴 **[missing-feature] Radius slider control is entirely missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/NearbyStores/NearbyStoresView.swift:104-128` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS renders a `controls` card with a 'Radius' label, a live value readout (e.g. '3.0 km' / '500 m'), and a Slider bound to `radius` (range 500...15000, step 500) that re-runs `search()` on every change. The radius defaults to 3000 m. Android has no radius UI at all and hardcodes radius=1500 in the repo default, so the user cannot adjust search radius.
  - **Fix:** Add a controls NBCard above the content containing: an HStack with t("nearby.radius") on the left (caption/mutedForeground) and the formatted value on the right (>=1000 -> '%.1f km', else 'N m'); a Slider/Compose Slider bound to a `radius` state (initial 3000f, range 500f..15000f, steps so step=500, tint = palette.foreground) that calls vm.fetch(lat,lng,radius) on value change (debounced). Pass radius through NearbyStoresViewModel.fetch and NearbyStoresRepo.fetch.
- 🔴 **[bug] API called with GET instead of POST (wrong HTTP method)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:474-479` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:293-298`
  - The backend route app/api/personal/nearby-stores/route.ts only exports POST. iOS calls ApiClient.post('/api/personal/nearby-stores', body{lat,lng,radius,lang}). Android calls api.get('/api/personal/nearby-stores', ...) with query params. A GET against a POST-only Next.js route returns 405, so Android nearby-stores will always error.
  - **Fix:** Change NearbyStoresRepo.fetch to POST with a serializable body matching iOS: data class NearbyStoresBody(lat, lng, radius: Int?, lang: String?); call api.post('/api/personal/nearby-stores', body, body.serializer(), NearbyStoresResponse.serializer()).
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] All-stores / Known-only filter is missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/NearbyStores/NearbyStoresView.swift:15-17,120-145` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS has two filter pill buttons inside the controls card: t("nearby.allStores") and t("nearby.knownOnly"); the active one uses foreground bg + background text, inactive uses muted bg + foreground text, both with a thin border and Radius.sm. The list is filtered via `showKnownOnly ? stores.filter { isKnown } : stores`. Android renders all stores unconditionally with no filter UI and no `showKnownOnly` state.
  - **Fix:** Add a `showKnownOnly` state and two filterButton composables (Radius.sm rounded, thin border, active=foreground bg/background text, inactive=muted bg/foreground text) using t("nearby.allStores") and t("nearby.knownOnly"). Apply `if (showKnownOnly) stores.filter { it.isKnown } else stores` before rendering the list, stat counts, and empty check. Use the existing nearby.allStores/nearby.knownOnly L10n keys.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Stats bar with two stat pills is missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/NearbyStores/NearbyStoresView.swift:149-182` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS shows a `statsBar` HStack of two pills before the brands/list: pill 1 = building.2.fill icon + filtered.count + t("nearby.stores"); pill 2 = star.fill icon + count of isKnown stores + t("nearby.knownBrands"). Each pill is an nbCard (Radius.sm, Shadow.sm) with value in bold(16) and uppercased mono(10) label. Android renders nothing of this; there is no summary of how many stores / known chains were found.
  - **Fix:** Add a statsBar Row(spacing=md) above BrandsRow with two stat pills, each a nbCard(Radius.sm, Shadow.sm) weight(1f): icon (use a Material building/storefront icon and star icon, tint=foreground, ~14dp bold) + Column(value bold(16) foreground, label mono(10).uppercase() mutedForeground). Use t("nearby.stores") and t("nearby.knownBrands").
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Per-store Navigate (open Maps) button is missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/NearbyStores/NearbyStoresView.swift:270-282,317-323` → Android: `absent`
  - Each iOS store card has a primary 'Navigate' button (t("nearby.navigate"), arrow.triangle.turn.up.right.diamond.fill icon, foreground bg / background text, Radius.sm) that opens Apple Maps with walking directions to store.lat/store.lng. Android's StoreCard ends after the address/hours rows and has no action buttons at all.
  - **Fix:** Add a Row(spacing=sm) at the bottom of StoreCard with a Navigate button (foreground bg, background text, Radius.sm, a directions/navigation icon + t("nearby.navigate")) that fires an Intent(ACTION_VIEW, Uri 'geo:lat,lng?q=lat,lng(name)' or 'google.navigation:q=lat,lng&mode=w') via ctx.startActivity. Use store.lat/store.lng/store.name.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Location-denied 'Open Settings' deep link is missing/incorrect**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/NearbyStores/NearbyStoresView.swift:344-373` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/nearbystores/NearbyStoresScreen.kt:154-177`
  - iOS denied state shows a location.slash.fill warning icon, title, description, and an 'Open Settings' button (t("nearby.openSettings")) that opens the app's system Settings. Android denied state instead shows a 'Enable location' button calling launchPermissionRequest(), and only shows the openSettings text as a tiny non-interactive caption when shouldShowRationale. Once the user permanently denies, Android has no working way to reach app settings (the request button silently no-ops), and there is no warning icon.
  - **Fix:** When permission is permanently denied (Denied && !shouldShowRationale), render an NBPrimaryButton labeled t("nearbyStores.openSettings") that starts Intent(Settings.ACTION_APPLICATION_DETAILS_SETTINGS, Uri 'package:'+packageName). Add a warning icon (location-off, tint=palette.warning, ~32dp) above the title to match iOS visual.
- 🟠 **[behavior] lang parameter not sent — results never localized**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/NearbyStores/NearbyStoresView.swift:77-83` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:293-298`
  - iOS passes lang = (locale==pl ? 'pl' : 'en') to the search call so the backend returns localized store categories/names. Android's fetch has no lang param and never sends it, so PL users will get default/English server-side localization.
  - **Fix:** Add a lang param to NearbyStoresRepo.fetch and NearbyStoresViewModel.fetch, populate it from LocalAppLocale.current.language in the screen, and include it in the POST body.
- 🟠 **[behavior] User radius never passed to the request; default differs**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/NearbyStores/NearbyStoresView.swift:11,79-82` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:293`
  - iOS initial radius is 3000 m and is user-adjustable; it is passed on every search. Android's ViewModel.fetch(lat,lng) never forwards a radius, so the repo default 1500 is always used (and there is no slider to change it). Both the default value (1500 vs 3000) and the inability to vary it differ from iOS.
  - **Fix:** Thread the radius from the slider state through ViewModel.fetch into the repo body; default to 3000 to match iOS. Re-fetch on slider change.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Per-store Phone call button is missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/NearbyStores/NearbyStoresView.swift:284-303` → Android: `absent`
  - When store.phone is non-empty iOS shows a secondary 'Phone' button (t("nearby.phone"), phone.fill icon, muted bg + foreground text + thin border, Radius.sm) that opens tel: URL (spaces stripped). Android never reads store.phone in StoreCard and renders no call button, even though the NearbyStore model has a `phone` field.
  - **Fix:** In the same action Row, when store.phone is non-blank add a secondary Phone button (muted bg, thin border, foreground text, Radius.sm, phone icon + t("nearby.phone")) that fires Intent(ACTION_DIAL, Uri.parse("tel:" + phone.replace(" ", ""))).
- 🟡 **[visual] Staged NBProgressCard replaced by generic spinner**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/NearbyStores/NearbyStoresView.swift:26-36` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/nearbystores/NearbyStoresScreen.kt:128`
  - On first load (isLoading && stores.isEmpty) iOS shows an NBProgressCard with title t("nearby.runningTitle") and 4 cycling stage strings (progress.locatingStores / crunchingData / formattingResults / almostDone) plus an estimated-time bar (estimatedSeconds:8). Android shows a plain NBLoadingCard (spinner + generic t("common.loading")). The Android NBProgressCard atom exists but is unused here.
  - **Fix:** Replace the Loading branch's NBLoadingCard() with NBProgressCard(title = t("nearby.runningTitle"), stages = listOf(t("progress.locatingStores"), t("progress.crunchingData"), t("progress.formattingResults"), t("progress.almostDone")), estimatedSeconds = 8). Ensure those L10n keys exist on Android.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Pull-to-refresh is missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/NearbyStores/NearbyStoresView.swift:58` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS attaches `.refreshable { await search() }` so the user can pull to re-run the search. Android's LazyColumn has no pull-to-refresh wrapper, so there is no manual refresh path besides the error-card retry.
  - **Fix:** Wrap the LazyColumn in a PullToRefreshBox (material3 pullToRefresh) whose onRefresh re-runs lastKnownLocation + vm.fetch. Track an isRefreshing flag from the Loading state.
- 🟡 **[crash-risk] Synchronous getLastKnownLocation on main thread in retry path (ANR/threading risk)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/NearbyStores/NearbyStoresView.swift:38,61-67` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/nearbystores/NearbyStoresScreen.kt:131,184-189`
  - viewModelRetry runs lastKnownLocation(ctx) synchronously inside the NBErrorCard onRetry click lambda (main thread) and toggles locating true->false instantly, so the 'locating' state never actually shows and the LocationManager call blocks the UI thread. iOS waits up to 30 x 300ms for a fresh location before searching; Android only ever reads cached last-known location and has no wait loop, so on a cold device lastKnownLocation is null and the screen immediately shows the noLocation empty state.
  - **Fix:** Move location retrieval into a coroutine (rememberCoroutineScope/LaunchedEffect or the ViewModel). Mirror iOS by polling for a location (or requesting a single fresh fix) for a few seconds before declaring noLocation, and keep `locating=true` for the duration. Do not call getLastKnownLocation on the main thread synchronously.
- ⚪ **[copy-l10n] i18n key namespace diverges from iOS (nearby.* vs nearbyStores.*)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:247-267,1258` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/L10n.kt:684-703`
  - iOS uses the 'nearby.*' key family (nearby.navTitle, nearby.title, nearby.subtitle, nearby.runningTitle, etc.). Android's screen uses a parallel 'nearbyStores.*' family with subtly different copy (e.g. EN subtitle 'Find shops close to you' vs iOS 'Find stores close to you'; eyebrow 'NEARBY STORES' hardcoded vs iOS deriving eyebrow from nearby.navTitle.uppercased(); noStores 'No stores in the area' vs iOS 'No stores nearby'). Both families exist in Android L10n which is redundant and risks drift. Copy is not identical to iOS.
  - **Fix:** Standardize on one key family and align the EN/PL strings exactly with iOS (shop->store, 'No stores nearby', etc.). Prefer reusing nearby.* to mirror iOS and remove the duplicate nearbyStores.* block, or update nearbyStores.* values to match iOS wording verbatim.
- ⚪ **[visual] Brands row adds a 'Known brands' header not present on iOS**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/NearbyStores/NearbyStoresView.swift:186-204` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/nearbystores/NearbyStoresScreen.kt:191-201`
  - iOS brandsRow is a single horizontally-scrolling row of brand chips with NO header label. Android's BrandsRow prepends an NBEyebrow header t("nearbyStores.knownBrands") above a wrapping FlowRow. Both the extra header and the wrap-vs-horizontal-scroll layout differ from iOS.
  - **Fix:** Remove the NBEyebrow header from BrandsRow (iOS has none) and render brands in a horizontally scrollable Row (LazyRow) of NBTag chips with spacing=xs to match iOS exactly.
- ⚪ **[visual] Brand chip shown inside store card; iOS store card has no brand tag**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/NearbyStores/NearbyStoresView.swift:216-235` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/nearbystores/NearbyStoresScreen.kt:232-234`
  - iOS store card shows name (+ checkmark.seal.fill success badge if isKnown) and category only. Android additionally renders an NBTag with store.brand when isKnown, which iOS does not do, making the card visually different.
  - **Fix:** Remove the brand NBTag from StoreCard to match iOS (iOS only shows the verified seal icon next to the name and the category text).
- ⚪ **[visual] isKnown verified badge uses a text glyph instead of a seal icon**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/NearbyStores/NearbyStoresView.swift:224-228` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/nearbystores/NearbyStoresScreen.kt:227`
  - iOS shows a checkmark.seal.fill SF Symbol (12pt, success color) for known stores. Android renders the plain text '✓' in bold(12) success color, which is a different glyph and weight than the iOS filled seal.
  - **Fix:** Use an Icon(Icons.Filled.Verified or a seal/check-circle vector, tint=palette.success, size 12-14dp) instead of the '✓' Text to match the iOS seal badge.
- ⚪ **[visual] Address/hours row icons use emoji instead of vector icons**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/NearbyStores/NearbyStoresView.swift:248-268` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/nearbystores/NearbyStoresScreen.kt:244-258`
  - iOS uses SF Symbols 'mappin' (11pt, mutedForeground) for address and 'clock' (11pt) for opening hours. Android uses emoji Text '📍' and '🕒' which render with platform emoji styling (color, baseline) inconsistent with the monochrome neobrutalist iOS look.
  - **Fix:** Replace the emoji Text with Icon(Icons.Filled.Place / Icons.Filled.Schedule, tint=palette.mutedForeground, size ~11-12dp) to match iOS monochrome icons.
- ⚪ **[visual] Background color application differs (screen vs scroll content)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/NearbyStores/NearbyStoresView.swift:50-51` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/nearbystores/NearbyStoresScreen.kt:96`
  - iOS applies Theme.background to the whole ScrollView/screen. Android applies palette.background to the LazyColumn modifier which fills the screen — acceptable — but there is no Scaffold/top app bar, whereas iOS sets navigationTitle(nearby.navTitle) inline. If the Android nav host does not supply an equivalent title bar, the screen lacks the inline nav title iOS shows.
  - **Fix:** Verify the Android navigation host renders an inline title equal to t("nearbyStores.navTitle")/'nearby.navTitle' for this destination; if not, add a top bar/title so it matches iOS's navigationBarTitleDisplayMode(.inline).

## Settings — parity ~20% (17 findings)

_The Android Settings screen (SettingsScreen.kt, 129 lines) is a drastic, skeletal reduction of the iOS SettingsView (SettingsView.swift, 1145 lines). iOS shows nine cards — Account, Appearance, Preferences (language + currency), Categories, Budgets, Merchant Rules, Export Data, About, and a Danger Zone (Sign Out + Delete Account) — plus loading/error/empty states, two editor sheets (Category, Budget) and six alerts (delete category, sign out, delete account, delete rule). Android shows only three cards: Appearance (theme), Preferences (language ONLY — no currency), and a bare Sign Out button with NO confirmation dialog. There is a separate CategoriesManagerScreen on its own route that partially covers the iOS Categories card, but Budgets, Merchant Rules, Export Data (GDPR), About (version/terms/privacy/support), the Account card (email copy), the currency picker, Delete Account (App Store/Play requirement), the saving/loading/error states, and every sheet/alert in Settings are entirely absent. The screen also reimplements its own SegmentedRow instead of using the shared NBSegmented atom (different track color, spacing, padding, radius), and uses raw NBEyebrow+Text instead of NBSectionHeader. Roughly 80% of the Settings feature is missing on Android._

- 🔴 **[missing-feature] Currency picker entirely missing**
  - iOS: `SettingsView.swift:386-423` → Android: `SettingsScreen.kt:78-91`
  - iOS Preferences card has a horizontal scrolling currency selector (PLN, USD, EUR, GBP, CHF, CZK) with a checkmark on the active code, mono-bold styling, and saves via SettingsRepo.updateSettings(currency:). Android Preferences card has ONLY the language toggle; there is no currency control at all, so users cannot change currency. The L10n key settings.currency exists but is unused.
  - **Fix:** Add a horizontal currency chip row under the language toggle: forEach listOf('PLN','USD','EUR','GBP','CHF','CZK'), each a pill (active = foreground bg / background fg + checkmark, inactive = card bg + border, Radius.sm, mono-bold 11, tracking 1). On tap call SettingsRepo.updateSettings(currency=code) and show a saving spinner + t('settings.preferencesSaved') toast. Wire vm.currency from SettingsRepo.fetch().settings.currency.
- 🔴 **[missing-feature] Delete Account (App Store/Play requirement) entirely missing**
  - iOS: `SettingsView.swift:807-847,191-198,254-272` → Android: `absent (SettingsScreen.kt); no AccountRepo; keys absent`
  - iOS Danger Zone includes account deletion (required by App Store 5.1.1(v) and Google Play data-deletion policy): description text + destructive 'Delete account' button → confirmation alert → AccountRepo.deleteAccount() then logout, with deleting spinner and done/fail toasts. Android has NO delete-account flow, no AccountRepo (grep found it only on iOS), and none of the deleteAccount L10n keys exist on Android. This is a store-compliance blocker for the Android release.
  - **Fix:** Add an AccountRepo.deleteAccount() (DELETE account endpoint) on Android, plus a Delete Account NBDestructiveButton in a Danger Zone with description, a confirm AlertDialog, deleting spinner, then session.signOut(). Add L10n keys settings.deleteAccount, settings.deleteAccountDesc, settings.deletingAccount, settings.deleteAccountConfirmTitle, settings.deleteAccountConfirmMsg, settings.deleteAccountDone, settings.deleteAccountFailed (PL+EN).
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Account card (signed-in-as email, tap-to-copy) entirely missing**
  - iOS: `SettingsView.swift:288-326` → Android: `absent (SettingsScreen.kt)`
  - iOS shows an Account card with a person icon badge, the user email (selectable), a 'cookie session' description caption, and a copy-to-clipboard button that fires a haptic + 'email copied' toast. Android shows nothing — the email only appears as the subtitle of the screen header.
  - **Fix:** Add an NBCard with NBSectionHeader(eyebrow='// '+t('settings.account'), title=t('settings.signedInAs')), an NBIconBadge(person), the email text, t('settings.cookieSessionDesc') caption, and a click handler copying email to clipboard with a t('settings.emailCopied') toast. Add the missing L10n keys settings.account, settings.signedInAs, settings.cookieSessionDesc, settings.emailCopied, settings.copyEmail to core/L10n.kt (PL+EN).
- 🟠 **[behavior] Settings does not persist language change to server**
  - iOS: `SettingsView.swift:368-383,438-465` → Android: `SettingsScreen.kt:82-90`
  - iOS, on language change, both flips local AppLocale AND calls SettingsRepo.updateSettings(language:) then session.refresh() and store invalidation, with a 'saving' spinner and 'preferencesSaved' toast. Android only calls locale.set(target) locally and never persists the language to /api/data/settings, so the choice is lost across devices/sessions and the server stays out of sync.
  - **Fix:** In the language onSelect, after locale.set(target) launch a coroutine calling SettingsRepo.updateSettings(language=code), then refresh the session and show t('settings.preferencesSaved'). Surface a saving indicator (vm.isSavingSettings) like iOS.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Budgets section + Budget editor sheet entirely missing**
  - iOS: `SettingsView.swift:546-611,99-149,1027-1144` → Android: `absent (SettingsScreen.kt)`
  - iOS has a Budgets card (section header with Add button disabled when no unbudgeted categories, empty state, list of budget rows showing category icon/name + 'Monthly' + formatted amount, tap-to-edit) and a BudgetEditorSheet (category chip selector + decimal amount field, create/edit modes, monthly-only).
