Android has none of this. SettingsRepo.upsertBudget exists in Repositories.kt but is unused from Settings; no budget UI is reachable.
  - **Fix:** Add a Budgets NBCard: header with Add button (disabled when categoriesWithoutBudget empty), NBEmptyState, list of budget rows (NBIconBadge + name + t('reports.monthly') + Fmt.amount(amount, currency)), tap opens a BudgetEditorSheet (ModalBottomSheet) with category chip selector + decimal amount NBTextField, saving via SettingsRepo.upsertBudget(period='monthly'). Add L10n keys settings.monthlyLimits, settings.noBudgets/noBudgetsTitle, settings.addBudget, settings.newBudget, settings.editBudget.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Merchant Rules section entirely missing**
  - iOS: `SettingsView.swift:615-692,199-215,218-231` → Android: `absent (SettingsScreen.kt)`
  - iOS shows a Merchant Rules card: description text, empty state, and up to 10 rule rows (building icon, capitalized vendor, mapped category icon+name, 'x times bought' count, delete button with per-row spinner) backed by MerchantRulesRepo.list()/delete(). Android Settings has nothing; MerchantRulesRepo exists in Repositories.kt but is never used by any screen.
  - **Fix:** Add a Merchant Rules NBCard with t('settings.merchantRulesDesc'), NBEmptyState, and list of merchantRules.take(10) rows (NBIconBadge building, vendor capitalized, category icon+name resolved via categories, optional count + t('settings.timesBought'), trash button). Load via MerchantRulesRepo.list(), delete via MerchantRulesRepo.delete(vendor) with a confirm dialog (settings.ruleDeleteTitle/ruleDeleteDesc with {vendor} substitution).
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Export Data (GDPR JSON download) section + share entirely missing**
  - iOS: `SettingsView.swift:696-725,233-252` → Android: `absent (SettingsScreen.kt); no ExportDataRepo in core/network/Repositories.kt`
  - iOS has an Export Data card with description, a primary 'Download JSON' button that calls ExportDataRepo.download(), writes a temp file and presents a share sheet, with exporting spinner and success/fail toasts. Android has no export UI and there is no ExportDataRepo on Android at all (grep found it only on iOS). The L10n keys settings.exportData/exportDataDesc/downloadJson/exporting/exportSuccess/exportFailed already exist on Android but are unused.
  - **Fix:** Add an ExportDataRepo (GET data export endpoint) on Android, plus an Export Data NBCard with description and an NBPrimaryButton that downloads the JSON and opens an Android share sheet (Intent.ACTION_SEND with a FileProvider URI), showing exporting state and success/error toasts.
- 🟠 **[missing-popup] Sign-out confirmation dialog missing**
  - iOS: `SettingsView.swift:183-190,811-818` → Android: `SettingsScreen.kt:93-95`
  - iOS sign-out requires a confirmation alert (settings.signOutConfirm title + settings.signOutMessage body, Cancel + destructive Sign Out) with a warning haptic before signing out. Android signs out immediately on the single button tap with no confirmation, risking accidental logout. The L10n keys settings.signOutConfirm and settings.signOutMessage exist on Android but signOutMessage is unused and there is no dialog.
  - **Fix:** Gate session.signOut() behind a confirm AlertDialog with title t('settings.signOutConfirm'), text t('settings.signOutMessage'), destructive confirm t('settings.signOut'), and Cancel. Add settings.signOutMessage value if missing.
- 🟠 **[behavior] Loading / error / empty (no-bundle) states missing**
  - iOS: `SettingsView.swift:35-60,852-903` → Android: `SettingsScreen.kt:42-97 (no VM, no fetch)`
  - iOS loads a SettingsRepo bundle and shows NBSkeletonList(rows:4) while loading and NBErrorCard(retry) on failure when no cached bundle exists; content renders only once bundle != nil, with pull-to-refresh. Android SettingsScreen never fetches any settings bundle — it reads only theme/language/user from local stores and renders immediately, so there is no loading skeleton, no error/retry state, and no pull-to-refresh.
  - **Fix:** Introduce a SettingsViewModel that fetches SettingsRepo.fetch() (categories/budgets/settings) + MerchantRulesRepo.list(); render NBSkeletonList while loading with no cache, NBErrorCard on error with retry, and wrap the screen in a pull-to-refresh container.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Categories section missing from Settings (split to separate route)**
  - iOS: `SettingsView.swift:469-542,80-98,150-182` → Android: `absent in SettingsScreen.kt; partial in features/categories/CategoriesManagerScreen.kt`
  - iOS embeds a full Categories card inside Settings: section header with an Add (+) button, empty state, and a list of category rows (icon badge, name, Default badge, edit + delete buttons) with delete-confirmation alert and a CategoryEditorSheet. Android has NO categories section in Settings; equivalent functionality lives in a separate CategoriesManagerScreen on its own nav route (features/categories). This is a structural/navigation divergence — on iOS categories are managed inline in Settings.
  - **Fix:** Either embed a Categories card in SettingsScreen mirroring iOS (header + Add button + NBEmptyState + rows with NBIconBadge/edit/delete + delete-confirm AlertDialog + CategoryEditorSheet), or confirm the standalone Categories route is the intended IA. To match iOS pixel/structure parity, embed it in Settings.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] About card (version, terms, privacy, support, copyright) entirely missing**
  - iOS: `SettingsView.swift:729-799` → Android: `absent (SettingsScreen.kt); keys absent in core/L10n.kt`
  - iOS shows an About card with app version+build row, and links to Terms, Privacy, and mailto support, plus a copyright line '© <year> Programo s.c. — solvio'. Android has none of this, and the L10n keys settings.aboutEyebrow/aboutTitle/version/terms/privacy/support do NOT exist on Android at all.
  - **Fix:** Add an About NBCard with a version row (BuildConfig.VERSION_NAME + VERSION_CODE), tappable rows opening URLs (terms/privacy via Custom Tab/Intent, support via mailto:support@solvio.app), NBDivider between rows, and a centered mono copyright line. Add L10n keys settings.aboutEyebrow, settings.aboutTitle, settings.version, settings.terms, settings.privacy, settings.support (PL+EN).
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Danger Zone visual section/separation missing**
  - iOS: `SettingsView.swift:801-847` → Android: `SettingsScreen.kt:93-95`
  - iOS isolates destructive actions in a 'Danger Zone' section (NBEyebrow header + extra top spacing) to reduce accidental taps, containing Sign Out and Delete Account as full-width destructive buttons (minHeight 48). Android has just one loose NBDestructiveButton at the bottom of the column with no Danger Zone eyebrow/section and no spacing separation.
  - **Fix:** Wrap destructive actions in a section with NBEyebrow(t('settings.dangerZone')) and top spacing, containing full-width Sign Out and Delete Account NBDestructiveButtons. Add L10n key settings.dangerZone (PL+EN).
- 🟡 **[visual] Reimplemented SegmentedRow instead of shared NBSegmented atom (visual)**
  - iOS: `Core/UI/NBComponents.swift:440-475 (NBSegmented)` → Android: `SettingsScreen.kt:99-129 (private SegmentedRow)`
  - iOS uses the shared NBSegmented atom: 4px inner spacing between pills, 4px outer padding, a Theme.muted track background, each pill rounded at Radius.sm with only the active pill filled (others transparent over the muted track), Radius.md outer with thin border, mono 11 / tracking 1 / uppercase labels. Android defines a private SegmentedRow that has NO inter-pill spacing, NO outer padding, NO muted track (inactive cells use palette.surface), clips only at the container at Radius.md, and uses 12.dp vertical padding (iOS pads 8). Result: noticeably different segmented control look, and the existing core/ui NBSegmented component is bypassed.
  - **Fix:** Delete the private SegmentedRow and use the shared NBSegmented from core/ui/NBComponents.kt for both theme and language selectors so spacing (4dp), outer padding (4dp), muted track, per-pill Radius.sm, and vertical padding (8dp) match iOS.
- ⚪ **[visual] Section headers use raw NBEyebrow+Text instead of NBSectionHeader**
  - iOS: `SettingsView.swift:332-333,363-364` → Android: `SettingsScreen.kt:57-58,79-80`
  - iOS uses NBSectionHeader(eyebrow:'// '+UPPERCASED, title:) for every card header, producing the '// EYEBROW' code-comment style plus a section title with consistent 2px spacing. Android Settings hand-rolls NBEyebrow(text=t('settings.appearance')) + a separate Text for the title, omitting the '// ' prefix/uppercasing and not reusing the shared NBSectionHeader, so eyebrow casing/prefix and spacing can drift from other screens.
  - **Fix:** Replace the manual NBEyebrow+Text pairs with NBSectionHeader(eyebrow='// '+t('settings.appearance'), title=t('settings.theme')) etc., matching iOS eyebrow formatting (the Android NBSectionHeader already strips the '// ' prefix and uses mutedForeground).
- ⚪ **[visual] Theme picker missing the Evening description placement parity check**
  - iOS: `SettingsView.swift:351-353` → Android: `SettingsScreen.kt:74-75`
  - Minor: iOS places t('settings.themeEveningDesc') as a caption under the segmented control (mutedForeground). Android does include it (line 75) but with a fixed 6.dp spacer rather than the card's spacing rhythm (iOS relies on VStack spacing.sm). Acceptable but slightly off the standard sm spacing used elsewhere.
  - **Fix:** Use SolvioTheme.Spacing.sm (consistent with iOS VStack spacing) instead of hardcoded 6.dp before the Evening description caption.
- ⚪ **[behavior] No haptic feedback on theme/language selection**
  - iOS: `SettingsView.swift:338-341,371-373` → Android: `SettingsScreen.kt:67-72,84-89`
  - iOS fires Haptics.selection() when the theme or language actually changes (only on real change). Android's SegmentedRow onSelect performs no haptic feedback at all.
  - **Fix:** Add a selection haptic (HapticFeedback / view.performHapticFeedback) when the selected theme or language changes, guarded so it only fires on an actual change like iOS.
- ⚪ **[visual] Screen content padding / spacing rhythm differs (ScrollView vs Column)**
  - iOS: `SettingsView.swift:27-57` → Android: `SettingsScreen.kt:42-49`
  - iOS uses a ScrollView with VStack spacing.md, horizontal padding md, top padding md, and a trailing Spacer(minLength: xl) for bottom inset. Android uses a Column(verticalScroll) with padding(md) all sides and spacedBy(md), but lacks the extra bottom xl spacer, so the last element (Sign Out) can sit close to the bottom nav/edge versus iOS's generous bottom breathing room.
  - **Fix:** Add a trailing Spacer(Modifier.height(SolvioTheme.Spacing.xl)) (or extra bottom contentPadding) at the end of the Column to match iOS bottom inset.

## Loyalty — parity ~58% (16 findings)

_Android's LoyaltyScreen.kt reproduces the overall wallet concept (list of cards, create sheet, barcode sheet, delete confirmation, ZXing barcode generation matching iOS's EAN13/QR/Code128 detection) and the L10n keys are present in PL/EN. However there are substantial deviations from the iOS source of truth. The single most serious issue is a broken DELETE endpoint: Android calls a non-existent path-param route while the backend (and iOS) expect a body-based DELETE on the collection path, so card deletion will 404. Beyond that, the per-card list row layout diverges heavily — Android embeds a full barcode preview, a "tap to enlarge" caption and an inline trash-emoji button inside every row, whereas iOS keeps rows compact (icon badge + text + a trailing barcode glyph) and exposes delete only via swipe action and context menu. Android also drops the data-store caching layer (re-fetching on every entry, no pull-to-refresh), omits all haptics, uses emoji instead of the shared NBIconBadge/SF-symbol icons, double-wraps the empty state in an extra card, gives the empty state no icon/action button, and strips the NavigationStack titles + Done/Cancel toolbar from the create and barcode sheets. Overall functionally close but visually and behaviorally off in many places, with one critical bug._

- 🔴 **[bug] DELETE hits wrong endpoint — card deletion will 404**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:324-326` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:194-195`
  - iOS deletes via DELETE /api/personal/loyalty with a JSON body {id}. The backend route app/api/personal/loyalty/route.ts has only a collection-level DELETE handler that reads `id` from rawBody (z.string().uuid()); there is NO [id] dynamic subroute. Android calls api.deleteVoid("/api/personal/loyalty/$id", JsonObject(emptyMap()), JsonObject.serializer()) — a path-param URL that does not exist, with an empty body. This will return 404 and never delete the card; the optimistic toast logic in delete() will report failure (or worse, succeed against a 404 depending on api error handling).
  - **Fix:** Change LoyaltyRepo.delete in android Repositories.kt to call api.deleteVoid("/api/personal/loyalty", body = LoyaltyDeleteBody(id), LoyaltyDeleteBody.serializer()) where the body is a @Serializable data class { val id: String }, matching iOS's IdBody. Do not use a path param.
- 🟠 **[visual] List row embeds full barcode + 'tap to enlarge' caption (not on iOS)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Loyalty/LoyaltyView.swift:197-238` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/loyalty/LoyaltyScreen.kt:241-285`
  - iOS card rows are compact: icon badge + store/member/masked-number text + a trailing `barcode` SF Symbol; NO barcode image and NO 'tap to enlarge' caption are rendered in the list. Android renders a full BarcodePreview (height 64dp) plus a 'loyalty.tapToEnlarge' caption (and a 'noNumber' fallback) inside every list row, making rows much taller and visually different.
  - **Fix:** Remove the BarcodePreview and tapToEnlarge/noNumber block from CardRow (lines 276-284). Keep the row compact and add a trailing barcode icon (Icons.* or shared glyph) matching iOS's `Image(systemName: "barcode")`. Reserve the barcode rendering for the BarcodeSheet only.
- 🟠 **[behavior] Inline trash-emoji delete button instead of swipe + context menu**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Loyalty/LoyaltyView.swift:87-100` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/loyalty/LoyaltyScreen.kt:273`
  - iOS exposes delete via a trailing swipeActions (role .destructive, allowsFullSwipe:false) AND a contextMenu on long-press; the row body itself has no delete button. Android instead puts an always-visible 🗑 emoji Text with .clickable inside the row and uses long-press for copy-number. So Android has no swipe-to-delete and no context menu, and adds a delete affordance iOS doesn't show in the row.
  - **Fix:** Remove the inline 🗑 from CardRow. Wrap each item in a SwipeToDismiss/anchoredDraggable destructive swipe action and add a long-press/context dropdown menu with a Delete entry, mirroring iOS swipeActions + contextMenu. Move the copy-number action to the BarcodeSheet's existing copy button rather than the row long-press (iOS does not copy on row long-press).
- 🟡 **[behavior] Row long-press copies number — not an iOS behavior**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Loyalty/LoyaltyView.swift:95-100` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/loyalty/LoyaltyScreen.kt:159-164,245-250`
  - iOS long-press (contextMenu) on a card offers a Delete action. Android long-press copies the card number to clipboard and shows a 'numberCopied' toast. This is a different gesture meaning entirely.
  - **Fix:** Repurpose long-press to open a context menu with Delete (matching iOS). Copy-number remains available via the BarcodeSheet copy button.
- 🟡 **[visual] Card icon uses emoji 💳 instead of shared NBIconBadge / creditcard.fill**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Loyalty/LoyaltyView.swift:209-210` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/loyalty/LoyaltyScreen.kt:255-262`
  - iOS uses NBIconBadge(systemImage: "creditcard.fill") — the shared neobrutalist badge with vector icon. Android hand-rolls a 36dp Box with a 💳 emoji Text, bypassing the shared NBIconBadge component (which exists at NBComponents.kt:379). Emoji rendering, color and weight differ from the themed vector icon.
  - **Fix:** Replace the hand-rolled Box+emoji with NBIconBadge(icon = Icons.Filled.CreditCard) to match iOS and reuse the shared atom.
- 🟡 **[visual] Empty state double-wrapped in an extra NBCard**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Loyalty/LoyaltyView.swift:56-68` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/loyalty/LoyaltyScreen.kt:145-153`
  - iOS renders NBEmptyState directly. NBEmptyState (NBComponents.kt:561) already applies nbCard internally. Android wraps NBEmptyState inside another NBCard(radius=md), producing a nested card/double border and double padding not present on iOS.
  - **Fix:** Render NBEmptyState directly without the surrounding NBCard wrapper (lines 147-153 → just call NBEmptyState).
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Empty state missing icon and 'Add card' action button**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Loyalty/LoyaltyView.swift:58-63` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/loyalty/LoyaltyScreen.kt:148-151`
  - iOS NBEmptyState passes systemImage: "creditcard.fill" and action: (label: loyalty.addCard, run: { showCreate = true }). Android passes only title + subtitle — no icon and no action button, so the empty wallet has no primary CTA.
  - **Fix:** Pass icon = Icons.Filled.CreditCard, actionLabel = locale.t("loyalty.addCard"), onAction = { showCreate = true } to NBEmptyState (the params already exist in the Android component).
- 🟡 **[visual] BarcodeSheet missing NavigationStack title + Done button**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Loyalty/LoyaltyView.swift:319,375-379` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/loyalty/LoyaltyScreen.kt:297-340`
  - iOS BarcodeSheet is a NavigationStack with inline navigationTitle loyalty.cardTitle and a confirmationAction 'Done' button. Android's ModalBottomSheet has no title bar and no Done button — the loyalty.cardTitle key exists but is never used on Android.
  - **Fix:** Add a top bar/header row inside the bottom sheet showing locale.t("loyalty.cardTitle") and a Done/Gotowe text button (locale.t("common.done")) that calls onDismiss, matching iOS.
- 🟡 **[behavior] No data-store caching — re-fetches every entry, no pull-to-refresh**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Loyalty/LoyaltyView.swift:17-19,115-125; native-ios/Solvio/Core/AppDataStore.swift:344-391` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/loyalty/LoyaltyScreen.kt:65-123`
  - iOS reads from AppDataStore.loyalty (shared cache) via ensureLoyalty()/awaitLoyalty()/didMutateLoyalty() with a TTL, so tab switches do NOT re-fetch, and it has a .refreshable pull-to-refresh with light/success haptics. Android uses a per-screen LoyaltyViewModel that calls vm.load() on every LaunchedEffect(Unit), always re-hitting the network, with no shared cache and no pull-to-refresh gesture.
  - **Fix:** Route loyalty through the shared Android data store / repository cache used by other features (mirroring AppDataStore TTL + ensure/await/didMutate). Add a SwipeRefresh/PullRefresh around the LazyColumn calling a force reload.
- ⚪ **[visual] Create sheet lacks NavigationStack title bar + toolbar layout**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Loyalty/LoyaltyView.swift:265-302` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/loyalty/LoyaltyScreen.kt:428-481`
  - iOS LoyaltyCreateSheet uses a NavigationStack with inline title loyalty.newTitle and Cancel (cancellationAction) / Save (confirmationAction) toolbar buttons at the top. Android renders an inline sectionTitle Text plus a bottom Row with NBSecondaryButton(Cancel) + NBPrimaryButton(Save). Functionally equivalent but the title/toolbar placement and button styling differ from iOS's nav-bar pattern.
  - **Fix:** Acceptable as a platform idiom, but to match iOS more closely consider a top title + trailing Save / leading Cancel header row. At minimum keep the title text styled as a sheet/nav title.
- ⚪ **[copy-l10n] Member name placeholder hardcoded '—' instead of common.none**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Loyalty/LoyaltyView.swift:276` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/loyalty/LoyaltyScreen.kt:457`
  - iOS uses placeholder: locale.t("common.none") ("None"/"Brak") for the member-name field. Android hardcodes placeholder = "—", which is not localized and differs visually.
  - **Fix:** Change the memberName NBTextField placeholder to locale.t("common.none") (key exists in Android L10n).
- ⚪ **[visual] Copy-number button missing leading icon**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Loyalty/LoyaltyView.swift:348-351` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/loyalty/LoyaltyScreen.kt:317-324`
  - iOS copy button is a Label with systemImage 'doc.on.doc' (icon + text). Android NBSecondaryButton renders text only (the component takes only label, no icon param), so the copy icon is absent.
  - **Fix:** Add an icon to the copy button (either extend NBSecondaryButton to accept a leading ImageVector, or place an Icon + label). Use a copy/content-copy icon to mirror doc.on.doc.
- ⚪ **[behavior] No haptic feedback anywhere**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Loyalty/LoyaltyView.swift:73,89,119-123,128,181,345` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/loyalty/LoyaltyScreen.kt:158,184,214,319`
  - iOS fires Haptics on every meaningful action: selection on card tap, impact(.medium) on FAB and delete-confirm, warning on swipe/context delete trigger, impact(.light)+success on pull-to-refresh, selection on copy. Android performs none of these.
  - **Fix:** Add HapticFeedback (LocalHapticFeedback / View.performHapticFeedback) on: card tap (selection), FAB press (medium), delete confirm (medium), copy (selection), and refresh. Match iOS's Haptics.* mapping.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Card number field missing keyboard/capitalization config**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Loyalty/LoyaltyView.swift:269-275` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/loyalty/LoyaltyScreen.kt:447-452`
  - iOS cardNumber field sets keyboardType: .asciiCapable and autocapitalization: .never to avoid auto-capitalizing alphanumeric card codes. Android's NBTextField for cardNumber uses defaults (no keyboardOptions), so auto-capitalization/keyboard may differ.
  - **Fix:** Pass KeyboardOptions(capitalization = KeyboardCapitalization.None, keyboardType = KeyboardType.Ascii) to the cardNumber NBTextField (extend NBTextField if it doesn't expose keyboardOptions).
- ⚪ **[visual] Barcode preview background hardcoded white vs themed Theme.card**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Loyalty/LoyaltyView.swift:399,414` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/loyalty/LoyaltyScreen.kt:367`
  - iOS barcode area uses Theme.card as the container background (theme-aware across Light/Dark/Evening) with Shadow.md. Android's BarcodePreview hardcodes Color.White as the background and uses no nbShadow on the sheet preview. In Dark/Evening themes iOS's barcode container adapts while remaining scannable; Android is always pure white with a thin border only.
  - **Fix:** Use palette.card (or Theme.card) for the barcode container background to match iOS, and add nbShadow(Shadow.md) on the enlarged barcode in the sheet. Note: ZXing bitmap itself stays black-on-white for scannability, but the surrounding container should be themed like iOS.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Missing accessibility labels/hints present on iOS**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Loyalty/LoyaltyView.swift:197-237,146-147` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/loyalty/LoyaltyScreen.kt:241-285,176-186`
  - iOS composes a VoiceOver label per row (store, member, 'Card number 1234' reading last 4 digits), an openCardHint, header traits, and FAB accessibilityLabel/accessibilityHint. Android sets no contentDescription/semantics on the card row or FAB; the loyalty.openCardHint key is unused on Android.
  - **Fix:** Add Modifier.semantics with contentDescription/role on CardRow (compose store + member + last4) and the FAB (label = addCard, hint via stateDescription). Add loyalty.openCardHint usage.

## Analysis — parity ~62% (16 findings)

_Android's AnalysisScreen reproduces the overall structure of iOS AnalysisView (header, period selector, run card, summary, predicted hero, insights, recommendations, anomalies, category trends + bar chart, weekday chart, bank stats) and shares the response model and 15-min per-key cache. However it diverges in several meaningful ways: (1) the entire "Category share" chart + legend section is missing; (2) the period selector is a plain self-sizing chip Row instead of the iOS full-width bordered segmented control; (3) loading uses a plain spinner card (NBLoadingCard) instead of the staged NBProgressCard with ETA, even though NBProgressCard and all progress.* keys already exist; (4) the cache key omits language so a PL->EN switch serves stale-language results; (5) currency is fetched independently and never sent to the AI (no currency in the request body), so the AI may answer in the wrong currency; (6) Polish pluralization of the insights count is lost (no tPlural). There are also smaller behavior gaps (no pull-to-refresh, no in-flight cancellation, no nav title, no cache hydration on first mount, no haptics) and visual gaps (anomaly date shows year, insight/anomaly/recommendation icons are plain glyphs instead of SF-symbol badges, local StatTile uses radius.sm). Functionally usable, but visibly and behaviorally below iOS parity._

- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Category-share chart + legend section entirely missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Analysis/AnalysisView.swift:105, 475-548` → Android: `android/.../features/analysis/AnalysisScreen.kt:213 (absent)`
  - iOS renders categoryShareSection right after categoryTrendsSection: a horizontal stacked share bar (share = abs(changePercent)/sum) plus a per-category legend with color swatch and share%. Android only renders CategoryTrendsSection and never renders any share section. The Android L10n even ships the keys analysis.categoryShareEyebrow/categoryShareTitle (L10n.kt:813-814 / 2181-2182) but they are completely unused.
  - **Fix:** Add a CategoryShareSection composable mirroring iOS: compute magnitudes=max(0.0001,abs(changePercent)), total, share=magnitudes[i]/total; render a single-row stacked Vico bar (or a Box of weighted colored segments) using a 5-color palette (foreground, destructive, success, warning, mutedForeground cycled) with radius.sm corners, then a legend Column of nbCard rows (12dp color square + category + share% in mono(12)). Use eyebrow=analysis.categoryShareEyebrow, title=analysis.categoryShareTitle. Call it from the Loaded branch right after CategoryTrendsSection when categoryTrends is non-empty.
- 🟠 **[visual] Period selector is a chip Row, not the iOS full-width segmented control**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Analysis/AnalysisView.swift:146-151; native-ios/Solvio/Core/UI/NBComponents.swift:440-471` → Android: `android/.../features/analysis/AnalysisScreen.kt:223-252`
  - iOS uses NBSegmented: equal-width buttons (maxWidth .infinity) inside a single Theme.muted container with one outer 2px border, 4pt inner padding, selected segment = filled foreground with radius.sm, labels in mono(11) uppercase tracking(1). Android PeriodSelector is a plain Row(spacedBy 6dp) of individually-bordered, self-sized Boxes (padding 10/8), each with its own border, surface/foreground fill, and SolvioFonts.chip style — a different shape, different border topology, not full-width, different typography.
  - **Fix:** Replace the inline chip Row with the shared NBSegmented composable (already in GroupCreateSheet.kt:300) or build an equivalent: Row(spacedBy 4.dp) of weight(1f) Boxes inside one outer Box with palette.muted background, single outer border radius.md, 4.dp inner padding; selected = palette.foreground fill / radius.sm / text palette.background; labels mono(11) uppercase letterSpacing 1sp. Keep onPick wiring to vm.setPeriod.
- 🟠 **[behavior] Loading uses plain NBLoadingCard instead of staged NBProgressCard with ETA**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Analysis/AnalysisView.swift:63-78` → Android: `android/.../features/analysis/AnalysisScreen.kt:204`
  - iOS shows NBProgressCard during the (slow, 8-15s) AI call: title=analysis.runningTitle and 5 cycling stage labels (progress.preparingRequest/contactingAI/crunchingData/formattingResults/almostDone) with estimatedSeconds:12 and a wall-clock progress bar. Android renders NBLoadingCard() — a bare spinner — even though Android already has a matching NBProgressCard (NBComponents.kt:705) and all five progress.* keys + analysis.runningTitle (L10n.kt:346-350/365).
  - **Fix:** In the Loading branch (AnalysisScreen.kt:204) replace NBLoadingCard() with NBProgressCard(title = locale.t("analysis.runningTitle"), stages = listOf(locale.t("progress.preparingRequest"), locale.t("progress.contactingAI"), locale.t("progress.crunchingData"), locale.t("progress.formattingResults"), locale.t("progress.almostDone")), estimatedSeconds = 12).
- 🟠 **[bug] Currency never sent to the AI request**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Analysis/AnalysisView.swift:709-713; native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:377-382` → Android: `android/.../features/analysis/AnalysisScreen.kt:138; core/network/Repositories.kt:219-223`
  - iOS calls AnalysisRepo.run(lang:, currency:, period:) and the request body includes currency, so the AI quotes amounts in the user's currency. Android AnalysisRunBody has only {lang, period} (Repositories.kt:219) and AnalysisRepo.run accepts only (lang, period); the ViewModel calls AnalysisRepo.run(period = raw) passing neither lang nor currency. Result: AI may produce wrong-currency amounts and is not told the UI language (relies on backend default).
  - **Fix:** Add currency to AnalysisRunBody (val currency: String? = null) and to AnalysisRepo.run(lang, currency, period). In AnalysisViewModel.run pass AnalysisRepo.run(lang = <appLocale language code>, currency = _currency.value, period = _period.value.raw). The ViewModel needs access to the current language (inject AppLocale language code or pass it into run()).
- 🟠 **[bug] Cache key omits language — stale-language results after PL/EN switch**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Analysis/AnalysisView.swift:53-55` → Android: `android/.../features/analysis/AnalysisScreen.kt:147`
  - iOS cache key is "period|currency|language" because the AI writes in the user's language; switching language must miss the cache and re-run. Android key is only "period|currency" (cacheKey() at line 147), so after the user switches language the previously-cached (other-language) analysis is served from cache and never refreshed.
  - **Fix:** Include the current language code in cacheKey(): "${_period.value.raw}|${_currency.value}|${lang}". Requires threading the AppLocale language into the ViewModel (same dependency as the currency-in-request fix).
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] Polish pluralization of insights count lost (no tPlural)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Analysis/AnalysisView.swift:250; native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:1263-1266` → Android: `android/.../features/analysis/AnalysisScreen.kt:289; core/AppLocale.kt:47-54`
  - iOS builds the insights count via tPlural("analysis.insightsCountFmt", count:) and ships .one/.few/.many PL variants (ustalenie/ustalenia/ustaleń) plus EN .one (finding). Android uses locale.format("analysis.insightsCountFmt", items.size) and L10n.kt only has the base form ("%d ustaleń" / "%d findings"), so Polish always says e.g. "1 ustaleń" / "2 ustaleń" (grammatically wrong) and EN always says "1 findings".
  - **Fix:** Add a tPlural helper to AppLocale that selects .one/.few/.many (Polish rules) / .one+base (EN) keys, add analysis.insightsCountFmt.one/.few/.many to L10n.kt for both languages, and call locale.tPlural("analysis.insightsCountFmt", items.size) in InsightsSection.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] No pull-to-refresh**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Analysis/AnalysisView.swift:132-138` → Android: `android/.../features/analysis/AnalysisScreen.kt:165-169 (absent)`
  - iOS attaches .refreshable that forces a real AI run (force:true), bypassing cache, with haptics on threshold and on success. Android's LazyColumn has no pull-to-refresh; the only way to force a fresh run is the Regenerate button.
  - **Fix:** Wrap the LazyColumn in a PullToRefreshBox (material3 pullrefresh) whose onRefresh calls vm.run(force = true). Optionally mirror iOS haptics via HapticFeedback.
- 🟡 **[bug] No in-flight task cancellation when flipping periods quickly**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Analysis/AnalysisView.swift:18, 162-170, 696-723` → Android: `android/.../features/analysis/AnalysisScreen.kt:115-145`
  - iOS tracks currentRunTask and cancels the prior task on every period change so a slow older-period response can't overwrite a newer one (30d->3m->6m fast => 6m wins). Android setPeriod just calls run(force=false) which launches a new viewModelScope coroutine without cancelling the previous one; two concurrent AI calls can land out of order and the later-arriving (older-period) response overwrites state with the wrong period's data, and it also caches each under its own (correct) key but UI state can flip to the wrong one.
  - **Fix:** Hold a Job? in the ViewModel; cancel it at the start of run() before launching the new coroutine (currentJob?.cancel(); currentJob = viewModelScope.launch { ... }). On cancellation, do not write state.
- 🟡 **[behavior] Anomaly date shows year (Fmt.date) instead of day-month (Fmt.dayMonth)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Analysis/AnalysisView.swift:365-367; native-ios/Solvio/Core/Formatters.swift:118-121, 47-52` → Android: `android/.../features/analysis/AnalysisScreen.kt:395; core/Formatters.kt:30-38`
  - iOS formats the anomaly date with Fmt.dayMonth (template "dMMM" — e.g. "5 Jun", no year). Android has no Fmt.dayMonth and uses Fmt.date(it) which renders "d MMM yyyy" (e.g. "5 Jun 2026", includes year), producing a longer, different string in the anomaly row.
  - **Fix:** Add Fmt.dayMonth(iso) to Formatters.kt using DateTimeFormatter.ofPattern("d MMM", Locale.getDefault()) (or ofLocalizedDate skeleton "dMMM") and use it for the anomaly date instead of Fmt.date.
- 🟡 **[visual] Insight icon never falls back to a typed symbol badge**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Analysis/AnalysisView.swift:251-265, 284-292` → Android: `android/.../features/analysis/AnalysisScreen.kt:301, 642-654`
  - iOS: when an insight has an emoji it shows the emoji in a muted-background bordered tile; when it has none it shows NBIconBadge with a type-specific SF symbol (warning=triangle/destructive, achievement=rosette/success, positive=seal/success, tip=lightbulb/warning, default=sparkles). Android always uses a local text IconBadge: emoji if present else the literal "✦", and never renders a typed Material icon. So no-emoji insights look generic and the warning/achievement/tip iconography is lost.
  - **Fix:** In InsightsSection, if item.icon is non-blank render the emoji in a muted-bg bordered tile; otherwise use the shared NBIconBadge(icon = <Material icon by type>, tint = insightTint(type)) with type->icon mapping (Warning, MilitaryTech/EmojiEvents, Verified, Lightbulb, AutoAwesome/default). Avoid the hardcoded "✦".
- ⚪ **[visual] Anomaly/recommendation icons are plain glyphs instead of icon badges**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Analysis/AnalysisView.swift:301, 348-356` → Android: `android/.../features/analysis/AnalysisScreen.kt:337, 387`
  - iOS anomaly badge is an exclamationmark.triangle.fill inside a warning-tinted 36x36 rounded square; recommendation badge is NBIconBadge(arrow.right.circle.fill, priorityColor). Android draws the local text IconBadge with the literal characters "!" and "→" (SolvioFonts.bodyMedium) instead of vector icons — visually thinner/lighter and not matching the filled-symbol look.
  - **Fix:** Use the shared NBIconBadge with Material vectors: Icons.Filled.Warning (tint warning) for anomalies and Icons.Filled.ArrowCircleRight / ArrowForward (tint priorityColor) for recommendations, instead of the textual IconBadge glyphs.
- ⚪ **[visual] Local StatTile uses radius.sm; iOS bank tiles use radius.md grid**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Analysis/AnalysisView.swift:651-662; native-ios/Solvio/Core/UI/NBComponents.swift:24-48` → Android: `android/.../features/analysis/AnalysisScreen.kt:602-607, 628-640`
  - iOS bank stats use the shared NBStatTile (nbCard radius.md, shadow.sm) inside a 2-column LazyVGrid. Android defines a private StatTile with nbCard radius.sm (smaller corner radius) and lays out two explicit Rows of two. The shared Android NBStatTile (NBComponents.kt:344) already matches iOS (radius.md, mono(10) label, bold(22) value), so the local copy is both redundant and visually off by one radius step.
  - **Fix:** Delete the local StatTile and use the shared NBStatTile(label, value, tint, modifier=Modifier.weight(1f)) so the corner radius (md) and styling match iOS.
- ⚪ **[missing-feature] No navigation title set**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Analysis/AnalysisView.swift:118-119` → Android: `android/.../features/analysis/AnalysisScreen.kt (absent)`
  - iOS sets .navigationTitle(analysis.navTitle) inline. Android AnalysisScreen has no top app bar / title wiring; the analysis.navTitle key (L10n.kt:357/1725) is unused for this screen. Depending on the host scaffold the user may see no screen title.
  - **Fix:** Ensure the hosting Scaffold/TopAppBar shows locale.t("analysis.navTitle"), or add a title within the screen if Analysis is presented without the shared nav bar.
- ⚪ **[behavior] First-mount cache hydration and ensureDashboard not performed**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Analysis/AnalysisView.swift:120-129` → Android: `android/.../features/analysis/AnalysisScreen.kt:163`
  - iOS .task calls store.ensureDashboard() and, if result==nil, hydrates from any warm cache entry for the current key so returning to the screen shows data immediately instead of the empty CTA. Android LaunchedEffect(Unit) only calls vm.loadCurrency(); it never pre-populates state from cache on mount (state stays Idle), so re-entering the screen always shows the empty run card even when a fresh cached result exists.
  - **Fix:** In the ViewModel init (or a first-load function called from LaunchedEffect), after currency is known, check cachedFor(cacheKey()) and set _state to Loaded if present; optionally trigger the dashboard ensure equivalent.
- ⚪ **[behavior] No haptic feedback on any interaction**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Analysis/AnalysisView.swift:83, 135-137, 155, 194` → Android: `android/.../features/analysis/AnalysisScreen.kt (absent)`
  - iOS fires Haptics on: retry tap (light), pull-to-refresh threshold (light) + success, period selection (selection tick), and the primary CTA (medium). Android has no haptic feedback on period change, generate/regenerate, retry, or refresh.
  - **Fix:** Add LocalHapticFeedback / view.performHapticFeedback calls: selection tick on period change, a press feedback on the generate/regenerate and retry actions, and success feedback when a forced refresh completes.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Currency hardcoded default 'PLN' until SettingsRepo resolves; iOS reads store.currency**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Analysis/AnalysisView.swift:19` → Android: `android/.../features/analysis/AnalysisScreen.kt:107, 122-128`
  - iOS reads currency synchronously from the shared AppDataStore (already hydrated). Android initializes _currency to "PLN" and only updates after an async SettingsRepo.fetch(); if the AI run or any amount formatting happens before that completes (e.g. fast Generate tap or a warm cache hit on mount), amounts render in PLN regardless of the user's real currency, and the cache key uses the wrong currency.
  - **Fix:** Source currency from the same shared store/state the rest of the app uses (or block/await currency load before first run), rather than a per-screen SettingsRepo.fetch with a PLN default, so it is correct immediately and consistent with Dashboard/Expenses.

## Audit (Shopping audit) — parity ~74% (15 findings)

_Android's AuditScreen.kt is a fairly faithful port of iOS AuditView.swift: same overall section order (header, generate card, KPI hero, AI summary, personal message, best store, top tip, top stores, top products, price comparisons, promotions), same models, same i18n keys for most strings, and matching spacing/radii/shadows on most cards. However there are several real divergences. The biggest behavioral gaps: (1) Android uses the plain NBLoadingCard during generation instead of iOS's rich staged NBProgressCard (5 stage strings + progress bar + 18s estimate) even though NBProgressCard exists on Android; (2) Android collapses the whole previous result into a Loading state on regenerate, so the prior audit disappears during the ~18s wait, whereas iOS keeps the result visible and only spins the button; (3) Android never sends currency or force to the audit API and never shows the error toast that iOS shows. There are also icon/visual mismatches (emoji glyphs and a generic info char vs iOS NBIconBadge SF Symbols for the cart/star/bubble/lightbulb badges, plus a wrong tip icon), a pluralization gap for the transaction count, a format-specifier mismatch for timesBoughtFmt, an Android-only expand/collapse toggle on the price list that iOS does not have, and three mono-label font size/tracking mismatches. Estimated parity ~74%._

- 🟠 **[behavior] Generation uses plain NBLoadingCard instead of staged NBProgressCard**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Audit/AuditView.swift:25-37` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/audit/AuditScreen.kt:133`
  - iOS shows NBProgressCard with title locale.t("audit.runningTitle"), 5 staged messages (progress.preparingRequest, progress.scanningWeb, progress.matchingProducts, progress.findingDeals, progress.almostDone) and estimatedSeconds:18 (animated progress bar + rotating stage text). Android shows NBLoadingCard() — just a spinner plus the generic 'common.loading' text. Android already has a working NBProgressCard(title, stages, estimatedSeconds) at NBComponents.kt:705 and all the progress.* strings exist in L10n.kt, so the rich card is available but unused. Also audit.runningTitle exists in L10n.kt:410/1778 but is never referenced.
  - **Fix:** Replace `item { NBLoadingCard() }` in the Loading branch with `item { NBProgressCard(title = locale.t("audit.runningTitle"), stages = listOf(locale.t("progress.preparingRequest"), locale.t("progress.scanningWeb"), locale.t("progress.matchingProducts"), locale.t("progress.findingDeals"), locale.t("progress.almostDone")), estimatedSeconds = 18) }`.
- 🟠 **[behavior] Regenerate discards previous result; iOS keeps it visible**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Audit/AuditView.swift:11-12,25,46,116,427-437` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/audit/AuditScreen.kt:73-85,131-155`
  - iOS keeps `result` in separate @State; during a regenerate isLoading becomes true but result stays non-nil, so the progress card is gated on `isLoading && result == nil` (only first load) and the previous full audit remains on screen with just an inline button spinner. Android's single sealed UiState sets `_state.value = UiState.Loading` at the start of run(), which clears the previously Loaded result entirely — so on 'Recalculate' the whole audit vanishes and is replaced by the loading card for ~18s, then reappears. Different UX from iOS.
  - **Fix:** Track loading independently of the loaded data. E.g. add `data class Loaded(data, refreshing: Boolean)` or keep a separate `isLoading` MutableStateFlow plus a retained `result` so the previous AuditResult stays rendered during regenerate; only show the progress card when there is no prior result (mirror iOS `isLoading && result == nil`).
- 🟠 **[bug] Audit API call omits currency and force parameters**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:356-361; native-ios/Solvio/Features/Audit/AuditView.swift:432` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/network/Repositories.kt:209-213; android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/audit/AuditScreen.kt:80`
  - iOS AuditRepo.generate(lang:currency:force:) sends a body with lang, currency and force, and AuditView passes the user's currency from the central store (store.currency). Android's AuditRepo.generate(lang) and AuditGenerateBody(lang) only send lang — currency and force are not part of the body or method. While the backend normalizes currency from user settings, the iOS contract still sends currency, and there is no way to force-bypass the 6h backend cache on Android (force=true), so a user tapping 'Recalculate' may get a stale cached result.
  - **Fix:** Extend AuditGenerateBody to `data class AuditGenerateBody(val lang: String? = null, val currency: String? = null, val force: Boolean? = null)` and AuditRepo.generate to accept currency + force. Pass the user's currency from the data store and force=true on the explicit Regenerate tap to match iOS cache-busting.
- 🟡 **[behavior] Error toast on failure is never shown**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Audit/AuditView.swift:433-436` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/audit/AuditScreen.kt:92,134`
  - iOS shows both an inline NBErrorCard AND a toast (toast.error(locale.t("audit.failed"), description: error...)) when generation fails. Android binds `val toast = LocalToast.current` (line 92) but never calls it; only the inline NBErrorCard is shown. So the audit.failed string (present in L10n.kt:434/1797) is unused on Android, and the toast feedback iOS gives is missing. The bound `toast` is also a dead variable (lint warning).
  - **Fix:** In the Error path (or in the ViewModel via a one-shot event/SharedFlow), call toast.error(locale.t("audit.failed"), description = message). Simplest: collect an error event from the VM in the composable and invoke toast there, matching iOS.
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] Transaction count not pluralized (missing CLDR plural support)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Audit/AuditView.swift:147; native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:1204-1207` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/audit/AuditScreen.kt:187; android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/L10n.kt:415,1783`
  - iOS uses locale.tPlural("audit.txnsFmt", count:) with full Polish CLDR forms (audit.txnsFmt.one='%d transakcja', .few='%d transakcje', .many='%d transakcji') and EN (.one='%d txn'). Android calls locale.format("audit.txnsFmt", r.transactionCount) and L10n.kt only defines the single base key (PL '%d transakcji', EN '%d txns') with no .one/.few/.many variants and AppLocale.kt has no tPlural/plural helper. Result: '1 transakcji' / '2 transakcji' / '1 txns' instead of correct plural forms.
  - **Fix:** Add a tPlural(baseKey, count) helper to AppLocale.kt implementing Polish CLDR (one/few/many) and EN (one/other), add the audit.txnsFmt.one/.few/.many keys (and EN .one) to L10n.kt, and call locale.tPlural("audit.txnsFmt", r.transactionCount) in KpiCard.
- 🟡 **[behavior] Price comparison sub-list has an Android-only expand/collapse toggle**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Audit/AuditView.swift:349-366` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/audit/AuditScreen.kt:350-422`
  - iOS Audit always renders the full per-store price breakdown inline inside a muted box (no toggle) sorted ascending. Android's PriceComparisonCard adds an expanded state with a clickable 'View all prices (N)' / 'Hide all prices' row plus a ▼/▲ chevron, defaulting to collapsed — so the price list is hidden by default. This interaction (and the prices.viewAllPricesFmt / prices.hideAllPrices strings) belongs to the separate iOS PricesView, not the Audit screen. Different default state and structure from iOS Audit.
  - **Fix:** Remove the expanded toggle from PriceComparisonCard; render the sorted price rows always-visible inside the muted box (RoundedCornerShape(Radius.sm), padding Spacing.xs) exactly like iOS lines 349-366, dropping the clickable header row and chevron.
- 🟡 **[visual] KPI card icon: emoji + wrong background vs iOS NBIconBadge**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Audit/AuditView.swift:139; native-ios/Solvio/Core/UI/NBComponents.swift:95-110` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/audit/AuditScreen.kt:174-181`
  - iOS uses NBIconBadge(systemImage: "cart.fill", size: 36) — an SF Symbol cart in a 36pt box with Theme.muted background and Theme.border stroke. Android draws an inline Box with background palette.foreground.copy(alpha=0.08f) (NOT palette.muted) and renders a 🛒 emoji glyph instead of a vector cart icon. Different background color and emoji-vs-vector rendering. Android already has NBIconBadge(icon: ImageVector, ...) at NBComponents.kt:379 with the correct muted bg.
  - **Fix:** Replace the inline Box+Text("🛒") with NBIconBadge(icon = Icons.Filled.ShoppingCart, size = 36.dp) so background = palette.muted, border = palette.border, and a vector cart icon matches iOS.
- 🟡 **[visual] Personal-message card uses info-char glyph instead of bubble NBIconBadge**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Audit/AuditView.swift:198-214` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/audit/AuditScreen.kt:139-141,232-248`
  - iOS messageCard places an NBIconBadge(systemImage: "bubble.left.fill", tint: Theme.info) — a bordered icon badge with muted background — next to the text inside an info-tinted card. Android's TintedNoticeCard renders a bare Text("ⓘ") (info-circle char) with no badge box and the wrong icon (info circle vs speech bubble).
  - **Fix:** In the personalMessage branch use a card that embeds NBIconBadge(icon = Icons.Filled.ChatBubble or AutoMirrored ChatBubble, tint = palette.info) instead of the generic ⓘ glyph; keep the info-tinted background/border.
- 🟡 **[visual] Top-tip card shows info-circle glyph instead of lightbulb icon**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Audit/AuditView.swift:234-251` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/audit/AuditScreen.kt:145-147,232-248`
  - iOS topTipCard uses NBIconBadge(systemImage: "lightbulb.fill", tint: Theme.warning). Android reuses the same TintedNoticeCard (tint = warning) which always renders Text("ⓘ") — so the tip card shows an info-circle character instead of a lightbulb. Both the personalMessage and topTip cards collapse to the identical ⓘ glyph on Android, losing iOS's distinct bubble vs lightbulb iconography.
  - **Fix:** Give TintedNoticeCard an icon parameter (ImageVector) or branch the two call sites: use a lightbulb icon (Icons.Filled.Lightbulb / EmojiObjects) for the tip and a chat-bubble for the message, rendered via NBIconBadge with the respective tint, mirroring iOS.
- ⚪ **[visual] Best-store badge: wrong background/border color and emoji glyph**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Audit/AuditView.swift:216-232; native-ios/Solvio/Core/UI/NBComponents.swift:95-110` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/audit/AuditScreen.kt:250-275`
  - iOS bestStoreCard uses NBIconBadge(systemImage: "star.fill", tint: Theme.success) — a star vector with Theme.muted background and Theme.border stroke (only the tint is success-colored). Android draws an inline Box with background palette.success.copy(alpha=0.15f) and a success-colored border plus a Text("★") glyph. So Android tints the whole badge background/border green and uses a star character instead of matching iOS's muted-bg/border + green star icon.
  - **Fix:** Use NBIconBadge(icon = Icons.Filled.Star, tint = palette.success, size = 36.dp) (default muted background + border) instead of the inline success-tinted Box and ★ glyph.
- ⚪ **[copy-l10n] timesBoughtFmt uses %@ template + %.0f formatting instead of %.0f directly**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Audit/AuditView.swift:296; native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:1216; native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n+EN.swift:1180` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/audit/AuditScreen.kt:323; android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/L10n.kt:424,1792`
  - iOS key audit.timesBoughtFmt = '%.0f× kupione' / '%.0f× bought' and is fed p.count (Double) via String(format:). Android changed the key to '%@× kupione' / '%@× bought' and pre-formats with String.format("%.0f", p.count) before substituting through the %@ regex. Output text is equivalent, but the key string diverges from iOS and relies on the naive %[@dDs] substitution which would NOT match a literal %.0f if the iOS string were kept — a latent inconsistency. Keep the keys identical across platforms.
  - **Fix:** Either keep '%.0f×…' in L10n.kt and make locale.format handle %.0f, or document the intentional change. Lowest-risk parity fix: restore the iOS key text and format the count to '%.0f' in a way the substituter handles, so the L10n strings match iOS byte-for-byte.
- ⚪ **[visual] potentialSaving/youPaid/cheapest labels use eyebrow (mono 11) instead of mono 10 + tracking**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Audit/AuditView.swift:154-156,333,341` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/audit/AuditScreen.kt:202,374,382`
  - iOS renders the POTENTIAL SAVING, ZAPŁACIŁEŚ (you paid) and NAJTANIEJ (cheapest) labels with AppFont.mono(10) and .tracking(1) (10pt monospace, 1pt letter spacing). Android uses SolvioFonts.eyebrow which is mono(11) with no letter-spacing (Typography.kt:30). So these labels are ~1pt larger and lack the tracking on Android.
  - **Fix:** Use SolvioFonts.mono(10) with a letterSpacing of ~1sp (or a dedicated style matching AppFont.mono(10)+tracking(1)) for these three labels instead of SolvioFonts.eyebrow.
- ⚪ **[visual] webSearchUsed tag wraps inside the savings row vs iOS Spacer push**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Audit/AuditView.swift:151-169` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/audit/AuditScreen.kt:192-215`
  - iOS lays the savings row as [label/value column] + Spacer() + optional web-search NBTag, so the tag is pushed to the trailing edge with the value column taking natural width. Android uses Modifier.weight(1f) on the column and appends the tag after it (CenterVertically) without an explicit Spacer; functionally similar but the column greedily takes all remaining width and the tag aligns center-vertically. Minor alignment nuance — verify the tag sits flush trailing and the value/label do not get squeezed for long PLN amounts.
  - **Fix:** Confirm trailing alignment: keep weight(1f) on the column but ensure the NBTag aligns Top (iOS HStack default with .top content) or matches iOS center alignment; verify long amounts don't truncate the value before the tag.
- ⚪ **[missing-feature] No per-screen nav title bar ('Audit') / no pull-to-refresh**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Audit/AuditView.swift:79-87` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/audit/AuditScreen.kt:97-101`
  - iOS sets .navigationTitle(locale.t("audit.navTitle")) with .inline display and a .refreshable pull-to-refresh that re-runs the audit (with light + success haptics). Android hosts AuditScreen() under a shared AppMobileHeader (MainTabScreen.kt:131) with no per-screen 'Audit' title and the LazyColumn has no pull-to-refresh, so audit.navTitle (L10n.kt) is unused and pull-to-refresh is absent. The in-content NBScreenHeader still carries the title, so impact is limited, but pull-to-refresh re-generate is missing.
  - **Fix:** If matching iOS chrome: surface the per-screen title (audit.navTitle) in the host header for this destination, and wrap the LazyColumn in a PullToRefreshBox (material3) whose onRefresh calls vm.run(lang) to mirror iOS .refreshable.
- ⚪ **[behavior] No haptic feedback on generate / refresh / retry**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Audit/AuditView.swift:42,84-86,110,112` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS fires Haptics.impact(.medium) on Generate tap, Haptics.impact(.light) + Haptics.success() on pull-to-refresh, and Haptics.impact(.light) on error retry. Android has no haptics on any of these (and no Haptics utility exists in core/ at all — this is an app-wide gap, not Audit-specific).
  - **Fix:** Introduce a shared Haptics helper (View.performHapticFeedback / HapticFeedbackType) and call medium impact on generate, light+success on refresh, light on retry to mirror iOS. Lower priority since the haptics infra is missing project-wide.

## Categories — parity ~62% (15 findings)

_Android's Categories area is functionally present (list, create, edit, delete) and actually adds an emoji icon picker + 7-color swatch palette that iOS lacks, but it diverges from the iOS source of truth in several meaningful ways. Data sourcing differs fundamentally: iOS reads categories from the prefetched AppDataStore dashboard slice (instant, cache-aware, keeps stale data on refresh failure, supports pull-to-refresh and live store sync), whereas Android refetches /api/data/settings on every entry into a hard 3-state machine (Loading/Error/Loaded) with no cache fallback, no pull-to-refresh, and no live sync. Create goes through the wrong endpoint on Android (SettingsRepo.addCategory -> /api/data/settings type:addCategory) vs iOS (CategoriesRepo.create -> POST /api/data/categories). The loading state uses NBLoadingCard instead of iOS's NBSkeletonList(6), the empty state is double-wrapped in an NBCard and drops both the folder icon and the "New category" action button, row spacing is wrong (md vs xs), the delete affordance/UX differs (iOS long-press context menu shown on ALL categories incl. defaults; Android explicit trash button hidden for defaults + AlertDialog), the FAB lacks the hard offset shadow and uses a text "+" at the wrong size, no haptics, and the icon rendering can't handle SF-Symbol-style names. parityPercent reflects "works but visibly and behaviorally off."_

- 🟠 **[behavior] Create category hits the wrong endpoint (SettingsRepo.addCategory vs CategoriesRepo.create)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Categories/CategoriesManagerView.swift:109; native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:128-130` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/categories/CategoriesManagerScreen.kt:71`
  - iOS create calls CategoriesRepo.create(.init(name,icon)) -> POST /api/data/categories and consumes the returned flat Category row. Android calls SettingsRepo.addCategory -> POST /api/data/settings with type 'addCategory' and additionally sends color + isDefault:false. This is a different API path/contract than iOS and also smuggles isDefault:false into the create payload, which iOS never sends.
  - **Fix:** Add CategoriesRepo.create(CategoryCreate) -> POST /api/data/categories in Repositories.kt (mirroring iOS) and call it from the VM create() path instead of SettingsRepo.addCategory. Do not send isDefault on create. Keep color only if the color feature is intentionally retained.
- 🟠 **[visual] Empty state is double-card-wrapped and drops the folder icon + 'New category' action button**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Categories/CategoriesManagerView.swift:31-37` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/categories/CategoriesManagerScreen.kt:139-146`
  - iOS NBEmptyState passes systemImage 'folder.fill', title, subtitle, AND an action (label: categories.new, run: showCreate=true). Android wraps NBEmptyState in an extra NBCard (double border/shadow box-in-box) and only passes title + subtitle — no icon, no action button. NBEmptyState already self-wraps in nbCard, so the outer NBCard is a visual bug.
  - **Fix:** Remove the outer NBCard wrapper. Call NBEmptyState(icon = <folder vector>, title, subtitle, actionLabel = locale.t("categories.new"), onAction = { showCreate = true }). Use a folder ImageVector (e.g. Icons.Filled.Folder) to match folder.fill.
- 🟠 **[behavior] No data-store integration: refetches /api/data/settings every entry, no cache fallback, no pull-to-refresh, no live sync**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Categories/CategoriesManagerView.swift:76-86,164-183` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/categories/CategoriesManagerScreen.kt:56-66,116`
  - iOS reads from AppDataStore (prefetched dashboard slice) so the screen is instant, honors a cache TTL (load force=false), supports .refreshable pull-to-refresh (force=true), keeps stale categories visible on refresh failure (error card only when nothing cached), and re-syncs the VM whenever store.categories changes after any CRUD mutation. Android has a plain VM that calls SettingsRepo.fetch() on every LaunchedEffect with a hard Loading/Error/Loaded state — no cache, no pull-to-refresh, full-screen error even if data was previously loaded, and after CRUD it just re-fetches (no shared store invalidation, so dashboard/budget/health caches elsewhere are not busted like iOS's store.didMutateCategoriesOrBudgetsOrSettings()).
  - **Fix:** Introduce a shared AppDataStore-equivalent (or at minimum: cache last-loaded categories, add pull-to-refresh via PullToRefreshBox, keep showing cached rows on refresh error, and after create/update/delete invalidate the shared dashboard/budget/settings caches instead of only re-fetching this screen).
- 🟡 **[behavior] Loading state uses NBLoadingCard instead of NBSkeletonList(rows: 6)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Categories/CategoriesManagerView.swift:24-25` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/categories/CategoriesManagerScreen.kt:133`
  - iOS shows NBSkeletonList(rows: 6) (six shimmering row placeholders) only when loading AND list is empty. Android shows a single generic NBLoadingCard. Different first-paint experience.
  - **Fix:** Replace `item { NBLoadingCard() }` with `item { NBSkeletonList(rows = 6) }` (NBSkeletonList already exists in NBComponents.kt:673).
- 🟡 **[visual] Category row spacing is md, iOS uses xs between rows**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Categories/CategoriesManagerView.swift:22,39` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/categories/CategoriesManagerScreen.kt:121,148-154`
  - iOS uses VStack(spacing: md) for the header-to-list gap but LazyVStack(spacing: Theme.Spacing.xs) for the gap BETWEEN category rows. Android's LazyColumn uses a single verticalArrangement = spacedBy(Spacing.md) for everything, so the rows are spaced md apart — visibly looser than iOS.
  - **Fix:** Set LazyColumn verticalArrangement to spacedBy(SolvioTheme.Spacing.xs) and instead add an explicit md spacer/padding under the NBScreenHeader item, OR wrap rows so inter-row spacing is xs while header gap stays md.
- 🟡 **[behavior] Delete UX differs: long-press context menu (all categories) on iOS vs explicit trash button hidden for defaults + AlertDialog on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Categories/CategoriesManagerView.swift:48-66` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/categories/CategoriesManagerScreen.kt:152,205-225,260-263`
  - iOS exposes Delete via a long-press .contextMenu on EVERY row (including default categories — backend 400s and surfaces a toast), with no confirmation dialog (the destructive context-menu item fires immediately) and a warning haptic. Android shows a persistent inline trash emoji button that is HIDDEN for default categories (c.isDefault != true), and wraps deletion in an AlertDialog confirmation. Net: defaults can be delete-attempted on iOS but not on Android, the interaction model (long-press vs visible button) differs, and Android adds a confirmation step iOS doesn't have.
  - **Fix:** Match iOS: trigger delete via a long-press context menu (Compose: combinedClickable onLongClick or a dropdown) available on all rows incl. defaults, fire delete immediately without the AlertDialog (or keep the dialog deliberately as an improvement but document it), and remove the isDefault gating so defaults also show the delete affordance and surface the backend error toast.
- 🟡 **[visual] FAB missing hard offset shadow and uses Text '+' at wrong size/weight vs iOS plus glyph**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Categories/CategoriesManagerView.swift:88-103` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/categories/CategoriesManagerScreen.kt:163-173`
  - iOS FAB: 56x56, foreground bg, border, Radius.md, AND .nbShadow(Theme.Shadow.md) (the signature neobrutalism hard offset shadow), glyph Image(systemName: plus) at font size 22 bold. Android FAB: 56x56, foreground bg, border, Radius.md, but NO shadow at all, and renders a literal Text("+") at size 28 bold. Missing the hard shadow breaks the neobrutalism look; the '+' character is also not centered/weighted identically to the SF plus symbol at 22.
  - **Fix:** Add the nbShadow(SolvioTheme.Shadow.md) modifier to the FAB Box (before/with background) so it gets the hard offset shadow, and render a proper plus icon (Icons.Filled.Add) sized ~22dp bold tinted palette.background instead of Text("+") at 28.
- 🟡 **[visual] Icon rendering cannot handle SF-Symbol-style names; row/edit/delete affordances use raw emoji text instead of NBIconBadge/icons**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Categories/CategoriesManagerView.swift:137,149; native-ios/Solvio/Core/UI/NBComponents.swift:95-124` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/categories/CategoriesManagerScreen.kt:243-262`
  - iOS renders the category icon via NBIconBadge -> CategoryIcon.render which detects emoji vs SF Symbol names and renders either; the row's trailing affordance is an SF Symbol pencil (Image systemName 'pencil'); default fallback icon is the SF Symbol 'folder.fill'. Android hand-rolls a 40dp Box rendering c.icon as plain Text (so any non-emoji icon name like 'cart.fill' would show as literal text), falls back to the '📁' emoji, and uses literal '✏️' and '🗑' emoji Text for edit/delete — not the NBIconBadge component and not vector icons. Backend default seeds are emoji today so the common case works, but any SF-Symbol-named icon (which iOS supports) breaks on Android, and the affordances look inconsistent with the neobrutalism icon set.
  - **Fix:** Build a CategoryIcon equivalent on Android that detects emoji vs symbol names and maps known symbol names to ImageVectors (fallback to a folder vector). Render the badge via NBIconBadge for consistency, and replace the emoji ✏️/🗑 with proper vector icons (Edit / Delete).
- 🟡 **[bug] Color is captured in the editor but silently discarded on edit/update**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Categories/CategoriesManagerView.swift:119-130` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/categories/CategoriesManagerScreen.kt:195-202; android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/categories/CategoryEditorSheet.kt:47,164-170; android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/models/Models.kt:58`
  - The Android editor lets the user pick a color (colorHex) and passes it through onSubmit, but the edit path (onSubmit = { name, icon, _ -> vm.update(...) }) ignores the color, and CategoryUpdate (Models.kt:58) has no color field, and CategoriesRepo.update only sends id/name/icon. So a user editing an existing category's color sees the swatch UI but the change is silently dropped — a confusing dead control. (On create, color IS sent via SettingsRepo.addCategory.) iOS has no color field at all, so this is an Android-introduced half-implemented feature.
  - **Fix:** Either (a) remove the color picker from the edit flow / the whole feature to match iOS, or (b) add color to CategoryUpdate + CategoriesRepo.update payload and wire the edit branch to pass colorHex so the control actually works.
- ⚪ **[behavior] No haptic feedback on row tap, FAB tap, or delete**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Categories/CategoriesManagerView.swift:42,50,89` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS fires Haptics.selection() on row tap (open editor), Haptics.warning() before delete, and Haptics.impact(.light) on FAB tap. Android has no haptics anywhere in the Categories feature, and there is no Haptics utility in core/ at all.
  - **Fix:** Add a Haptics helper (HapticFeedback / view.performHapticFeedback) and fire selection on row tap, a heavier/warning haptic before delete, and a light impact on FAB tap to mirror iOS.
- ⚪ **[copy-l10n] Delete failure toast drops the iOS error description**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Categories/CategoriesManagerView.swift:62,113,127` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/categories/CategoriesManagerScreen.kt:184,199,216`
  - iOS error toasts pass a description (error.localizedDescription) alongside the localized title for create/update/delete failures — important because deleting a default category returns a 400 whose message tells the user why. Android only shows the bare localized title (categories.deleteFailed etc.) with no description, so the user loses the backend reason.
  - **Fix:** Capture the thrown error message in the VM create/update/delete catch blocks and pass it as the toast description (toast.error(title, description = e.message)) to match iOS.
- ⚪ **[behavior] Total count shows 0 during loading/error instead of being omitted like iOS**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Categories/CategoriesManagerView.swift:23,158-159` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/categories/CategoriesManagerScreen.kt:124-129`
  - iOS subtitle uses vm.categories.count which, on a warm AppDataStore, is already populated before the network settles (prefetched), so the user rarely sees '0 total'. Android computes total as (state as? Loaded)?.categories?.size ?: 0, so during the initial Loading state it always renders the subtitle 'Łącznie: 0' / '0 total' under the skeleton/loading card, which iOS does not show.
  - **Fix:** Once cached/prefetched categories are available (see store-integration fix), derive the count from cached data; or suppress/skeleton the subtitle while in Loading state so it never flashes '0 total'.
- ⚪ **[visual] Default badge uses NBTag pill whereas iOS uses plain caption text**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Categories/CategoriesManagerView.swift:142-146` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/categories/CategoriesManagerScreen.kt:255-257`
  - iOS renders the 'Default' marker as a plain small caption text in mutedForeground directly under the category name (no chrome). Android renders it as an NBTag (a bordered/colored pill), a heavier visual treatment than iOS.
  - **Fix:** Replace NBTag with a plain Text(locale.t("categories.default"), style = SolvioFonts.caption.copy(color = palette.mutedForeground)) to match iOS.
- ⚪ **[visual] Editor sheet is bottom-sheet with inline header/buttons vs iOS NavigationStack sheet with toolbar Cancel/Save**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Categories/CategoriesManagerView.swift:186-235` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/categories/CategoryEditorSheet.kt:60-176`
  - iOS presents CategoryEditSheet inside a NavigationStack with an inline navigation title (New/Edit category) and toolbar Cancel (cancellationAction) + Save (confirmationAction, disabled when name empty). Android uses a ModalBottomSheet with an inline section-title Text and bottom-of-form NBSecondaryButton(Cancel)+NBPrimaryButton(Save). Plus Android adds an emoji icon-picker grid and a 7-color swatch palette (categories.color) that iOS does not have. Save-disabled-when-name-empty parity is preserved on both. This is an intentional Android enhancement per the file comment but is a structural visual deviation from the iOS source of truth.
  - **Fix:** If strict parity is required, drop the color picker + categories.color usage and present a top app bar with Cancel/Save to mirror the iOS NavigationStack toolbar. If the icon/color picker is a deliberate enhancement, leave it but be aware it deviates from iOS and the color value is silently discarded on edit (onSubmit ignores color in the edit branch, line 195).
- ⚪ **[visual] Icon picker grid chunked at 8 but only one row will ever render given 15 items / narrow width risk**
  - iOS: `absent (iOS has no icon picker)` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/categories/CategoryEditorSheet.kt:91-108`
  - The 15-emoji iconChoices list is chunked(8) into rows of 8 inside a plain Row (no horizontal scroll / no wrap). Eight 40dp tiles + 6dp gaps ~= 362dp which can overflow narrow screens, and the second row of 7 tiles is fine, but on small/compact widths the first row may clip. This is an Android-only control with no iOS counterpart, so it cannot be 'parity' but is a layout robustness risk in the added feature.
  - **Fix:** Use a FlowRow (accompanist/Compose foundation FlowRow) or a horizontally scrollable Row, or chunk to a count that fits the minimum width, so tiles never clip.

## Prices (AI price comparison) — parity ~72% (14 findings)

_Android's PricesScreen.kt reproduces the overall structure of iOS PricesView.swift fairly faithfully — header, run card, summary card, per-product expandable comparison cards, inner all-prices list, and empty/error states are all present with matching layout, spacing tokens, radii and colors. However it diverges in several user-visible and behavioral ways: the rich animated AI progress card (stages + ETA bar) is replaced by a generic single-line loading spinner; pull-to-refresh, all haptics, and the failure toast are entirely absent; currency is hardcoded to "PLN" instead of reading the central settings currency, so EUR users see wrong currency symbols; Polish plural handling is missing (Android lacks tPlural and the .one/.few/.many keys, so counts always render the genitive-plural form, e.g. "1 produktów"); the description copy is truncated relative to iOS in both languages; SF Symbol icon badges are substituted with raw emoji Text; the empty-state icon is dropped; and the expand-all control loses its uppercase styling and chevron. None of these are crashes, but together they represent a meaningful parity gap in polish, correct localization, and currency correctness._

- 🟠 **[missing-feature] Generic loading card instead of animated AI progress card**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Prices/PricesView.swift:31-42` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/prices/PricesScreen.kt:116`
  - iOS shows NBProgressCard with title prices.runningTitle, 4 rotating stage strings (progress.preparingRequest/scanningWeb/matchingProducts/almostDone) and an estimated 14s progress bar — only while result==nil (first run). Android shows a generic NBLoadingCard() (a single spinner + common.loading text) for every Loading state. NBProgressCard already exists on Android (NBComponents.kt:705) with a matching signature, so this is a wiring miss, not a missing component.
  - **Fix:** Replace `item { NBLoadingCard() }` (PricesScreen.kt:116) with NBProgressCard(title = locale.t("prices.runningTitle"), stages = listOf(locale.t("progress.preparingRequest"), locale.t("progress.scanningWeb"), locale.t("progress.matchingProducts"), locale.t("progress.almostDone")), estimatedSeconds = 14). Note iOS only shows it on first load (result==nil); to fully match, render the progress card only when there is no previously Loaded data, otherwise keep the recompare button's inline spinner.
- 🟠 **[bug] Currency hardcoded to PLN instead of central settings currency**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Prices/PricesView.swift:14-17` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/prices/PricesScreen.kt:76`
  - iOS reads currency from the central store (`store.currency`, derived from settings, uppercased) and passes it both to PricesRepo.compare and to every Fmt.amount call. Android hardcodes `val currency = "PLN"`, so a user whose settings currency is EUR will both request the wrong currency from the backend and render all amounts (totalPotentialSavings, savings, youPaid, best, per-store prices) with a złoty format. iOS comments explicitly call out that hardcoding PLN was a previously-fixed bug. DashboardScreen.kt:99 already reads `data.settings?.currency ?: "PLN"`, proving the value is available.
  - **Fix:** Source currency from the same settings/dashboard store the rest of the app uses (e.g. read settings.currency via the dashboard/settings ViewModel or a shared store) instead of the literal "PLN", and uppercase it. Pass that into vm.run(...) and into all Fmt.amount(...) calls in this screen.
- 🟠 **[copy-l10n] Polish plural forms not handled for product/comparison counts**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Prices/PricesView.swift:134,196; native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:1161-1171` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/prices/PricesScreen.kt:139,213; android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/AppLocale.kt:47-54`
  - iOS uses locale.tPlural("prices.productsCountFmt", count:) and tPlural("prices.comparisonsCountFmt", count:) with CLDR Polish categories, and the PL L10n table defines .one/.few/.many variants (e.g. '1 produkt', '2 produkty', '5 produktów'). Android calls locale.format(...) which has no plural logic, and the Android L10n.kt only defines the base keys 'prices.productsCountFmt'='%d produktów' and 'prices.comparisonsCountFmt'='%d porównań' — no .one/.few/.many. Result: Polish always renders the genitive-plural form, e.g. '1 produktów', '2 produktów', '1 porównań'. AppLocale.kt has no tPlural function at all.
  - **Fix:** Add a tPlural(baseKey, count) to AppLocale.kt mirroring iOS (Polish one/few/many categories), add the .one/.few/.many keys to L10n.kt (both PL and EN), and switch PricesScreen.kt lines 139 and 213 from locale.format(...) to locale.tPlural(...) with the count, substituting %d. EN needs .one ('%d product'/'%d comparison') and base/many ('%d products'/'%d comparisons').
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Pull-to-refresh missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Prices/PricesView.swift:70-75` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS wraps the ScrollView in .refreshable that re-runs compare() with light haptic on start and success haptic on completion. Android's LazyColumn has no pull-to-refresh affordance at all.
  - **Fix:** Wrap the LazyColumn in a PullToRefreshBox (material3 pulltorefresh) whose onRefresh calls vm.run(lang, currency); drive the refreshing flag from the Loading state.
- 🟡 **[copy-l10n] Run description copy truncated vs iOS (PL and EN)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n.swift:1155; native-ios/Solvio/Core/L10n+EN.swift:1125` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/core/L10n.kt:440,1808`
  - iOS prices.description ends with an extra instruction: PL '… Najpierw zeskanuj paragony, aby zasilić dane.' and EN '… Scan receipts first to populate the dataset.'. Android's prices.description omits that final sentence in both languages, losing the onboarding hint that drives users to scan receipts first.
  - **Fix:** Append the missing sentence to prices.description in L10n.kt for both PL (line 440) and EN (line 1808) so the strings exactly match iOS.
- 🟡 **[visual] Summary icon badges rendered as raw emoji instead of NBIconBadge/SF Symbols**
