  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Prices/PricesView.swift:125,146,167` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/prices/PricesScreen.kt:197-205,229-236,261`
  - iOS uses NBIconBadge with SF Symbols: sparkles (size 36, success tint) in the savings header, star.fill in the best-store row, and lightbulb.fill in the tip row. Android renders literal emoji Text ('✨', '★', '💡') — the sparkles/star are placed inside hand-rolled bordered boxes (acceptable shape) but the glyphs are platform emoji that won't visually match the monochrome tinted SF Symbol look, and the tip lightbulb is a bare emoji with no badge. NBIconBadge exists on Android (NBComponents.kt:379) and takes an ImageVector.
  - **Fix:** Replace the emoji Text with NBIconBadge(icon = Icons.Filled.AutoAwesome/Star/Lightbulb, tint = palette.success/warning, size = 36/default) to match iOS tinting and weight. At minimum use vector icons (Material AutoAwesome, Star, Lightbulb) rather than emoji glyphs.
- 🟡 **[behavior] Failure toast not shown on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Prices/PricesView.swift:451-454` → Android: `absent`
  - On a failed compare, iOS both sets errorMessage (rendered as NBErrorCard) AND fires toast.error(prices.compareFailed, description: error). Android only surfaces the inline NBErrorCard via UiState.Error; there is no toast/snackbar with the prices.compareFailed message, so the dedicated failure copy ('Porównanie nieudane'/'Compare failed') is never shown.
  - **Fix:** Emit a toast/snackbar event from the ViewModel on the catch branch (a one-shot SharedFlow consumed by the screen / global ToastCenter equivalent) using locale.t("prices.compareFailed") as title and the exception message as description, in addition to the inline error card.
- ⚪ **[visual] Empty-notice icon dropped on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Prices/PricesView.swift:434-441` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/prices/PricesScreen.kt:125-131,170-176`
  - iOS emptyNotice uses NBEmptyState(systemImage: "doc.text.magnifyingglass", title: prices.emptyTitle, subtitle: message). Android calls NBEmptyState(title=..., subtitle=...) with no icon (NBEmptyState's icon param defaults to null) and additionally wraps it in an extra NBCard, which iOS does not do — iOS shows the empty state directly.
  - **Fix:** Pass icon = Icons.Outlined.ManageSearch (or equivalent magnifier-on-doc vector) to both NBEmptyState calls, and drop the wrapping NBCard so the empty state renders inline like iOS.
- ⚪ **[visual] Expand-all control lacks uppercase, letter tracking and chevron icon**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Prices/PricesView.swift:216-227` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/prices/PricesScreen.kt:143-151`
  - iOS renders the bulk expand/collapse toggle as a chevron icon (chevron.down or chevron.up.chevron.down) plus AppFont.mono(11) text with .tracking(1) and .textCase(.uppercase). The L10n strings ('Rozwiń wszystkie'/'Expand all') are mixed-case, so iOS uppercases them at render. Android renders plain SolvioFonts.mono(11) text with no letterSpacing, no uppercase transform, and no chevron icon — so it appears as mixed-case 'Expand all' with no leading glyph.
  - **Fix:** Add the chevron icon (▼ / change-glyph) before the text, apply letterSpacing ~1.sp, and uppercase the label (text.uppercase()) to match the iOS mono/tracked/uppercase treatment.
- ⚪ **[behavior] savingsPercent truncated (toInt) instead of rounded**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Prices/PricesView.swift:292-294` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/prices/PricesScreen.kt:299-301`
  - iOS formats the percent with String(format: "(%.0f%%)", pct) which rounds to nearest integer (e.g. 12.6 -> 13%). Android uses '(${pct.toInt()}%)' which truncates toward zero (12.6 -> 12%). For the same backend value the two platforms can display different percentages.
  - **Fix:** Use kotlin.math.roundToInt(): Text("(${pct.roundToInt()}%)") to match iOS rounding.
- ⚪ **[behavior] All haptic feedback missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Prices/PricesView.swift:97-98,207,267,375,71-74,46` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS fires distinct haptics throughout: medium impact on the Compare/Recompare CTA, selection ticks on card-expand, expand-all bulk toggle, and the inner view-all-prices toggle, light impact + success on pull-to-refresh, and light impact on error-card retry. Android performs none of these — taps are silent.
  - **Fix:** Add HapticFeedback (LocalHapticFeedback / view.performHapticFeedback) on: the NBPrimaryButton onClick, the card clickable, the expand-all clickable, the view-all-prices clickable, the error retry, and the pull-to-refresh, mapping to the iOS impact/selection/success semantics.
- ⚪ **[bug] Inner view-all-prices toggle is inside the card's clickable area**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Prices/PricesView.swift:391-393` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/prices/PricesScreen.kt:350-364`
  - iOS explicitly swallows the inner price-list toggle tap (.onTapGesture { /* swallow */ }) so toggling the price list does not also collapse/expand the parent card. On Android the inner Row has its own .clickable(onClick = onTogglePrices), but it sits inside the Column that also has .clickable { onToggleCard() }. In Compose the inner clickable consumes the event so the card toggle should not also fire — this likely works, but it is fragile and depends on event consumption order; worth verifying that tapping 'view all prices' does not flip the card.
  - **Fix:** Verify (and if needed isolate) the inner toggle so it never propagates to the card toggle — e.g. keep the inner .clickable on a tightly-scoped Row and confirm the outer card toggle does not also fire on the same tap.
- ⚪ **[visual] Hero savings amount can overflow (no scale-to-fit / single-line cap)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Prices/PricesView.swift:127-131` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/prices/PricesScreen.kt:207-210`
  - iOS renders the total-savings hero with .minimumScaleFactor(0.6) and .lineLimit(1) so large amounts shrink to fit one line. Android renders Fmt.amount(...) in SolvioFonts.hero with no maxLines/autoSize, so a long amount (large value + multi-char currency) can wrap to two lines or get clipped, breaking the summary layout.
  - **Fix:** Apply maxLines = 1 plus autosize (e.g. BasicText with TextAutoSize, or a downscaling approach) to the hero amount Text to mirror iOS minimumScaleFactor(0.6)/lineLimit(1).
- ⚪ **[visual] Best-store badge size differs from iOS**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Prices/PricesView.swift:146` → Android: `android/app/src/main/java/com/programo/solvio/features/prices/PricesScreen.kt:229-236`
  - iOS uses NBIconBadge(systemImage: "star.fill", tint: success) with the default badge size (the NBIconBadge default), whereas Android hardcodes a 28.dp box for the star. iOS NBIconBadge default size differs from 28 (the sparkles badge is explicitly 36; the star uses the default), so the two star badges may not match in size.
  - **Fix:** Use NBIconBadge with its default size for the best-store star instead of a hardcoded 28.dp box, so it matches iOS's default badge dimensions and styling.

## DesignSystem — parity ~82% (14 findings)

_The core token layer (Spacing, Radius, Shadow, Border, and all three palettes Light/Dark/Evening) is a near-perfect 1:1 port — every Light and Dark hex value computed from the iOS asset catalog matches the Android Palette exactly, and the Evening palette matches the iOS ThemePalette.evening RGB values. The neobrutalism atoms (NBCard, NBPrimaryButton, NBSecondaryButton, NBDestructiveButton, NBSegmented, NBStatTile, NBEmptyState, NBErrorCard, NBLoadingCard, NBProgressCard, skeletons, NBTextField, NBTag, NBDivider, NBEyebrow, NBIconBadge, NBSectionHeader, NBScreenHeader, NBRow) are all present and visually close. However, several whole iOS atoms have no Android equivalent: VendorLogo (brand favicon badge, used in 3 screens), CurrencyConverterCard, VirtualReceiptTile (component form), the nbGlassCard glass variant, and a real CategoryIcon.render() that can draw emoji-or-symbol. The single largest behavioral gap is haptics: iOS calls Haptics.* 267 times app-wide and Android has zero haptic feedback. Typography also diverges because Android maps Inter→system SansSerif (Roboto) while iOS maps Inter→SF Pro, so weights/metrics will not be pixel-identical. Plus a handful of smaller visual misses (missing sparkles icon in NBProgressCard, NBSegmented inner-corner radius/clip ordering, NBTextField losing keyboard-type-driven autocaps/autocorrect behavior, button corner-radius press artifact)._

- 🟠 **[behavior] Haptic feedback completely absent on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/UI/Haptics.swift:17-65 (called 267x app-wide)` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS has a centralized Haptics enum (success/error/warning/impact/selection, gated on Reduce Motion) invoked 267 times across the app — on save, fetch error, FAB tap, segmented/picker change, swipe actions, refresh, destructive confirm. Android has ZERO haptic calls (grep for haptic/vibrate/HapticFeedback returns 0). Every tactile interaction iOS gives the user is silent on Android.
  - **Fix:** Create core/ui/Haptics.kt wrapping android.view.HapticFeedbackConstants / Vibrator (or Compose LocalHapticFeedback / View.performHapticFeedback) with success()/error()/warning()/impact(style)/selection() mirroring the Swift API, gated on Settings.System reduced-motion / accessibility. Wire it into NBPrimaryButton/NBSecondaryButton/NBDestructiveButton onClick, NBSegmented onSelect, and the ToastCenter equivalent so the 267 iOS call sites have a counterpart.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] VendorLogo atom missing on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/UI/VendorLogo.swift:15-245 (used in ReceiptsListView.swift:403, ExpensesListView.swift:694, OkazjeHubView.swift:1066)` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS VendorLogo looks up a vendor name in a curated 100+ retailer map (exactMap + containsMap), fetches the favicon from icons.duckduckgo.com/ip3/<domain>.ico via AsyncImage, renders it in an NBIconBadge-shaped tile, and falls back to a symbol badge on failure. It is used on three list screens. Android has no VendorLogo, no DuckDuckGo favicon fetch, and no vendor->domain map (grep duckduckgo/VendorLogo/favicon = 0 hits), so receipt/expense/deal rows will show only generic icons.
  - **Fix:** Add core/ui/VendorLogo.kt: a composable that resolves vendor->domain via a Kotlin map identical to VendorLogo.exactMap + containsMap, loads https://icons.duckduckgo.com/ip3/<domain>.ico with Coil AsyncImage (padding size*0.16, muted bg, hairline border, Radius.sm), and falls back to NBIconBadge(icon, size) on empty/error. Wire it into the Android Receipts/Expenses/Deals row composables.
- 🟠 **[missing-feature] CategoryIcon.render() (emoji-or-symbol) not ported; NBIconBadge cannot render emoji icons**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/UI/NBComponents.swift:115-140; NBIconBadge:95-111` → Android: `android/.../core/ui/NBComponents.kt:117-122 (CategoryIcon only has isEmoji); NBIconBadge:378-401`
  - iOS CategoryIcon.render(value) inspects the string: if it contains a non-ASCII scalar it renders the emoji as Text at size*0.55, otherwise it renders Image(systemName:) at size*0.52 semibold. iOS NBIconBadge takes a String and delegates to render(), so a category whose icon field is an emoji (legacy web data) shows the emoji. Android CategoryIcon only exposes isEmoji() and NBIconBadge takes an ImageVector — it has no path to render an emoji string at all, and no string->ImageVector resolver. Categories with emoji icons (common in existing data) will not render.
  - **Fix:** Add CategoryIcon.render(value: String, tint, size) composable: if isEmoji(value) render Text(value, fontSize = size*0.55), else map the SF-Symbol-like name to a Material ImageVector (lookup table) and render Icon at size*0.52. Add an NBIconBadge(iconName: String, ...) overload that calls render(), so emoji-valued category icons display like iOS.
- 🟠 **[visual] Typography falls back to different system font family than iOS**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Theme/Theme.swift:158-201; FontLoader.swift` → Android: `android/.../core/theme/Typography.kt:17-18`
  - iOS AppFont uses Inter (body) + JetBrains Mono (eyebrows/amounts), registered from a bundled Fonts/ dir, falling back to SF Pro if absent. Android hardcodes InterFamily = FontFamily.SansSerif and MonoFamily = FontFamily.Monospace (Roboto / system mono). There is no res/font directory (ls returns NO res/font dir). So even ignoring the missing TTFs, Android renders Roboto where iOS renders SF Pro/Inter — different glyph metrics, weights (Black/SemiBold), and the mono eyebrow/amount look. Not pixel-identical anywhere text appears.
  - **Fix:** Bundle Inter (Regular/Medium/SemiBold/Bold/Black) and JetBrainsMono (Regular/Bold) ttf into res/font and set InterFamily/MonoFamily to FontFamily(Font(R.font.inter_*, weight=...)) etc. This is the same fix iOS needs (drop the ttf into Fonts/), but Android additionally must change the FontFamily declarations away from SansSerif/Monospace.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] CurrencyConverterCard atom missing on Android**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/UI/NBComponents.swift:618-710` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS ships a reusable CurrencyConverterCard (amount in PLN/EUR/USD, source badge, refresh button with rotation animation, FXRates.shared NBP rates, as-of footer). Android has no CurrencyConverterCard and no FXRates equivalent (grep CurrencyConverter/FXRates = 0). Any iOS screen embedding this glanceable tile has no Android counterpart.
  - **Fix:** Port FXRates (NBP rate fetch + cache + convert) and a CurrencyConverterCard composable mirroring the layout: NBSectionHeader + refresh icon (rotate while fetching), per-target rows with code + optional source badge + Fmt.amount value + hairline separators + mono footer, wrapped in nbCard(Radius.md, Shadow.sm).
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] nbGlassCard (glassmorphism variant) not ported**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Theme/Theme.swift:238-247` → Android: `absent`
  - iOS exposes nbGlassCard(): .ultraThinMaterial background + softened 2px border (foreground@15%) + blurred low-opacity shadow (foreground@8%, radius 12, y 4) for floating panels/sheets/stats overlays. Android has no glass variant (grep nbGlass/ultraThin/GlassCard = 0). Any iOS surface using nbGlassCard will look like a plain nbCard or be missing on Android.
  - **Fix:** Add Modifier.nbGlassCard(palette, radius) using a translucent blurred background (Compose RenderEffect.createBlurEffect / a frosted scrim), 2dp border at foreground 15%, and a soft drop shadow (foreground 8%). Apply wherever the iOS port uses nbGlassCard.
- 🟡 **[visual] NBProgressCard missing the sparkles icon next to the stage label**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/UI/NBComponents.swift:310-321` → Android: `android/.../core/ui/NBComponents.kt:745-750`
  - iOS NBProgressCard renders the current stage as HStack { Image(systemName: "sparkles") (muted) + Text(stage) } with a fade transition on stage change. Android renders only the Text(stage) with no leading sparkles icon and no transition/animation on stage change.
  - **Fix:** In NBProgressCard, wrap the stage text in a Row with a leading Icon(Icons.Filled.AutoAwesome /* sparkles */, tint = mutedForeground, size 14-16dp) and animate the stage swap (Crossfade or AnimatedContent) to match the iOS .opacity transition.
- 🟡 **[visual] NBSegmented inner-corner radius and clip/border ordering differ from iOS**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/UI/NBComponents.swift:440-471` → Android: `android/.../core/ui/NBComponents.kt:466-504`
  - iOS NBSegmented: each option uses Radius.sm (8) for its active fill, and the outer container is .padding(4) then .background(muted) then .overlay(stroke, RoundedRectangle Radius.md=10, widthThin) then .clipShape(Radius.md). The selected pill background is clipped to Radius.sm inside a Radius.md container. Android clips the OUTER Row to Radius.md BEFORE applying background+border, and each option clips to Radius.sm — visually close, but the order (clip outer before border in Android vs border-as-overlay-then-clip in iOS) plus Android applying clip to the outer row can crop the 1px border differently, and the outer border in Android draws on the clipped shape so the hairline can be eaten. Minor but visible on the segmented control edges.
  - **Fix:** Match iOS ordering: background(muted) + border(widthThin, Radius.md) applied to the padded row, then the active pill uses clip(RoundedCornerShape(Radius.sm)) over foreground — i.e. ensure the outer hairline isn't clipped away. Verify the active pill radius is Radius.sm (it is) and the container radius is Radius.md (it is); reorder so border is not consumed by the outer clip.
- 🟡 **[behavior] NBTextField drops keyboard-type-driven autocapitalization & autocorrect behavior**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/UI/NBComponents.swift:473-500` → Android: `android/.../core/ui/NBComponents.kt:429-463`
  - iOS NBTextField exposes autocapitalization (default .sentences) and disables autocorrection when keyboardType == .emailAddress. Android NBTextField only passes keyboardType into KeyboardOptions; it never sets capitalization or autoCorrect, so email fields keep autocorrect/autocaps on (likely mangling typed emails) and there is no per-field capitalization control.
  - **Fix:** In Android NBTextField KeyboardOptions, set autoCorrect = keyboardType != KeyboardType.Email and capitalization = if (keyboardType == KeyboardType.Email) KeyboardCapitalization.None else KeyboardCapitalization.Sentences; optionally add an autocapitalization parameter mirroring the iOS signature.
- ⚪ **[visual] Button corner-radius mismatch produces shadow peeking on press (nbShadowOnly fixed radius vs button radius)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Theme/Theme.swift:261-318 (button shadow follows the Radius.md shape)` → Android: `android/.../core/ui/NBComponents.kt:194-210, 302-319`
  - Android buttons call nbShadowOnly(radius = Radius.md) and the content clip is also Radius.md, which matches. However nbShadowOnly is applied BEFORE the .offset(pressOffset) on the SAME node chain, while the content clip/background come AFTER the offset — so on press the content shifts +2dp/+2dp but the drawn shadow rect (drawBehind on the pre-offset modifier scope) does not move identically, and because the shadow draw uses the node size before offset it can momentarily reveal a sliver. iOS resolves this cleanly because .nbShadow is applied after the offset on the label. Minor.
  - **Fix:** Order the modifiers so the shadow (drawBehind) is applied on the same post-offset geometry as the fill, or compute shadow offset relative to the pressed state (already does shadowOffset->0 on press) and apply .offset before .nbShadowOnly so both move together; verify no shadow sliver on press.
- ⚪ **[behavior] NbSpring / NbGentle defined but never used; iOS nbSpring spring params not faithfully mapped**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Theme/Theme.swift:252-257 (used 10x app-wide)` → Android: `android/.../core/theme/Theme.kt:128-135 (0 usages)`
  - iOS Animation.nbSpring (response 0.35, damping 0.85) / nbGentle (response 0.5, damping 0.9) are used 10 times. Android defines NbSpring/NbGentle but maps response to Spring.StiffnessMedium/Low (a coarse approximation, not the real response value) and grep shows zero call sites use them — Android animations are ad hoc instead of the shared spring. Visual timing of shared transitions will differ.
  - **Fix:** Map iOS response->stiffness precisely (stiffness = (2*pi/response)^2 ≈ 322 for 0.35, ≈ 158 for 0.5) keeping dampingRatio 0.85/0.9, then actually use NbSpring/NbGentle in the ported screens wherever iOS uses .nbSpring/.nbGentle.
- ⚪ **[visual] NBProgressBar is Android-only; iOS lacks the shared atom (inconsistent source of truth)**
  - iOS: `absent in NBComponents.swift (goals/budget screens inline their own bar, e.g. GoalsListView.swift, DashboardView.swift)` → Android: `android/.../core/ui/NBComponents.kt:783-805`
  - Android added a shared NBProgressBar(progress, over) atom (6dp, Radius 4, foreground/destructive fill). iOS has no such shared component — each iOS screen draws its own progress bar inline (NBProgressCard has one; Goals/Dashboard draw bespoke bars). Risk: the Android shared bar's height/radius/colors may not match every bespoke iOS bar. Confirm 6dp height + 4 radius + muted track + foreground fill matches each iOS callsite; the over=destructive flag must match iOS over-budget styling.
  - **Fix:** Cross-check the Android NBProgressBar dimensions against each iOS inline bar (Goals, Dashboard budget, NBProgressCard). If iOS over-budget bars use destructive only above 100%, ensure Android callers pass over correctly; otherwise this atom is fine to keep.
- ⚪ **[visual] NBTag uses single muted background default vs iOS muted (verify uppercase + tracking parity)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/UI/NBComponents.swift:51-71` → Android: `android/.../core/ui/NBComponents.kt:73-97`
  - iOS NBTag applies .textCase(.uppercase) on the rendered Text AND mono(10) tracking(1) with 8h/4v padding, Radius.sm, hairline border. Android NBTag does NOT uppercase the text (iOS forces uppercase via textCase) — it passes the raw text into Text with mono(10) letterSpacing 1. If callers pass non-uppercase strings, Android tags will render mixed-case where iOS renders uppercase.
  - **Fix:** In Android NBTag, render text.uppercase() to match iOS .textCase(.uppercase) (matching what NBStatTile/NBSegmented already do).
- ⚪ **[bug] darkColorScheme onError mapping differs between dark and light (onError=background vs onSurface)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Theme/Theme.swift:46 (destructive token only; no Material color scheme concept)` → Android: `android/.../core/theme/Theme.kt:99, 110`
  - In SolvioComposeTheme the dark bucket sets onError = palette.background while the light bucket sets onError = palette.surface. iOS has no Material color-scheme equivalent (it uses Theme tokens directly), and destructive buttons hardcode foregroundColor(.white). Any Material3 component that reads onError will get background in dark but surface in light — inconsistent, and neither matches the iOS convention of white text on destructive. Low impact since custom atoms hardcode Color.White, but stray Material3 components (Snackbar errors, etc.) could show wrong contrast.
  - **Fix:** Standardize onError to a high-contrast value (e.g. Color.White, matching NBDestructiveButton) in both branches, or ensure no Material3 error surfaces are used; align with iOS destructive=white text.

## Reports — parity ~62% (10 findings)

_The Android Reports screen is a faithful structural port of iOS ReportsView: same year-block model, expandable year cards with a generate icon button, month rows, per-format download links (PDF/CSV/DOCX) plus mini-download letters, the "generating" banner, the same NB atoms (NBScreenHeader, NBTag, NBCard), and identical L10n keys (all present in both PL/EN). However it has one CRITICAL functional bug: report generation will always fail. The web backend /api/reports/generate reads parameters strictly via request.formData() with fields type + year/ym, and iOS posts them as multipart formData (ApiClient.postForm). Android instead posts a JSON body via api.post with wrong field names (year as Int, yearMonth instead of ym, and no type field), so request.formData() fails and the endpoint returns HTTP 400 — every Generate tap errors. On top of that there are several parity gaps: no pull-to-refresh, no haptics anywhere, the first/most-recent year is not auto-expanded on load, the loading state uses a single spinner card instead of the iOS 4-row skeleton, the empty state is missing its icon and is double-wrapped in an extra card, error UI replaces loaded data on refresh failure, the iOS inline nav-bar title is absent, and the SF Symbol icons for downloads and month status are substituted with plain text glyphs. Visual tokens/spacing/colors otherwise match well._

- 🔴 **[bug] Report generation request format is wrong — endpoint expects formData, Android sends JSON with wrong field names (generation always fails)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:391-403 (postForm fields type/year/ym); ReportsView.swift:381,394` → Android: `android/.../core/network/Repositories.kt:229-238; core/network/ApiClient.kt:60-64,142`
  - Backend app/api/reports/generate/route.ts reads params exclusively via request.formData() and validates fields type ('yearly'|'monthly') + year (4-digit) or ym (YYYY-MM); if formData() fails it returns 400 'Invalid form data'. iOS posts these as multipart formData via ApiClient.postForm. Android ReportsRepo.runYearly/runMonthly call api.post(...) which serializes a JSON body (application/json) with ReportYearlyBody(year: Int) / ReportMonthlyBody(yearMonth: String). The JSON body has no 'type' field, sends 'year' as a number not a 4-digit string, and uses 'yearMonth' instead of 'ym'. request.formData() on a JSON body fails -> 400. Result: every yearly/monthly Generate tap on Android errors with toast 'Generate failed'. Report generation is completely non-functional.
  - **Fix:** Add a form-post path to ApiClient (e.g. postForm(path, fields: Map<String,String>) building application/x-www-form-urlencoded via FormBody.Builder, or MultipartBody.FORM with addFormDataPart). Change ReportsRepo.runYearly to send fields mapOf("type" to "yearly", "year" to year.toString()) and runMonthly to mapOf("type" to "monthly", "ym" to yearMonth). Drop the JSON ReportYearlyBody/ReportMonthlyBody bodies (or keep only for typing the form map). Ensure year is a 4-digit string and the monthly key is 'ym' not 'yearMonth'.
- 🟠 **[behavior] Most-recent year not auto-expanded on load**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Reports/ReportsView.swift:370 (if let first = years.first { expandedYears = [first] })` → Android: `android/.../features/reports/ReportsScreen.kt:141 (var expandedYears by remember { mutableStateOf(setOf<Int>()) })`
  - iOS auto-expands the first (most recent) year's month list after loadData so the user immediately sees months. Android initializes expandedYears to an empty set and never seeds it from the loaded data, so every year card opens fully collapsed and the user must tap 'Show months' first.
  - **Fix:** After load completes, seed expandedYears with the first/most-recent year. E.g. collect the loaded years in a LaunchedEffect keyed on the Loaded state and set expandedYears = setOf(years.first().year) when it is still empty, or move expandedYears into the ViewModel and set it in load().
- 🟡 **[visual] Loading state uses single spinner card instead of 4-row skeleton**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Reports/ReportsView.swift:34-38 (NBSkeletonList(rows: 4) when isLoading && yearBlocks.isEmpty)` → Android: `android/.../features/reports/ReportsScreen.kt:159 (NBLoadingCard())`
  - iOS shows NBSkeletonList(rows: 4) — a list of 4 skeleton placeholder rows that previews the upcoming year/month rows. Android renders NBLoadingCard() (a single centered spinner card). NBSkeletonList exists on Android (NBComponents.kt:673) but is not used here, so the loading visual diverges.
  - **Fix:** Replace NBLoadingCard() in the Loading branch with NBSkeletonList(rows = 4) to match iOS.
- 🟡 **[visual] Empty state missing icon and double-wrapped in an extra card**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Reports/ReportsView.swift:84-91 (NBEmptyState systemImage: "chart.bar.doc.horizontal"); NBComponents.swift:142-178 (NBEmptyState always shows icon + self-wraps in nbCard)` → Android: `android/.../features/reports/ReportsScreen.kt:164-172 (NBCard { NBEmptyState(title, subtitle) } — no icon arg); NBComponents.kt:561-600`
  - iOS passes a systemImage (chart.bar.doc.horizontal) so the empty state renders its 72x72 icon badge, and NBEmptyState already wraps itself in an nbCard. Android omits the icon param (so no icon is drawn) AND wraps NBEmptyState inside an additional NBCard, producing a double border + double hard-shadow box not present on iOS.
  - **Fix:** Pass an icon to NBEmptyState (a chart/bar-document equivalent ImageVector, e.g. Icons.Outlined.Assessment/BarChart) and remove the outer NBCard wrapper since NBEmptyState already provides its own nbCard.
- 🟡 **[behavior] Error UI replaces loaded data on refresh failure**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Reports/ReportsView.swift:39-47 (error card only when yearBlocks.isEmpty)` → Android: `android/.../features/reports/ReportsScreen.kt:158-162 (UiState.Error always renders NBErrorCard and discards prior Loaded data)`
  - iOS only shows the error card when there is nothing already on screen (yearBlocks empty); a transient refresh failure does not blow away already-loaded year cards. Android's load() unconditionally sets _state to UiState.Error on any failure, replacing any previously Loaded list with a full-screen error, so a flaky refresh wipes the visible reports.
  - **Fix:** Track error separately from the loaded list (e.g. keep last Loaded data and a nullable error message), and only render NBErrorCard when there are no loaded years; otherwise surface refresh failures via a toast/banner while keeping the existing cards.
- 🟡 **[missing-feature] Pull-to-refresh missing**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Reports/ReportsView.swift:65-71 (.refreshable { Haptics.impact(.light); await loadData(); if errorMessage==nil { Haptics.success() } })` → Android: `absent (ReportsScreen.kt:145 LazyColumn has no PullToRefresh)`
  - iOS supports swipe-down to refresh the report list (with light impact on start and success haptic on completion). The Android LazyColumn has no pull-to-refresh; the only way to reload is leaving and re-entering the screen.
  - **Fix:** Wrap the LazyColumn in a PullToRefreshBox (material3 pulltorefresh) that calls vm.load(), matching the pattern used on other refresh-capable screens.
- ⚪ **[behavior] No haptic feedback on any action**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Reports/ReportsView.swift:235 (Haptics.impact(.medium) on generate), :154 (Haptics.selection() on expand), :291,322 (Haptics.impact(.medium) on download), :68-70 (refresh haptics)` → Android: `absent (ReportsScreen.kt GenerateIconButton:374 clickable; YearCard toggle:289 clickable; DownloadRow:419 / MiniDownloadLink:447 clickable — no haptics)`
  - iOS fires medium impact on Generate, medium impact on download/mini-download taps, selection tick on expand/collapse, and light+success on pull-to-refresh. Android performs all these actions with no haptic feedback.
  - **Fix:** Add HapticFeedback (LocalHapticFeedback / view.performHapticFeedback) on generate (LongPress/medium-equivalent), expand toggle (selection), download taps, and refresh start/success to mirror iOS Haptics calls.
- ⚪ **[copy-l10n] Inline navigation-bar title (reports.navTitle) not shown**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Reports/ReportsView.swift:62-63 (.navigationTitle(locale.t("reports.navTitle")) .inline)` → Android: `absent (MainTabScreen.kt:133 composable(Reports.route){ ReportsScreen() } — no TopAppBar/title)`
  - iOS shows an inline nav-bar title 'Raporty'/'Reports' (reports.navTitle). Android does not render any top app bar / title for the Reports destination; the reports.navTitle key exists in L10n.kt (line 465/EN) but is unused. (This is consistent with other Android tab screens, so likely a global host decision rather than Reports-specific.)
  - **Fix:** If matching iOS, render a TopAppBar (or equivalent inline title) using locale.t("reports.navTitle") for the Reports destination in MainTabScreen, or document the deliberate omission.
- ⚪ **[visual] SF Symbol icons substituted with plain-text glyphs (downloads, month status, generate, chevron)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Features/Reports/ReportsView.swift:204 (checkmark.circle.fill), :242 (arrow.clockwise), :164 (chevron.up/down), :263-265 NBIconBadge(doc.richtext/tablecells/doc.text)` → Android: `android/.../features/reports/ReportsScreen.kt:354 ("✓"), :380 ("⟳"), :298 ("▲"/"▼"), :413 (label.first() letter), :449 (P/C/D letters)`
  - iOS uses SF Symbols: a filled checkmark.circle for generated months, arrow.clockwise for the generate button, chevron.up/down for expand, and NBIconBadge with doc.richtext/tablecells/doc.text for the PDF/CSV/DOCX download rows. Android substitutes Unicode/text glyphs: '✓', '⟳', '▲/▼', a single-letter badge (P/D etc.) and mini buttons labelled P/C/D. The download rows in particular show a letter where iOS shows a document icon, a noticeable visual divergence.
  - **Fix:** Use vector icons to match iOS: a check-circle for generated months, a refresh icon for GenerateIconButton, KeyboardArrowUp/Down for the expand chevron, and document/table/file icons inside NBIconBadge for PDF/CSV/DOCX rows (the mini P/C/D row may stay as letters if desired, but the full DownloadRow badge should be an icon).
- ⚪ **[bug] Unused 'format' field on report request bodies (dead/incorrect param)**
  - iOS: `native-ios/Solvio/Core/Network/Repositories.swift:391-403 (only type + year/ym sent)` → Android: `android/.../core/network/Repositories.kt:229,232 (ReportYearlyBody(val year, val format: String?=null), ReportMonthlyBody(val yearMonth, val format: String?=null))`
  - Android's request bodies declare an extra 'format' field that iOS never sends and the backend schema does not accept (it expects type/year/ym only). Combined with encodeDefaults=true on the Json instance it would serialize format:null. This is dead/incorrect surface that should be removed when fixing the form-post issue.
  - **Fix:** Remove the unused 'format' field from ReportYearlyBody/ReportMonthlyBody (or remove these data classes entirely once switching to form-encoded fields per the critical finding).
